# Plan of attack

The order of work: what is open and planned (§4), its sequencing (§5), the decisions only the owner can make (§6) and the first-release checklist (§7). [VISION.md](VISION.md) sets the priorities; this doc turns them into tasks with acceptance criteria.

**History:** the finished work moved to [history/plan-history.md](history/plan-history.md) on 2026-11-01: the architecture review's triage (its §1–§3, so this doc starts at §4), the records of Phases 1–5 (tasks 1.1–5.6), the driver upgrade, versioning and decision 45, and every external review and fix verification through R50. A review's record stays here until its work is finished, then moves there.

## 4. Plan of attack

Sizes: **S** is up to half a day, **M** is 1–2 days, **L** is longer. Each phase ends green: fmt, clippy, unit tests, and the new CLI tests.

### Carried over from finished work

What the finished records left open and is still open (checked 2026-11-01 against the later records, AGENTS.md's Status and the code):
- **The timeout trial:** agent trial 3 met no timeout (the profile's 60 s), so how a model reads a `-t` stop and recovers is untried; Phase 7 counts it (plan-history.md, Phase 5: connection reuse).
- **Diagnosed and fixed, the flake `the_read_uncommitted_note_counts_against_the_output_cap` (2026-11-07):** it failed now and then under a full parallel live run (plan-history.md, R45 fix verification), and failed on SQL Server 2016 in the first full run after the owner rebooted both servers' host, with its output kept: `-- 59 of ≥300 rows shown (--max-output; counting stopped)`. It read 1,000 rows and asserted that the 250 ms count of the 944 hidden ones finished (`rows shown (--max-output)`), which the budget never promised, and a stopped count has a line more, so fewer rows fit and its comparison of the two runs was off too. The same run failed `a_note_about_the_sql_stays_within_the_output_cap` on 2025 the same way (`56 of ≥600`), and `a_stalled_reader_cannot_hang_the_run` with exit 4 for 7: a cross join of `sys.all_objects` on a server just started didn't fill the queue and the pipe within `-t 2`, so the query's own deadline came first. The note tests now count with `--exact-count`, whose closing lines don't depend on a clock, the note's cost in rows staying held against the fake server; the stalled reader's rows are made in memory. All three passed on both servers after. Not loosened: no bound changed. `a_fast_narrow_result_is_bounded_by_t_in_every_mode` (below) may be the same class; its output wasn't kept.
- **The flake `engine::tests::a_cancel_that_does_not_settle_discards_the_connection` (2026-11-02):** failed once in 53 `cargo test --lib` runs, during a mutation run alongside a release build and a server sweep; its bound is on the wall clock (`SETTLE_GRACE` + 2 s). Its output wasn't kept and it wasn't reproduced, so the cause is unverified; when it fails, record its figures.
- **The flake `a_fast_narrow_result_is_bounded_by_t_in_every_mode` (2026-11-07):** failed once in the release live run on SQL Server 2016, in the full parallel suite (`scripts/check.ps1 -Live`, the uncommitted decision 56 change, which its SQL doesn't touch): its last case, the default bounded mode, ended with a code other than 0 (`assert_eq!(r.code, 0)`). Its output wasn't kept, so the code and the run's time are unknown; run alone afterwards, once, for its figures, it passed in 6.8 s. The debug live run before it passed, and so did both on 2025. When it fails, keep its output: whether a busy server held the 250 ms count past `-t 1` is the first thing to look at. **Failed again 2026-11-13, output kept, one cause fixed (test only):** in the full live run against the Azure VM (§7, real network), its `--max-rows 0` case (default 1 MB output budget, `-t 1`) ended exit 4: 78,034 rows (624 KB) arrived within the second, short of the budget, so the deadline came while rows were still being shown, which is exit 4 by the rule (DESIGN.md §4.4). sqlh was right; the case assumed about 125,000 rows a second, a throughput a live clock can't promise. It now sets `--max-output 4096`, the smallest budget, about 500 rows, so the budget and then the count end it at any throughput, and the case still checks that rule. Passed alone on the VM, `test` and `test2016`, then in the full live run on the VM. Whether the 2016 failure was this case (it has the same `assert_eq!(r.code, 0)`) or the default mode's, as recorded, is unknown; if the default mode's fails, keep its output.
- **A live test's deadlock with its neighbours (2026-11-01):** `schema_commands_describe_what_the_catalog_holds` failed once in three debug runs on SQL Server 2016, 60 of 61 passing: its `find @count --text` read the catalog of `sqlh_test` for 5,372 ms and was the deadlock victim (error 1205, severity 13, state 56, exit 1), reported correctly. The other live tests make and drop objects in that database at the same time under the shared scratch lock, and sqlh's session setup runs at low deadlock priority (decision 7), so the likely cause is the test's sharing, not sqlh; that is an inference, since the deadlock graph wasn't captured. Not seen on 2025, nor in three release runs on 2016. The test now takes `target_alone()` (owner, 2026-11-01); if it still fails, the sharing wasn't the cause, so capture the deadlock graph. The first full run after that failed differently, on 2025 in release: `Probe::on` named tables by process and clock, and two tests read the same tick (`probe_<pid>_<nanos>`), giving errors 2714 and 208. Tests waiting for the scratch lock start together when it is released, which may have made it likelier (not measured). The name now carries a counter too.
- **A connect refused by this computer's own sockets (2026-11-23, output kept; decided 2026-11-26, decision 95, built in §4's Phase 6 slice 2 record):** in `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` on `test` with decision 92's change, the release live tests' `the_servers_read_ai_generate_embeddings_as_the_guard_does` failed at a `describe` of its own table: exit 2 in 4 ms, `cannot connect to <server> (...): IO error: Only one usage of each socket address (protocol/network address/port) is normally permitted. (os error 10048); check the server name; ...`. 10048 is WSAEADDRINUSE, from this computer's TCP stack, not the server: each sqlh run is a new connection, the release live tests start right after the debug ones against the same server, and Windows keeps a closed connection's local port in TIME_WAIT for 120 s by default (1,411 were in TIME_WAIT after the run; the dynamic range is 16,384 ports), so a new connect's local port can collide with one still waiting to that server. The same test passed in that run's debug tests, and every other test passed in both; `test2016` passed whole. Not rerun to make it pass. First seen; no earlier record of 10048. Two things for the owner: whether a connect that fails with 10048 is tried once more (the OS picks another port; DESIGN.md §4.2's `-l` says "nothing is retried", so it is a decision), and the hint, which sends the reader to the server name for an error on this computer.
- **The one-off live failure at `9b5318d`:** one debug live test failed once, its name not kept; the possible cause found in 5.4 (one `Reader` login name per test process, fixed in `fbbe1a8`) is not confirmed; if it recurs, keep the test's name and output (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes).
- **An IPv6 address with an instance name** (`addr\INST`): no TCP-only spelling, since a port replaces the instance, so a pipe open can follow a failed TCP connect (`db::data_source`, connect-failures.md); `addr,port` avoids it (plan-history.md, Phase 2 follow-up). **Closed on the driver branch (`15a0e87`, 2026-11-08):** mssql-tds 0.4.0 splits `tcp:` off an address, so `data_source` sends `tcp:addr\INST`: SQL Browser, then TCP only (driver-0.4.0.md §1.2).
- **`text`, `ntext` and `image` are read whole:** the driver has no streaming path for them (upstream-issues.md U3) and refuses a value past 100 MB (exit 10; DESIGN.md §11) (plan-history.md, Phase 2 follow-up).
- **Windows authentication's connect time, measured 2026-11-01, and its stall, fixed:** without a name lookup a Windows-auth connect takes 31–94 ms, against about 250 ms for a SQL login. By address with no PTR record, the driver's blocking reverse lookup added about 5 s and stalled the runtime: `-l 1` and `-l 3` failed a healthy server (exit 4, a message blaming the server), and `sqlh mcp`'s calls waited on each other's lookups. The owner chose a bounded lookup (2026-11-01): sqlh runs it on its own thread for 500 ms, then gives the driver the SPN it would fall back to, so that connect now takes 571–725 ms and those failures are gone (DESIGN.md §4.2, `-E`; connect-latency.md, Windows authentication; upstream-issues.md U9). Replaced by decision 72's per-hop provider (`db::spn`, 2026-11-08): the same bound and cap, inside the login deadline, with R52's exclusions closed. It touches connect and cancellation, so it goes in the next external review (decision 52). Live runs against the test server only, and only by hand; the live tests use a SQL login.
- **A unit test's leak into %TEMP%, fixed 2026-11-01 (test-only; self-verified, for the next brief, decision 52):** `files::tests`' `dir` helper had no cleanup, so `replace_writes_the_whole_file_and_leaves_nothing_else` and `the_profiles_lock_is_held_by_one_edit_at_a_time` left `sqlh-files-replace-<pid>` and `sqlh-files-lock-<pid>` on every `cargo test`, and the other three left theirs when an assertion failed. All five now hold an `OwnScratch` of the file's own, removed on drop. Checked with `scripts/check.ps1` (no `sqlh*` entry in %TEMP% added or changed, by name and timestamp, since a reused PID rewrites an old directory under the same name) and with a panic injected into all five while their files were open (all five directories gone). Still open: `config.rs`'s `migration` tests remove `sqlh-migration-<test>-<pid>` on their last line, so a failed assertion leaves it.
- **MCP text that still names CLI flags:** a `GO N` past 1000 labeled `-Q`; a profile's resolution errors at launch (`connect over TCP: -S host`, `drop -S/-U, or set SQLCMDPASSWORD`); the unknown-flag refusal's `(sqlh mcp takes its connections from its profiles)` for flags such as `--format`; the 18456 hint naming the saved password when it came from `SQLCMDPASSWORD` (mcp-mode.md §3, §4, Still naming a flag) (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes).
- **MCP cases with no automated test:** the list kept in mcp-mode.md §9, "Not covered by an automated test yet" (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes).
- **MCP's memory with requests held back:** 64 requests or 16 MiB plus one message are bounds, not a measured peak; keep the caps until the combined memory is measured (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3 fix verification).
- **A code comment to correct:** `src/mcp/protocol.rs`'s "a call costs at most three scans" should say the scans come on top of `serde_json`'s parse, bounded but not linear in the message (mcp-mode.md §4, Parser work); left for the next code change there (plan-history.md, Phase 5.4, built 2026-10-18). **Corrected 2026-11-21** with Phase 6 slice 2's `errorlog`, whose `archive` made the scans four: the comment now says the rereading comes on top of `serde_json`'s parse, bounded by the message limit but not linear in it.
- **A stored password that isn't UTF-8** is refused, but its bytes, copied out of Credential Manager, are dropped unwiped with `from_utf8`'s error (`credman.rs`; F14's wiping) (plan-history.md, Follow-ups and Phase 4.1-4.3 initial review). **Fixed 2026-11-08:** the copy is held in a `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` from the start, and `credman::password_from` takes the password out of it without a copy or, refusing it, gives `from_utf8`'s bytes back to it, so they are wiped on drop; the blob in `CredReadW`'s block is zeroed before `CredFree` too, which DESIGN.md §8 had listed as beyond sqlh (the block is the caller's until freed). `a_password_is_taken_from_the_wiping_buffer_or_left_in_it` (refused bytes back in the buffer; an accepted one the same allocation, the buffer left empty) and `credman_refuses_a_password_that_is_not_utf8` (ignored, the real Credential Manager: a raw `p\xffss` blob is refused, `InvalidData`, its binding still read); `imp::write` takes bytes so the test can store one. Credentials only, so it goes in the next brief (decision 52).
- **Credential Manager across concurrent clients (measured 2026-11-08, `scratch/credrace`, not kept in the repo):** a read of a credential right after the same process wrote or deleted it can miss the change while other processes change theirs: with two processes cycling write, read, delete, read on names of their own, 26 of 600 reads after a write missed it and one after a delete still found it; every miss was found again within 50 ms. With three processes, 1,800 cycles, 22 missed, and **one credential stayed unreadable for the whole 2 s watched** after `CredWriteW` succeeded. **What that was is unconfirmed (R60):** the probe took every failed `CredReadW` as missing without keeping `GetLastError`, ignored its deletes' results, and named targets by tag and worker without the process id, so a lost write, slower visibility, another error, or the probe's own pattern can't be told apart. A probe that could would keep unique process and target names and its launch arguments, record the OS error of every failed read, write and delete, tell `ERROR_NOT_FOUND` from the rest, and keep its last observation before cleaning up. Threads within one process (4 × 300 cycles) saw none, and an unchanged credential read 20,000 times during the churn was never missing. For sqlh: `profiles add` and `profiles rm` hold the profiles lock throughout, so two sqlh processes never write at once, and sqlh never reads back its own write in a process; what is left is another program (`cmdkey`, a credential helper) writing at the same moment, which could make `profiles add` report a password saved that a run straight after it doesn't find ("no stored password", rerun `profiles add`). Not fixed, and recorded rather than built for 0.1.0 (the R59-R61 review agreed): a read-back after the write could report a save that isn't visible, but proves no persistence, and a blind retry would hide the distinction; the error-aware probe comes first. The owner's call. The ignored credman tests (`credman.rs`, and `config.rs`'s R20 test) failed about one run in eight in parallel (`credman_round_trip`: `exists` false straight after its write, and the legacy move's own reads; 3 of 40 at `88ac335`, before the fix above); they now hold `credman::serial()`, 0 of 60 after. That orders one test binary's tests only: the debug and release binaries (their targets are fixed names) or another program writing credentials could still fail one; `scripts/check.ps1` doesn't run them.
- **`profiles add`'s status line counts the password prompt (found 2026-11-13, the Sandbox check; open, not a 0.1.0 gate):** its connection test ended `-- ok: 1 batch, 4782 ms; ..., connect 211 ms`, and `info` straight after took 311 ms. `profile::run` passes the process's start (`started`) to `test`, so the run's time includes how long the person took to type the password. Only a person runs `profiles add` (the one command that reads the console), so no agent reads that figure; timing from after the prompt would make it the test's own. A change to a status line's time, so the owner's call. **Fixed 2026-11-14 (decision 77; P3, self-verified under decision 52, for the next brief):** `profile::run` gives the connection test `Instant::now()`, taken after the save, so neither the prompt nor a wait for the profiles lock is counted; `list`, `rm` and `test` keep the process's start, since nothing waits on a person before them. `the_connection_test_after_profiles_add_times_only_itself` holds the profiles lock 3 s in the test process, runs `profiles add` with Windows auth (no prompt) to `127.0.0.1,1`, and requires the run to have waited and its `connection failed, N ms` to be under 3 s; with the old `started` it failed (`3453 ms counts the wait`). DESIGN.md §8's `profiles add` entry says how it is timed. `scripts/check.ps1` passed (128 debug and 122 release process tests).
- **The M0 spike's two unverified items,** Windows auth (SSPI) and Ctrl+C mid-query (m0-driver-spike.md, Still to verify): §7's Windows-auth and cancellation lines (decision 50) (plan-history.md, 2. Docs re-review). Windows auth's success path passed on 2026-11-01 on the test server (Standard Developer), NTLM, with a same-named local user on each side, so no `runas /netonly`: a query, `profiles add` and `test`, schema commands, the guard's refusal and `sqlh mcp` with a pooled connection. A refused login's error was seen on the 2016 instance (error 18452, exit 2, "the server rejected Windows auth for this login; use -U with a SQL login"). Still unchecked: a user without a SQL login, Standard edition, and Kerberos.

### MCP client feedback (October 31, 2026)

The owner ran `sqlh mcp` from Claude Desktop (chat and Cowork) against a real SQL Server 2016 in an availability group; [notes/mcp-client-feedback.md](notes/mcp-client-feedback.md) keeps the findings, without the environment's names. Decision 45 (§6) came from the first item.
1. **Built (2026-10-31), decision 45:**
   - a capped result in a batch with later statements counts for 5 s, so they run;
   - a run that still loses them ends `-- partial:` (exit 0) and names the batches;
   - a single-output batch's note says only that its total is a lower bound.
   Tests:
   - `guard::tests::several_outputs_counts_the_statements_a_stop_could_cancel`;
   - `db::tests::a_batch_with_later_statements_counts_for_longer`;
   - `app::tests::a_run_that_lost_statements_to_a_stopped_count_is_partial`;
   - `output::tests::the_note_says_the_rest_did_not_run_only_where_it_could_show_something`;
   - live: `a_capped_result_lets_the_later_statements_run_or_the_run_is_partial`, run against the test server: 200,000 rows let the later statements run; a three-way cross join ends `partial` after 5 s.
   The live tests that pinned the old note were updated, and their batches made single-statement where they test the 250 ms path.
2. **Built, the MCP schema:**
   - `query` takes `exact_count`;
   - `info` takes no `max_rows` (it took no `max_cell` either until R49 restored it);
   - each limit's description gives its default;
   - `check`'s `sql` says it is compiled, not run;
   - the `query` description and the instructions give the status forms, the escapes, `≥N` and the `GO` advice;
   - the MCP advice names `exact_count = true` in place of the profile's key.
   Test: `mcp::tools::tests::the_schemas_state_their_defaults_and_exact_count_reaches_the_run`, plus refusals in `calls_are_checked_against_the_schema`.
3. **Planned, not started:**
   - (a) paging for `definition` (a line range), since a 63,000-character procedure only reached the model through the client's fallback file;
   - (b) `info`: the name the connection dialed, the availability group role (`sys.dm_hadr_availability_replica_states`, which needs `VIEW SERVER STATE`), and the time zone name where the server has `CURRENT_TIMEZONE()` (2019 and later). Its catalog SQL must still run on 2016, which the client used.
4. **Declined:** a key=value status line (`format: json` is that), cut markers moved to a footer, and the `definition` line count (correct). The reasons are in the note.

### The profiles file under a packaged app (found and built 2026-11-01, decision 53)

The Claude desktop app is a packaged (MSIX) Windows app, and Windows redirects `%APPDATA%` for what it starts. A Code-tab shell's `C:\Users\<user>\AppData\Roaming\sqlh\profiles.toml` was served from `...\AppData\Local\Packages\Claude_<id>\LocalCache\Roaming\sqlh\profiles.toml`; a profile added in the user's shell, or in the app's Terminal panel, wrote the real Roaming file and never appeared there, and the run failed with `no profile named "..."` and nothing to say why. Credential Manager isn't split.

Probes with throwaway files, between a Code-tab shell (in the package) and the Terminal panel (not):

| Case | Result |
|---|---|
| A folder the real `AppData` has first; the package reads, adds to and changes files in it | passes through to the real files; nothing in the cache |
| A folder the package made first; the same name then made outside | the package sees only its cached copy |
| The package deletes its cached copy | the real file appears to it |
| The package deletes that real file | the real file is gone |
| The package writes under `%USERPROFILE%` | a real file, read from outside |

Built: the file is `%USERPROFILE%\.sqlh\profiles.toml`, and runs bring in what's under `%APPDATA%` (DESIGN.md §8). On the owner's machine, from a Code-tab shell, one `profiles list` took the cached copy (two profiles), then the real file behind it (a third): nothing lost, no conflict, the cached copy's `default` kept, and the Terminal panel then read the same file. `test` and `test2016` connect with the passwords already stored, and `sqlh mcp` run from that shell does too. Tests: unit tests in `config.rs` (a copy, a merge, a difference set aside, a broken file, a file that can't be removed, both moves in a row) and one process-level test. The uncovering of a real file can't be arranged in a test, so it rests on the probes and that one run.

Not measured: `sqlh mcp` launched by the desktop app itself, as its configured server; it should be the same as the Code-tab shell, also a child of the package. This touches where credentials' profiles are read, so it goes in the next external review (decision 52).

### Setting the default profile (owner, 2026-10-26, from manual testing; planned, not started)

`profiles.toml` has a top-level `default = "NAME"` (DESIGN.md §3 and §8: used when no `--profile`, no `SQLH_PROFILE` and no `-S`/`SQLCMDSERVER` is given), but nothing in the CLI sets it after the first `profiles add` (which makes the first profile the default, `profile.rs`, `became_default`). `profiles rm` of the default clears it silently (`Config::remove`), so removing the first of three profiles leaves two profiles, no default and no CLI way to choose one; the only fix is editing the file. `--help` mentions the default only as "Without -S: the default one" on the `--profile` line. (S)
1. **`sqlh profiles default NAME`** sets the default (`NAME` must exist, as the file's load check already requires; case as `find` matches it); **`sqlh profiles default --clear`** removes it. A text note says what it did, and the profiles file's path is written as typed (decision 38).
2. **`profiles rm` says so when it clears the default**, naming the fix: `NAME was the default profile: runs without -S or --profile now need one (sqlh profiles default NAME)`.
3. **`--help`:** one more line under `PROFILES`, and the `--profile` line points to it. Keep `--help` under about 60 lines (it is at 62 now, so trim elsewhere).
4. **Not chosen, with reasons:** promoting another profile when the default is removed (with two or more left it is a guess, and a wrong guess makes a bare `sqlh -Q ...` hit the wrong server silently, worse for an agent than a clear "no profile selected" error; promoting only when exactly one is left was considered and rejected for one rule in every case); an `add --default` flag (redundant once `profiles default` exists).
5. **Same change:** DESIGN.md §8 (the subcommand and the `rm` note), `src/help.txt`, tests (`profile.rs` unit tests with a fake store, a process-level test in `tests/cli.rs`, an `rm` of the default that prints the note), and `docs/agent-features.md` only if it changes what an agent gains (likely not: it is a person's setup command).
6. **Open:** the owner has not yet approved the design; record it in §6 as a decision when taken.

### Phase 6: command categorization (VISION priority 4)

**Build order (decision 51, 2026-11-01):** Part 1 in six thin slices, each released and used before the next, starting with refusal wording and the five small items; Part 2 waits for real use. §6, decision 51, lists the slices and the live checks each needs.

**Slice 1, as built:** decision 30(a), the note when the SQL itself reads uncommitted (DESIGN.md §4.3); decision 30(b), documented (DESIGN.md §7; docs only, decision 52); decision 30(c), the login's application name per run kind (DESIGN.md §4.3); decision 30(d), the refusal of `sys.dm_db_index_physical_stats` over every database in a mode that scans (DESIGN.md §7); decision 23's refusal wording, the "Now" list of command-categories.md §6.9 (DESIGN.md §7); decision 30(e), `sqlh mcp`'s refusal of `SET AUTOPILOT`, `SET IDENTITY_INSERT`, `OPEN SYMMETRIC KEY` and `OPEN MASTER KEY` (DESIGN.md §7, mcp-mode.md §5), until command-categories.md §10, item 5 tests the reset on them.

**Internal review of slice 1 (2026-11-01, three lenses, each finding reproduced first; not the external reviewer).** Everything here but the note's and the rules' own spellings is older than the slice, identical on `6df4269`.
- **Fixed, the lexer (P1 ×3, DESIGN.md §7, "Tokens end where the servers end them"):** a `--` comment ended only at LF, not at a lone CR; a number swallowed the letters glued to it (`1DELETE`, `$1DELETE`, `£1DELETE`); every byte >= 0x80 was a word character, though the servers read U+00A0, U+3000 and others as whitespace. Each reached the servers: `COMMIT` of the wrapper, `DELETE`, `DROP`, `EXEC`, `DBCC`, `SET NOEXEC OFF` under `--check`, and `WAITFOR` in `sqlh mcp`. The sets were measured for every code point on both servers. Tests: `guard::tests::tokens_end_where_the_servers_end_them`, `nothing_is_hidden_by_a_comment_a_number_or_a_space`, `the_guards_spaces_and_currency_signs_are_exactly_the_servers`; `a_keyword_is_seen_past_a_lone_cr_a_glued_number_or_a_unicode_space`, `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`; live, `the_servers_read_the_guards_characters_as_it_does`.
- **Fixed, found while checking those (not in the reviewers' list):** the servers compare words they don't reserve, and parameter names, by collation, so `SET NOEXEC<joiner> OFF` ran a `--check` input, `@updateusage<joiner> = ...` reached `sp_spaceused`'s parameter past `ARG_CHECKS`, and `NEXT<joiner> VALUE FOR` and `BEGIN DIALOG<joiner>` passed; money literals take spaces and a sign (`$ -1.5EXEC`); `\` is a currency sign. `guard::spells`, `guard::keyword`, `an_argument_check_reads_through_what_a_collation_ignores`, `the_fold_leaves_real_names_alone_and_weakens_nothing`.
- **Fixed, decision 30(d) and 30(e)'s spellings (P2 ×2):** the two above cover them; DESIGN.md §7 now says what is seen and names what isn't (a letter a collation ignores, accents under an accent-insensitive one). `the_cost_and_session_rules_read_past_every_space`, `what_a_collation_ignores_is_seen_where_a_rule_goes_by_a_name`.
- **Fixed, the note (P3 ×2):** trailing spaces in a quoted hint, a `#t` after a qualifier, and `TRIM(x FROM f(nolock))`, with `IS [NOT] DISTINCT FROM` (same shape, not in the list). `hints_are_found_in_every_spelling_the_lexer_can_see`, `a_from_inside_an_expression_opens_no_table_list`, live `the_note_goes_by_what_the_servers_take_as_a_hint`.
- **Fixed, wording (P3):** `--help` said `5 write blocked`, now `5 blocked`; under `--check` an unknown statement's advice promised a run (`to compile it as written`); the README's `query` row, mcp-mode.md's advice table and this plan's stale "would write" line (below); AGENTS.md's Status. U10 added to docs/notes/upstream-issues.md for the driver's DONE-token limit.
- **Not changed, settled by decision 30(d) (P3):** `sys.dm_db_index_physical_stats(NULL, 5, 1, NULL, 'DETAILED')` keeps its reason, though the servers would reject that call (2561): the decision's rule reads the first and the fifth arguments, a test holds the shape (`(null, 5, 1, 1, 'SAMPLED')`), and an `OBJECT_ID()` that returns NULL looks the same and does scan. DESIGN.md §7 now says so.

**Second internal review of slice 1 (2026-11-01, four lenses on `a16b69c`, each finding reproduced on both servers before its fix, with a release build of that commit, except where stated).** External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard).
- **Fixed, the lexer (P1 ×2, older or from `a16b69c`, not run on the older commits):** a sign after an exponent or a currency sign is the literal's with no digit after it, so `SELECT 1e--1 DELETE ...` and `SELECT $--1 DELETE ...` (also `$ --1`, `\--1`, `£--1`) hid a statement behind what the guard read as a comment. `guard::tests::tokens_end_where_the_servers_end_them`, `a_sign_after_an_exponent_or_a_currency_sign_hides_nothing`; live rows in `the_servers_read_the_guards_characters_as_it_does`.
- **Fixed, the batch's start (P1, P2 ×2, read as older than `a16b69c`):** `.p`, `...p`, `#p` and `##p 1` are procedure calls to the servers and skipped the allowlist. Found while fixing it: `.sp_who` and `.sp_spaceused 'a', 'true'` ran past the qualifier and argument checks. `guard::starts_an_implicit_call`, shared with `several_outputs`; `a_batch_that_starts_with_a_dot_or_a_temp_name_is_an_implicit_call`.
- **Fixed, the collation's letters (P1 ×4, P2, P3 ×3, older; `a16b69c` closed only the joiners):** `SET NOEXEC<U+0218> OFF` and `SET <U+0274>OEXEC OFF` ran real rows under `--check` and through the `check` tool, and `DISABLE`, `ENABLE`, `RECEIVE`, `SEND`, `CONVERSATION`, `DIALOG` and `NEXT VALUE FOR` so spelled reached their statements. `SET AUTOPILOT`, `OPEN SYMMETRIC KEY` and `OPEN MASTER KEY` passed decision 30(e), and the note and `XACT_ABORT`'s count missed their spellings. The measured table replaces the guess at "not alphanumeric": 10,700 code points in 173 ranges (`src/guard_ignored.rs`), 8 small capitals, 22 ligatures (`SET N<U+0153>XEC OFF`, which no reviewer had), DESIGN.md §7. Real names aren't refused: a whole word must spell the keyword, and `SEND时间` doesn't. `the_table_of_what_a_word_ignores_is_sorted_and_whole`, `what_a_collation_ignores_is_seen_where_a_rule_goes_by_a_name`, `spelling_goes_by_the_fold`, `hints_are_found_in_every_spelling_the_lexer_can_see`; live `the_servers_ignore_what_the_guard_ignores`, which sweeps the BMP.
- **Fixed, what the fold refused or silenced for nothing (P2, P3 ×3, new in `a16b69c`):** `Dele<U+0301>te` and a full-width `SAVE` as aliases were refused as writes, with no protection gained, since reserved words are exact on the servers. `DISTINCT<U+200D>`, `JOIN<U+200D>` and the like as aliases silenced the note. `guard::keyword` now folds only `COLLATED`, the words the servers don't reserve, and only by what they do: no full-width form (their error 195) and no U+0301 (102). `the_fold_leaves_real_names_alone_and_weakens_nothing`.
- **Fixed, decision 30(d)'s spellings (P2, older in the rule):** the name and mode by the same letters, and a mode without the `N` by best fit (`'D<U+0112>TAILED'`), which isn't the collation's doing. Found while fixing it: the parameter is `nvarchar(20)` and the servers cut what is longer, so `'DETAILED            zz'` scanned. A character beyond ASCII in a mode now counts as the letter in its place, which refuses only what is the servers' error 2561 anyway. Live `the_servers_take_the_spellings_the_scan_refusal_goes_by`.
- **Fixed, `ADD SIGNATURE` (P1, older):** `ADD SIGNATURE`, `ADD COUNTER SIGNATURE` and, found while fixing it, `ADD SENSITIVITY CLASSIFICATION` (2019 and later) were in no list, and reached the servers anywhere but a batch's first word. `add_signature_and_classification_are_refused_where_they_stand`.
- **Fixed, smaller (P3 ×2):** `GO --c<CR>SELECT 2` dropped the statement without a word (a CR ended the comment everywhere but there; older), and is now the server's error 102 (`a_statement_after_a_lone_cr_on_a_go_line_is_not_lost`). `SET SHOW<joiner>PLAN_XML ON` now counts in `sets_output_modes` and `several_outputs`; a stopped count cancelling statements unseen was read, not reproduced (older). The note after an alias spelled like a temp table and on a name with a leading dot (older).
- **Fixed, tests, docs, cost (P3 ×6, new in `a16b69c`):** two live rows that couldn't fail now end in garbage; the mutants that survived (`XACT_ABORT`, `ISOLATION`, `LEVEL`, the nested count, `depth_bit`, the byte after a CR, `c as u8`) each have a row, though the mutation run wasn't repeated. `READ` and `KEY` are exact, as on the servers. The false comments, `tokens`, DESIGN.md's counts (19,181 inside `NOE<c>XEC`, measured here; the reviewers' 19,180 and 19,183 came from other shapes, and the old 21,230 and their 21,231 include the surrogates or close to it, not run down further). A name over 128 UTF-16 units is error 103, so a comparison reads at most 384 bytes of one: the worst shape, 10 times the pre-rewrite cost at `a16b69c` (`BEGIN ` and joiners, 1,065 ms), is 224 ms against 104 ms, within the budget's comment again.
- **Built, decision 30(d) as the owner amended it (2026-11-02, "yes, add them all"; §6, decision 30):** the refusal takes `0`, `'0'`, `''` and `N''` as the database, and a binary literal as the mode, decoded as UTF-16 LE and refused only when it reads as `DETAILED` or `SAMPLED`. Measured on both servers with `'LIMITED'` and an object id, which is error 2561 with no database and reads nothing, and with scoped calls for a binary mode: every other literal that converts to the `smallint` 0 acts the same and is in the rule, the same rule: numbers under 1, since a fraction is cut (`00`, `0.0`, `.0`, `0e0`, `0.9`, `1e-5`), a number's sign (`-0`, `+0`), `money` that rounds to 0 (`$`, `$0`, `$0.49`), binary whose last two bytes are 0 (`0x`, `0x00`, `0x010000`), strings of spaces, one sign and zeros (`N'0'`, `'00'`, `' '`, `' 0'`, `'+'`), and without the `N` a character beyond ASCII, by best fit (`'<U+3000>'`, `'<U+FF10>'`). DESIGN.md §7 has each, with what passes (`1.0`, `0x0001`, `$0.5`, `'1'`, `'0 0'`). A sign on anything but a number stays out as an expression, as does `'DET' + 'AILED'`, though `+''`, `+NULL` and `+'DETAILED'` do act (DESIGN.md §7). In the mode, measured and built: a last lone byte is a unit, odd digits are padded on the left, a NUL and the 20th unit cut it, no best fit. Cost on 9.4 MB of one binary literal, closed port, median of 7, exit 5 at a `DELETE` after it: as the first argument 73 ms before, 72 ms after; as the mode 71 ms before, 76 ms after (the digits counted once for the padding, 20 units decoded); split every 80 digits 75 ms, 83 ms. `every_literal_the_servers_take_as_no_database_is_refused`, `a_first_argument_that_is_a_real_database_or_no_literal_passes`, `a_binary_mode_is_read_as_the_servers_read_it`, `a_long_binary_literal_costs_no_more_than_its_length`; a process test, MCP rows; live `the_servers_take_as_no_database_what_the_scan_refusal_goes_by` and `the_servers_take_the_spellings_the_scan_refusal_goes_by` on both servers. External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard): goes in the next brief.
- **Fixed, found while measuring that (P1, older):** a binary literal goes on past a backslash at a line's end, and the lexer ended it at the backslash, so `SELECT 0xAA\<LF>AWAITFOR DELAY '00:00:02'` passed the guard as a word `AWAITFOR` and waited 2 s on both servers in a read-only run, where the server read `0x0AAA` and a statement. Any statement goes the same way (`AUPDATE`, not run: the wait shows the parse). `guard::number_end` now goes on with the literal and counts its lines; `a_keyword_after_a_binary_literal_split_over_lines_is_seen`, rows in `tokens_end_where_the_servers_end_them`, `nothing_is_hidden_by_a_comment_a_number_or_a_space`, `plain_tokens_end_where_the_lexer_ends_them` and `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`; live `the_servers_read_a_binary_literal_past_a_backslash_at_a_lines_end`. `script::split` reads a line at a time and still sees the backslash as a `money` literal, which only decides whether a later `--` on that line is a comment, and the guard reads the whole text.
- **Confirm step on `47ee73c` (2026-11-02, read-only, both servers; no call that could mean every database was sent with `DETAILED` or `SAMPLED`):** no P1. Sweeps: about 1.1M first-argument texts up to 5 characters over a 16-character alphabet, every ASCII pair and BMP code point around the usual literals (2025; 2016 up to 4 characters and the backslash and mode sweeps), and every BMP unit in and around each letter of the modes, in binary and with the `N`; all 242,488 scanning spellings are refused. External review is still owed (decision 52).
  - **Fixed, a backslash and a lone CR (P2, in the amended rule's class; the bytes also passed at `b9aa3a6`):** the servers take them as the end of a binary literal, so `0x\<CR>` and `0x00\<CR>` as the database and `0x4400...\<CR>` as the mode act as without them, and they passed, through every route tried. No statement could hide this way. `guard::number_end` now takes them and ends the literal; rows in `every_literal_the_servers_take_as_no_database_is_refused`, `a_binary_mode_is_read_as_the_servers_read_it`, `tokens_end_where_the_servers_end_them`, `plain_tokens_end_where_the_lexer_ends_them`, `sqlh mcp`'s refusal rows and the three live tests.
  - **Fixed, a test (P3):** removing `binary_as_text`'s cut at 20 units survived mutation, 46 of 52 killed; `a_long_binary_literal_costs_no_more_than_its_length` now holds it. The other five survivors differ only on the servers' syntax errors, or not at all.
  - **Cost beyond the one literal above (P3, not changed):** linear in all 20 shapes at 4.7 MB and 9.4 MB, no budget constant changed. Against `b9aa3a6` the largest ratios: eight nested calls with a big binary 77 ms to 170 ms, signs 132 ms to 220 ms, a split string 99 ms to 178 ms, calls with a binary mode 150 ms to 205 ms; the worst in absolute time, names with `0` in four arguments, 301 ms to 402 ms.
  - **Known, false refusals (P3, not changed):** a five-argument call with a non-ASCII schema and name, a `0` or the like first and a mode that scans, is refused, as with `NULL` before (`guard::names`); `<U+00E9>.<U+00E9>.<U+00E9>(0,` nested more than 8 times is now "too complex", which fails closed.
  - **Decided, not changed (owner, 2026-11-02; §6, decision 30):** a sign on anything but a number (`+''`, `+NULL`, `+'DETAILED'`), which the servers take, stays out and documented as an expression (DESIGN.md §7).

**Findings of the last confirm step on `57e81b4` (2026-11-02, each reproduced at that commit, on both servers where a server is the point: with its release build, or with its guard's functions in a unit run for `OPEN SYM<mark>ETRIC KEY` and `adds_output`, whose statements were parsed on the servers).** External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this round's commits go in its brief.
- **Fixed, repeat marks (P1, P2, P3):** eight characters read as the letter before them, in place of the second of two that match, and `guard::word_letters` had no such rule (DESIGN.md §7, which has what was swept). So `ADD SENSITIVITY CLAS<mark>IFICATION` reached SQL Server 2025 in a read-only run (error 16108 for a table that doesn't exist; 2016 has no such statement, so its 102); `OPEN SYM<mark>ETRIC KEY` passed decision 30(e) on both; the note was missing for `READ UNCOM<mark>ITTED` and `READUNCOM<mark>ITTED`, and the level did change. `guard::is_repeat_mark`; `a_repeat_mark_stands_only_for_the_second_of_two_letters`, which tries every position of every word the comparison reads and finds the five with a pair, `a_repeat_mark_is_seen_by_each_rule_that_goes_by_the_comparison`; a row each in `a_keyword_is_seen_past_a_lone_cr_a_glued_number_or_a_unicode_space` and `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`; live `the_servers_read_a_repeat_mark_as_the_letter_before_it`, `a_repeat_mark_takes_effect_and_is_reported`.
- **Fixed, narrower than reported (P3):** `SET SHOWPLAN_AL<mark> ON` as written was already counted: `adds_output`'s `SHOWPLAN_` prefix matches it. What it missed was the mark with anything beyond ASCII in the first nine bytes as well (`SET SHOW<joiner>PLAN_AL<mark> ON`, `SET S<U+029C>OWPLAN_AL<mark> ON`), which both servers read as the option. Same fix and tests.
- **Fixed, found while measuring (P3, older):** a hint after `NOLOCK` with spaces was compared exactly, so `WITH (NOLOCK FORCESEEK<joiner>)` and `WITH (NOLOCK FORCESE<mark>K)`, both of which parse, got no note. `a_hint_after_nolock_goes_by_the_comparison`.
- **Not changed (P3):** `MERGE t USING<joiner> s (NOLOCK)` gets no note, under `--allow-write` only. Reading `USING<joiner>` as the word would lose the note on `FROM t USING<joiner> (NOLOCK)`, an alias and a hint in a read, which `hints_are_found_in_every_spelling_the_lexer_can_see` holds. DESIGN.md §4.3 states it.
- **Fixed, `script::split` and the guard (P3, fails closed):** the split took `1e--` and `$--` as comments after `0f2a097`, so `SELECT 1e-- 1 AS [a<LF>GO<LF>b]` was refused, exit 5, on both servers (`b starts the batch`). It now ends names, numbers and `money` literals where the guard does. `guard::plain_token_end` repeats the arms of `Lexer::read` rather than sharing a function, which cost 20% (218 ms against 182 ms on 9.4 MB of ASCII text), and `plain_tokens_end_where_the_lexer_ends_them` holds the two together on every pair of pieces and 20,000 random lines. `a_sign_a_literal_takes_is_no_comment_when_finding_terminators`, live `a_go_after_a_literals_sign_stays_in_the_batch`.
- **Found, a binary literal as the mode (P3):** `0x4400...` is `N'DETAILED'` in UTF-16 and scans on both servers (with a scoped call). Refused since, by the owner's amendment of decision 30(d) above.
- **Fixed, the mode's cut (P3, a test):** `the_mode_is_cut_at_twenty_utf16_units` tells units from bytes (`N'DE<6 joiners>TAILED'`) and from characters (`N'DETAILED<6 supplementary>zz'`), and has `cut_to_units`' own table; live rows in `the_servers_take_the_spellings_the_scan_refusal_goes_by`, with 13 joiners as their 2561.
- **Corrected, docs (P3 ×2):** DESIGN.md §7's "nothing else parses in its place" was false; it now says what was swept and what wasn't. The two README entries that said every character on the usual collation now say as measured, and that a spelling nobody tried isn't ruled out. Its costliest shape was no longer the costliest: `ADD ` and one joiner, 367 ms, against 216 ms for the one it named; still linear.

**Third internal review of slice 1 (2026-11-02, three testers on `6d18321`, each finding reproduced on both servers with a release build of that commit, except where stated; all of them older than the slice).** External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard, the input's decoding); this round's commit goes in its brief.
- **Fixed, a dot after a temp name (P1 ×2, P3):** `require_scratch` read one token, so `#x.dbo.t`, `#x.t` and `#x.a.b.c` passed as temp tables in `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `INTO`, `TRUNCATE`, `ALTER TABLE`, `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX` and `DROP TABLE`, and reached the servers (2702, 3701, 208, 1088, 7202), and `DROP TABLE #a.b, dbo.T` stopped its list check at the first name, so every later name went unread and was dropped until the rollback (shown only on a temp table and on names that don't exist; no real table was dropped). A dot after the name now refuses it. `a_dot_after_a_temp_name_makes_it_no_temp_table`, `a_write_past_a_dotted_temp_name_never_connects`, rows in `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`.
- **Fixed, `RAISERROR ... WITH LOG` (P2):** on no list, so it passed every read-only run and `sqlh mcp`, and a rollback can't undo the log entry. Seen by parsing and by `IF 1=0 RAISERROR(...) WITH LOG` reaching the servers; the entry itself was never made. Refused by the `WITH`, with the options by the comparison (measured, DESIGN.md §7); a CTE named `log` passes, and `WITH LOG (SELECT 1)`, which the first draft of the rule would have let by as a CTE, doesn't. `raiserror_with_log_is_refused_and_a_cte_called_log_is_not`, `with_log_stays_linear`, live `the_servers_parse_what_the_target_rules_go_by`.
- **Fixed, `$(SQLCMDPASSWORD)` (P2):** the fallback to the environment printed the password sqlh logs in with, in rows, refusals (with no server at all) and server errors, and sent it as SQL text. Now exit 3, in any case, unless `-v` gives it. `the_password_variable_is_never_read_from_the_environment`, `the_password_variable_is_never_substituted_from_the_environment`. Other environment variables still resolve: below.
- **Fixed, the `MERGE` hints (P2):** `INNER MERGE JOIN`, `OPTION (MERGE JOIN)` and `OPTION (MERGE UNION)` were refused as `MERGE`, with advice (`[MERGE]`) that is a syntax error there, and no way to run them under `deny` or in `sqlh mcp`. `JOIN` and `UNION` are reserved, so they can't be the statement's target, and they are matched exactly, since `MERGE JOIN<U+0218> USING` parses as the statement. `several_outputs` doesn't count the hint as a second statement. `the_merge_hints_are_reads_and_the_statement_is_not`, live `a_hint_or_a_scratch_write_the_guard_refused_now_runs`. No §6 decision names `MERGE`; only DESIGN.md's "anywhere", now corrected.
- **Fixed, `ALTER TABLE #t SWITCH TO dbo.T` (P3):** the target after `TO` went unread, and reached 2025 and 2016 (1088; the tester's timeout on 2016 didn't come back, with nothing else on the server). `a_switch_from_a_temp_table_goes_to_a_scratch_table_only`, the live test above runs `#t` to `#u`.
- **Fixed, what a refusal said (P3 ×6):** `[#t]`, `"#t"` and `tempdb..#t` stay refused, as the rule is one bare token's, but say `Write it as #t` first (`a_temp_table_written_another_way_is_told_how_to_write_it`; the options are below). `TOP (n) PERCENT` made `PERCENT` the target (`a_top_clauses_percent_is_no_target`). `FOR UPDATE` was `UPDATE ? would write` (`a_cursor_for_update_is_refused_for_what_it_is`). A byte order mark in `-Q` or mid-file read as `SELECT starts the batch` (`a_byte_order_mark_in_a_refusal_can_be_seen`, `a_refusal_is_short_and_shows_a_byte_order_mark`). A name of 5,000,000 characters came back whole, to `--max-output` (`a_refusal_cuts_a_name_that_cant_be_real`). `LINENO n`, a real statement, was refused as a call at a batch's start, and now passes as it did mid-batch, since `LINENO 5 sys.sp_who` is error 102 (`lineno_starts_no_call`).
- **Partly not reproduced (P3):** the same finding said the batch-start reason is false for `DUMP`, `LOAD` and `DISK`. At a batch's start the servers do read them as procedures (error 2812), though mid-batch they are 102, so the reason is true. It is false for a reserved word that starts no statement (`FROM x`, 156). Left, with "(a typo?)", and stated in DESIGN.md §7.
- **Fixed, input (P3 ×2):** UTF-16 without a BOM and UTF-32 decoded as text with NULs, and the guard refused it with a false reason and the offer of a flag; now exit 3 (`wide_text_without_a_usable_bom_is_no_utf8`, `wide_text_without_a_usable_bom_is_a_usage_error`). `-c ""` made every blank or comment line a terminator and `-c " "` could never match; now exit 3 (`a_batch_terminator_is_one_word`, `a_batch_terminator_with_no_word_in_it_is_a_usage_error`).
- **Documented (P3 ×5):** "fails closed" overstated the classifier, which mid-batch is lists of refusals (DESIGN.md §7 now says what passes, and the README's Safety section says it plainly); a CTE or a derived table spelled like a temp table passes, as an alias does; 911 and 245 end the wrapper too, and the stop overrides `--continue-on-error` (live `an_ordinary_error_that_ends_the_wrapper_stops_the_run`); `sp_help*` is a list of names; `AI_GENERATE_EMBEDDINGS`, since refused (below, decision 55). The budget's costliest shape is `#,` at 2.5 units a byte, not `#a,` at 1.7, which predates this round; a test holds it.
- **The check of this round's fixes (one tester on `afaf04c`, both servers; no P1 or P2, about 240 hand-written neighbours, BMP sweeps of 68 shapes, differential lexer fuzz with no hit, 41 mutants of two fixes):** its five P3s are closed. A byte order mark was printed raw in three refusals (the `BLOCKED` arm, a global temp name, an unknown DBCC); `shown` now writes it out, so every path does. `WITH log (#a) AS` was refused as `RAISERROR`; a temp-shaped column name now counts (`names_a_cte`). `keyword`'s carrying of the letter past an ignored character had no test, the one surviving mutant; it has a row. `ALTER TABLE #t` passes only `ADD` and `SWITCH`, which DESIGN.md §7 now says; the refusal of `ALTER COLUMN` and the rest stays, closed, for later. A numbered `RAISERROR` is the question below.
- **Not attempted, a much larger design:** a mid-batch refusal of what is on no list needs statements found, which the classifier doesn't do without semicolons (classifier v2, Phase 6).
- **Tests that leaned on the long refusal:** the three slow-reader tests of R11 used a 96 KB refusal as a response bigger than a pipe. They now use `undefined variable $(...)`, which still quotes its name whole, twice (48,000 characters each), exit 3. That error is the next thing a cut like the refusal's would reach; it wasn't in the findings, and an agent meets it only by typing such a name.
- **Built, `AI_GENERATE_EMBEDDINGS` refused (decision 55, 2026-11-02, on `9e80926`; both servers).** It was this list's question; DESIGN.md §7 has the rule. External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this commit goes in its brief.
  - **Measured first, and what that decided.** By parsing and compiling only, with a model that doesn't exist (15151), and no model defined; none exists on the server (`sys.external_models`). Only the bare word reaches the function: any ASCII case, comments and whitespace before `(`, `USE MODEL m`, and a second form no one had listed, `AI_GENERATE_EMBEDDINGS(x, m)`. `[...]` and `"..."` are 102, a schema is 156 or 4121 (a user's function), so OPENROWSET's reading of quoted names is enough. The sweep (`sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set`, 45 places, every BMP code point but the surrogates, 2,856,960 parses, 2025) found no character read into the word, so it is exact, unlike the unreserved words of `COLLATED`: `AI_GENERATE<U+0218>_EMBEDDINGS`, small capitals, `EMBED<mark>INGS`, a full-width letter and an accent are 195, and pass. The word isn't reserved on either server, so a bare column, alias, table or CTE of that name loses its bare spelling, as for `SEND`, and `[...]` gets it back.
  - **Built.** `guard::SENDS_TEXT_OUT`, an arm of `classify` on the token, since `keyword` gives "" for a word over `KEYWORD_MAX`, which stays 16. The reason adds `: it can send its text to an outside endpoint`, and the advice is an unknown procedure's (`to run it as written`), since the function writes nothing. Not in `BLOCKED` as code, though DESIGN.md's list names it. `ai_generate_embeddings_is_refused_by_its_bare_word`, `ai_generate_embeddings_never_connects_unless_writes_are_allowed`, a row in `refusals_say_what_they_are_and_what_lifts_them`, two in `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`, `mcp::ai_generate_embeddings_is_refused_in_each_tool_that_takes_sql`, live `the_servers_read_ai_generate_embeddings_as_the_guard_does` (both profiles; it also runs the `[...]` advice, `--allow-write` to 15151 or 156, and `describe`, `def`, `find`, `deps` over a column and a view holding the word). sqlh's own SQL holds no such word and isn't classified.
  - **The cost** (the process's start to the refusal at a `DELETE` after 9.4 MB, closed port, median of 7, the two exes in turn; a copy of one exe differs by up to 2.3%, and a rebuild of `9e80926` by up to 3.5%): ASCII text 215.7 ms to 215.9 ms; beyond ASCII 196.7 ms to 201.7 ms; `a,` 359.0 ms to 352.5 ms; `ADD ` and a joiner 189.5 ms to 199.3 ms; `BEGIN ` and a joiner 173.2 ms to 186.3 ms, with 120 joiners 147.8 ms to 136.1 ms; `<U+00E9> ` 271.4 ms to 263.2 ms; `sys.sys.` 260.0 ms to 266.4 ms; `<U+00E9>.<U+00E9>(` 361.2 ms to 359.9 ms; `<U+00E9>.<U+00E9>(NULL,1,1,1,1) ` 329.0 ms to 330.7 ms; the stats call with `'LIMITED'` 164.2 ms to 175.1 ms; eight nested `<U+00E9>.<U+00E9>(NULL,` 917.9 ms to 956.5 ms, still the costliest; `DROP TABLE #,#,...` 414.8 ms to 425.9 ms. `[AI_GENERATE_EMBEDDINGS] ` repeated 65.5 ms to 68.0 ms. **A text of nothing but long words rises:** 17, 22 and 23 letters 84.9 ms to 98.4 ms, 81.8 ms to 97.9 ms, 82.8 ms to 94.2 ms, 22 letters with one beyond ASCII 108.8 ms to 128.8 ms, the same 13 ms or so at 16 and at 48 letters (88.6 ms to 101.7 ms, 70.8 ms to 84.3 ms), less at 8 and 4 (115.9 ms to 128.0 ms, 161.0 ms to 170.3 ms), so by the byte of a word, and not by the word. It isn't the rule: `KEYWORD_MAX` at 22 with the word in `BLOCKED` (the first draft), and the refusal out of line, gave the same, and with both builds at `-C llvm-args=-align-loops=32` it is level from 2 to 48 letters (-2.2% to +2.6%), at this build's times (22 letters 92.6 ms to 95.0 ms), so `9e80926` had a loop that happened to align well. Three attempts didn't get that back, and the flag costs 31 KB, so it isn't taken; ordinary text is level. No budget changed. Exe 2,987,520 to 2,988,544 bytes, budget 3,145,728.
  - **Found while measuring, for the owner; no refusal added.** `sys.fn_ledger_retrieve_digests_from_url(@path)`, new since 2016, passes the guard as a read and is `OpenRowset(TABLE LEDGER_DIGESTS_FROM_URL, @path)` over a URL (its definition, read; never called), so its path carries text to a host the query names; `fn_get_audit_file_v2`, also new, takes a path as `fn_get_audit_file` does. Decision 25's D4b, not built, would refuse either only with a non-local literal, by its rule for any other `fn_` function: neither is on its list, so a variable path, the shape that carries text, stays past it. `sys.fn_full_dblog`, named in no doc until now, passes too and has a branch `OpenRowset(TABLE DBLOG, ..., N'URL', ..., @backup_account, @backup_container, ...)`; its arguments are plain names, so D4b's test wouldn't catch it; never called, and it may be Azure's only. And a server fault: on 2025 RTM (17.0.1000.7) `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set(N'SELECT VECTOR_DISTANCE(N''x'' USE MODEL m) AS d', NULL, 0)`, which passes the guard, ended the session (errors 207, then 596 at severity 21) and wrote stack dumps to the server's log folder: it was run twice, while checking whether `USE MODEL` binds in other functions, and left `SQLDump0002` to `SQLDump0004`, with no restart. The shape wasn't run again, or varied to see how far it goes, so whether a plain run of it does the same is unknown; no `AI_GENERATE_EMBEDDINGS` shape did it, and `AI_GENERATE_CHUNKS`, `VECTOR_DISTANCE` and `VECTOR_SEARCH` have no model to name (`VECTOR_SEARCH` is 156 here, with `PREVIEW_FEATURES` off).
- **Built, `USE MODEL` inside a call refused (decision 57, 2026-11-07, on `7e35880`; both servers).** It was this list's question, from the stack dumps of decision 55's measurement ("Found while measuring" above); DESIGN.md §7 has the rule. External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this commit goes in its brief.
  - **Measured first,** without `VECTOR_DISTANCE`: seven spellings inside `AI_GENERATE_EMBEDDINGS` with a model that doesn't exist, compiled by `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set`, the error log read before and after (no new dump). `MODEL` goes by the comparison of unreserved words (a joiner inside is still the word); quoted, it is 102.
  - **Built.** An arm of `classify`: `USE` at a depth above 0 with `MODEL` next (`Lexer::spells`), `Blocked::unknown` with `as_written`. Decision 55's test had 17 passing rows with `USE MODEL` after a name that isn't the function (the servers' 102 or 195); they now use the comma form, which tests the same name. `use_model_inside_a_call_is_refused`, `use_model_inside_a_call_never_connects_unless_writes_are_allowed`, rows in `refusals_say_what_they_are_and_what_lifts_them` and `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`, live `the_servers_read_use_model_as_the_guard_does`.

  - **Not done:** `VECTOR_DISTANCE(... USE MODEL ...)` run as it stands, to see whether it too ends its session; reporting the fault to Microsoft is the owner's to decide. The describe route stays open: the guard doesn't read strings.
- **Built, `sys.fn_ledger_retrieve_digests_from_url` refused (decision 56, 2026-11-07, on `ab0dc7a`; both servers).** It was this list's question; DESIGN.md §7 has the rule. External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this commit goes in its brief.
  - **Measured first** (`OBJECT_ID` alone, so the function was never called; 2025 in `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS`, and 2016, which has no such function): it resolves bare and as `sys.`, `master.sys.`, `master..`, `sqlh_test.sys.`, `[sys].[...]`, in any case, with a joiner, a small capital, a ligature, one full-width letter, and wholly full-width; not as `dbo.`, nor with an accent in that collation, nor as `fn_ledger_retrie` and full-width letters to the end. Its one parameter is `@path nvarchar(max)`. The error log had no new dump after.
  - **Built.** `guard::READS_A_URL`: in the main pass, a `Word` or `Quoted` token just before a `(` that `guard::names` takes for it and in which `guard::mostly_spelled` finds at least half its letters known, refused like decision 55's word (`Blocked::unknown`, `as_written`, held while a `TOP (` clause is open). The first draft compared every word with `names` alone: it refused every call of a function named beyond ASCII (`<U+8868>.<U+95A2><U+6570>(1)`, two unit tests caught it), whence `mostly_spelled`, and it cost ASCII text 5.8% and other shapes up to 20%, whence the comparison only at a `(`, with the token before kept (`Token` is `Copy`, a view of the input). `a_call_of_the_function_that_reads_a_url_is_refused_by_any_name_of_it`, `the_function_that_reads_a_url_never_connects_unless_writes_are_allowed`, rows in `refusals_say_what_they_are_and_what_lifts_them` and `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`, live `the_servers_resolve_the_function_that_reads_a_url_by_the_names_the_guard_goes_by`.
  - **The cost** (the process's start to the refusal at a `DELETE` after 9.4 MB, closed port, median of 7, the two exes in turn): ASCII text 210.0 ms to 208.5 ms, `<U+00E9> ` 263.1 ms to 252.7 ms, `f ` 289.3 ms to 274.0 ms, the name with no call 90.0 ms to 90.0 ms; text of nothing but calls, each name now compared: `f(1) ` 251.2 ms to 273.4 ms (8.8%), `<U+00E9>.<U+00E9>(1) ` 298.8 ms to 315.2 ms (5.5%), a near miss and `(1) ` 103.0 ms to 111.3 ms (8.1%). Exe 2,988,544 to 2,990,080 bytes.
  - **To D4b, not built (command-categories.md D4b):** `fn_get_audit_file_v2` joins its list; `fn_full_dblog` needs a rule of its own, its URL branch taking plain names.
- **Built, a `RAISERROR` that doesn't quote its message refused (decision 59, 2026-11-07; both servers).** It was this list's question; DESIGN.md §7 has the rule. External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this commit goes in its brief.
  - **Measured first** (catalog reads only; no event-logged message raised): 669 event-logged messages from 14151 to 49968 on both servers, over 600 at severity 18 or below; no SQL Server Agent alert on either (`msdb.dbo.sysalerts`), so an alert firing is from the documentation. `RAISERROR('x', 20, 1)` without `WITH LOG` is error 2754 on both, with a sysadmin login, nothing raised: severity 19 and above stays behind the `WITH LOG` refusal.
  - **Built.** An arm of `classify`: `RAISERROR` not followed by `(` and a `Str` token (`guard::quotes_its_message`, one read of the two tokens), `Blocked::write_or` with the advice `Quote the message instead, as in RAISERROR('%s', 16, 1, @msg)` and `as_written`. `raiserror_raises_only_a_quoted_message`; WITH LOG's test row `RAISERROR(50000, 1, 1) WITH LOG, NOWAIT` now quotes its message. `a_numbered_raiserror_never_connects_unless_writes_are_allowed`, rows in `refusals_say_what_they_are_and_what_lifts_them` and `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`, live `the_quoted_raiserror_the_refusal_advises_raises_what_the_variable_form_does` (the catalog's premise, and the advised form raising error 50000 with the variable's text, as `RAISERROR(@msg, 16, 1)` under `--allow-write` does).
  - **The cost** (closed port, 9.4 MB, median of 7, the two exes in turn): ASCII text 208.1 ms to 207.7 ms, `PRINT 1 ` 171.6 ms to 172.0 ms, nothing but `RAISERROR('x', 10, 1) ` 147.8 ms to 159.8 ms (8.1%; two `Lexer::kind` lookups cost 13.2%). Exe 2,990,592 to 2,991,104 bytes.
- **Built, a temp table's name refused where it can only be an alias or a CTE's name (decision 61, 2026-11-07; both servers).** It was documented in DESIGN.md §7 and never asked; DESIGN.md §7 has the rule. External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this commit goes in its brief.
  - **Measured first** (compiling only, `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set`, in `tempdb` and `sqlh_test`, both servers): 32 shapes of where a `#` alias goes (every alias place, and as a CTE's name, quoted too; not after a `WITH (...)` hint or `TABLESAMPLE`), and 14 spellings of an alias against a bare `#o`: the quoted, padded, ignored-character and full-width ones bind, a leading space doesn't. Then a BMP sweep of `=` under the collation: `#` and `<U+FF03>` equal `#`, 19,179 characters vanish before a name, none in ASCII; the bare-word table `guard_ignored.rs` has 10,700, all but U+0E4C among them, so it couldn't serve.
  - **Built.** An arm of the main pass on `Temp` and `Quoted` tokens: `guard::reads_as_temp` (a superset of the collation's reading with no table), `guard::temp_place` by the token before (`precedes_a_temp_table`, by length then `eq_ignore_ascii_case`; a `TOP (` clause's `)` by a flag the pass sets when one closes), and `guard::temp_alias`, which settles the rest from the first byte past the name and any ASCII whitespace, reading ahead only after a comment, a byte beyond ASCII, or `AS` or `(` after a comma (`defines_a_cte`, which unlike `names_a_cte` needs `AS (`). `Blocked::write_or`, `Name it without the #`, `as_written`. The first draft carried a pending name to the next token; that state cost plain text 3% to 8%, so nothing is carried. `SET [IDENTITY_INSERT] #t ON`, the servers' 102, moved from the MCP state test's passing rows to the new test's refused ones. `a_temp_like_name_is_refused_where_it_can_only_be_an_alias_or_a_cte` (with the work of the shapes that look ahead), `a_write_through_an_alias_named_like_a_temp_table_never_connects`, rows in `refusals_say_what_they_are_and_what_lifts_them` and `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`, live `the_servers_bind_a_temp_name_to_the_aliases_the_guard_refuses`.
  - **The cost** (closed port, 9.4 MB, median of 7, the two exes in turn, against `a7c152d`): ASCII text 205.0 ms to 211.3 ms (3.1%, inside the 3.5% two rebuilds of one commit have differed by), quoted names 137.9 ms to 143.6 ms (4.2%), quoted names beyond ASCII 146.6 ms to 154.9 ms (5.6%), nothing but qualified temp columns 194.9 ms to 205.6 ms (5.5%), nothing but temp lists (`FROM #a, #b, #c `) 157.1 ms to 176.4 ms (12.3%, the costliest of any guard rule so far). With the arm compiled out, every shape was level. Exe 2,991,104 to 2,995,712 bytes.
- **Built, a listed procedure taken only in its lowercase spelling (decision 62, 2026-11-07; both servers).** From the measurement in "Another collation", open item (ii), below; DESIGN.md §7 has the rule. External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this commit goes in its brief.
  - **Built.** In `check_proc`, after the qualifier rule: a name of `PROCS` with an ASCII capital, or `sys` or `dbo` with one (`local_system_qualifier` now gives `guard::Qualifier`: `Local`, `Cased`, `Other`), is refused, `Blocked::write_or` with `Write it as <name in lowercase>` and `as_written`. `every_list_matches_in_any_case` now holds each listed name in lowercase, `sys.`, `[MASTER].[dbo].`, `Master.sys.`, `mAsTeR..` and alone, and five other spellings refused; `long_words_are_names_not_keywords` refuses `SP_DESCRIBE_UNDECLARED_PARAMETERS`. `a_listed_procedure_in_another_case_never_connects`, rows in `refusals_say_what_they_are_and_what_lifts_them` and `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`.
  - **Not done:** a live test. It would need a case-sensitive database made on every run, a database-level change beyond decision 21; the measurement stands as recorded.
- **Server faults these tests caused, on the 2025 test server (error log, read 2026-11-02; no restart, start time unchanged).** The three dumps of decision 55's measurement, above. And two stack overflows earlier the same day (exception `c00000fd`, 02:05:27 and 02:06:04, server time): the log's input buffer is a confirm step's query from the review of the lexer, deep `/*` nesting built with `REPLICATE` and given to `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set`. They weren't recorded when they happened. Nor were three more on the 2016 test server from the same query (`c00000fd`, 02:04:59, 02:06:04 and 02:07:20, server time; found 2026-11-07 in its previous log, after both hosts' instances stopped at 11:47 for a system shutdown, "SQL Server is terminating because of a system shutdown", and started again at 11:55 and 11:58). No dump line on either server since 2026-11-02. Testers go by the log from now on: before and after a sweep that hands a server odd text, read `xp_readerrorlog` for `SQLDump` and report any new line. The files in the server's log folder are the owner's to delete.

**Another collation, measured (2026-11-02, on `03eb5ec`).** The owner approved one scratch database for this on 2026-11-02: `sqlh_collation_ai`, `Latin1_General_100_CI_AI`, empty, on the 2025 test server, whose own collation is `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS`, with `sqlh_test` as the control; dropped the same day, once the fix below was checked. Two lenses and a check of them, nothing written, no `--allow-write`; the `NULL` shape of the stats function was never sent, and its verdicts come from the exe against a closed port. What follows the database's collation and what doesn't is in DESIGN.md §7, "Near, not whole". External review is still owed (decision 52: the guard); this round's commit goes in its brief.
- **Answered, the parser:** keywords and tokens don't follow a database's collation: 428 templates over the whole BMP, 56.1 million parses in all, 427 identical, the one difference a column name's binding. So `DIS<U+00C1>BLE` is a name in such a database. The allowlists and `ARG_CHECKS` stay closed, and the stats mode doesn't fold either.
- **Fixed, decision 30(d)'s names (P2):** names do follow the database, and the rule read a character it didn't know as nothing. `s<U+00FD>s.dm_db_index_physical_stats(NULL,NULL,NULL,NULL,'DETAILED')` passed the guard, where the plain spelling is exit 5. By a sweep of every position and every BMP code point, 893 spellings resolve in that database and 118 in the control; 775 passed, and the other 118 were the control's set. A three-part name reaches it from any database on the server, and so do `USE` and `sqlh mcp`. `guard::names` now reads a character beyond ASCII as any number of the target's letters, with no table, so no collation's folds beyond ASCII matter to it: all 893 are exit 5, bracketed and bare, through the release exe. Collations that fold ASCII itself are the third open item below. Allowlists compare exactly, so no other rule needs this (DESIGN.md §7 goes through each). `a_name_goes_by_its_ascii_letters_alone` (with every name up to six characters over small alphabets held to the rule written the slow way), `an_accent_doesnt_hide_the_scan_of_every_database`, `the_stats_scan_counts_the_name_however_it_is_spelled`, process-level `an_accented_spelling_of_the_scan_never_connects`, a row in `mcp::a_call_cannot_hide_a_statement_from_the_guard`. No live test: it would depend on the scratch database.
- **Found while fixing it (no finding had it):** a bit per letter, as first estimated, was fine on the match but not on the rest. Any name beyond ASCII now spells `sys`, so the lookahead from it read such text twice, 24% to 32% more on `<U+00E9> ` and the like. Decision 30(d)'s call is now followed in the pass (`guard::StatsCallSeen`), with no lookahead, which also takes 13% off `sys.sys.`, and a run of ASCII is found by moving on through the target rather than by a bit per letter. The scan's limit counts a name only with its `(`, or a subquery over nine columns beyond ASCII was refused as too complex. The figures are in DESIGN.md §7, "The cost". ASCII text costs 3.9% and `a,` 4.6% more, which isn't the "no costlier" the fix aimed at; the costliest shape is level.
- **The cost of the reading:** a call of a user's function whose schema and name have no ASCII letter to tell them from the target, with `NULL` first and a literal scanning mode fifth, is refused, and so are nine calls of such a function inside a `NULL`-first call's arguments, as too complex (DESIGN.md §7; a test holds the first).
- **The check of the fix (one tester on `fe9bc58`; no P1 or P2, four P3s, all closed in the commit that records them):** no spelling that resolves in the measured database passes: the 893 again on the exact exe, 19 shapes each over the whole BMP, 1,200 random many-letter spellings and 58 special shapes, with every route (`-Q`, `-i`, `-v`, `--check`, a later batch, `CROSS APPLY`, a subquery, a CTE, `IF EXISTS`, `TOP`). 4,636 SQL files showed no new refusal but the known shape. Of 36 mutants of the fix, 28 were killed, four survivors now have test rows, and the tester judged the others equivalent. Its findings: the docs' "depends on no collation" overclaimed (below), the nine-call refusal was undocumented, the costliest shape wasn't the one named (DESIGN.md §7, "The cost"), and those test gaps. It also measured the control characters the fix had added unmeasured: they resolve in neither database, so that reading is gone again.
- **Open, for the owner:**
  - **(i) A server installed under an accent-insensitive collation (decided: decision 54, documented, no instance for `0.1.0`).** Unmeasured, because the parser's keyword comparison may be fixed or may follow the server, and on a server of the collation it matches the two can't be told apart. If it follows the server, `SET N<U+00D3>EXEC ON`, a second-statement `DIS<U+00C1>BLE TRIGGER`, `ALTER TABLE #t SW<U+00CD>TCH TO dbo.t` and `OPEN SYM<U+00C9>TRIC KEY` would be real statements the guard passes (each passes it now, through the exe on a closed port), and `src/guard_ignored.rs` would be the wrong table. The transaction wrapper limits the damage to data, but not session state in `sqlh mcp`. Settling it takes an instance installed under such a collation and these sweeps rerun, which matters most if colleagues' servers aren't `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS`.
  - **(ii) A case-sensitive database (measured 2026-11-07, at the owner's word: "Measure it first in a case-sensitive scratch database"; decided and built: decision 62, below).** `EXEC SP_WHO` passes the allowlist, which is case-insensitive in ASCII, and in a `_CS` database that name isn't `sys.sp_who`. Measured in a database of its own, `sqlh_cs_probe` (`Latin1_General_CS_AS`), on both servers, with user procedures `dbo.SP_WHO`, `dbo.Sp_Who`, `dbo.sp_who`, `dbo.XP_READERRORLOG`, `dbo.SP_HELP` and, in a user schema `[SYS]`, `sp_who` and `SP_WHO`, each returning a marker; the database was dropped after. Through sqlh, read-only, each passed the guard and ran the user's procedure: `EXEC SP_WHO`, `EXEC Sp_Who`, `SP_WHO` alone, `EXEC SYS.sp_who`, `EXEC SYS.SP_WHO`, `EXEC XP_READERRORLOG` (the user's, error 8146: it takes no parameters), `EXEC SP_HELP`. The exact spellings ran the system procedure, even beside `dbo.sp_who`: `sp_who`, `sys.sp_who`, `sp_help`, `xp_readerrorlog`; so did every `master.` form in any case (`master..SP_WHO`, `master.SYS.sp_who`, `MASTER..sp_who`), the server being case-insensitive. Other cases are the servers' 2812 (`sP_wHo`, `sys.SP_WHO`, `Sys.sp_who`, `Sp_Help`). So in such a database, anyone who can create a procedure can have a read-only run execute it, with the caller's rights, under a listed name in another case. The premise of the qualifier rule holds only for the exact spelling.
  - **(iii) Collations that fold ASCII itself (decided: decision 63, documented).** `sis.dm_db_index_physical_stats` (Frisian), `[sy-s].` (Maori), `[sy:s].` (Mohawk) and `su<U+0308>s.` (Nordic) equal `sys` by `=` and pass in the `NULL` shape (DESIGN.md §7). That they resolve is untried, for want of a database of those collations. Only decision 30(d)'s cost rule depends on it, and a model won't type these by accident. Recommended: leave it documented.

**Phase 6 slice 2, as built (branch `phase6-slice2`, 2026-11-21 to 2026-11-26; decisions 83–90 and 92–95; awaiting its external review).** Built from decisions 83–89 and live check 10 (command-categories.md §10 item 10, done), then decisions 90 and 92–95 and the follow-ups below. External review is owed under decision 52 (the run policy, the output contract, the engine and MCP), on `865cdc9..` the follow-ups' last commit.
- **Built:** `info`'s last column, `server_permissions` (schema-commands.md §6.1), so `info`, MCP's `info` and `profiles test` say which server-wide reads the login may make; the lookup's label in its three fixed messages, and the rule that a server command's step after its lookup is one `SELECT` (`guard::several_outputs` false for it: no longer count and no `-- partial:` advice a command user can't act on; DESIGN.md §7's `INSERT ... EXEC` warning; docs/notes/upstream-issues.md U14 and U15); `sqlh errorlog [--since TIME] [--search TEXT] [--agent] [--archive N]` and the MCP tool `errorlog` (schema-commands.md §6.9; decisions 84 and 89); `sqlh activity` and the MCP tool `activity` (schema-commands.md §6.8; decisions 83, 85, 86 and 88). Then `--help`'s SERVER line and an `errorlog` example within its 62 lines (the PROFILES heading joined to its `add` line, its `--profile NAME uses one` already on CONNECTION's `--profile` line); the README's three entries (57 built, 2 planned) and its Planned entry, whose example of a community procedure is now `sp_Blitz` and which points to `activity` for what `sp_WhoIsActive` is mostly run for; AGENTS.md's Layout and Status; command-categories.md §6.7 (built, as the decisions amended it), §10 item 10 and its doc-fix bullet.
- **Commits and the exe after each** (`cargo build --release --locked`, each commit built on its own; the budget 3,145,728 bytes, decision 87): `865cdc9` (decisions 83–89, docs) 3,111,424; `4ed7865` (`server_permissions`) 3,112,448; `8e58afe` (the lookup's label, U14, U15) 3,112,448; `5690228` (`errorlog`) 3,126,784; `c13f934` (its internal review's fixes, below) 3,127,296; `00377cb` (`activity`) 3,134,464; this record's commit (`--help`, docs) 3,134,464. Slice 2 cost 23,040 bytes, past decision 87's estimate of 12–18 KB, and leaves 11,264 bytes of the budget; decision 87's measured proposal comes before slice 3.
- **MCP's share** (decision 20's gate, by mcp-mode.md §8's method: the same commit built as it is and with `sqlh mcp`'s dispatch replaced by a usage exit, which lets LTO drop `src/mcp/`, the engine and `serde_json`): 305,152 bytes, **298.0 KiB of 350 KiB at `00377cb`** (52.0 KiB left); 302,592 bytes (295.5 KiB) at `865cdc9`, so the two tools cost MCP 2.5 KiB. This base is narrower than the 309.0 KiB "over Phase 4" of `e60a1de` (it leaves the run's split in), mcp-mode.md §8.
- **Beyond what the decisions spelled out, each the smallest change that kept them:** from decision 88's text ("the timeout's advice naming the CLI's `sqlh activity`"), an `activity` call's wait for a free connection that times out ends `; sqlh activity on the command line opens a connection of its own` (`engine::wait_for_connection`, a message only; an engine file, so the brief lists it under decision 52); `@search` is `nvarchar(1000)`, not the `nvarchar(500)` first planned, so 500 characters outside the BMP fit (the review below).
- **Live tests added** (each passed, debug and release, in both full runs below): `info_names_the_logins_server_permissions`, `errorlog_reads_the_servers_and_the_agents_log`, `errorlog_finds_a_whole_entry_and_reads_back_through_archives`, `errorlog_needs_a_permission_and_says_which`, `mcp_tools::the_errorlog_tool_reads_the_log`, `mcp_tools::activity_shows_the_tests_own_chain_to_who_may_see_it`, `mcp_tools::the_activity_tool_shows_a_busy_sqlh_mcp_session_and_not_an_idle_one`.
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` at this record's commit (2026-11-22), both servers:** `test` (SQL Server 2025) passed in 349 s: 534 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests, 94 debug and 87 release live tests, none failed, exe 3,134,464 bytes of 3,145,728, in-box imports. `test2016` (SQL Server 2016 SP3) passed in 204 s, with the same counts. Neither run had a failure to retry.
- **Open:** a server with no Agent log, which AppVeyor's next certification run measures; the external review; decision 87's proposal (made 2026-11-27: decision 99).

**Internal review of `errorlog` (2026-11-22, `5690228`; not the external reviewer; each finding reproduced first).** Fixed in the follow-up commit (exe 3,127,296 bytes), with the errorlog live tests run on both servers:
- **Documented, a search's companions (P2):** they are every line of the same time and `ProcessInfo`, which at a startup includes unrelated lines (8 for `Server process ID is` on `test`), and in the Agent log, whose `ProcessInfo` is the level and whose times are whole seconds, every line of that level in that second (15 for `Waiting for SQL Server to recover` on `test`; on 2016 an unrelated warning for `idle CPU`), though each Agent message is one line. The tool description, schema-commands.md §6.9, DESIGN.md §9 and the README said "whole entries"; they now say what is kept. The behavior was kept then, and a unit row and a live check (`errorlog_reads_the_servers_and_the_agents_log`) held it. **Decided by decision 90 (2026-11-22):** decision 89 names "the same time and source" and the Agent log has no source, so the owner chose companions for the server's log only; an Agent-log search keeps the matching lines alone, built with the slice's follow-ups (below), the unit row and the live check changed to hold that.
- **Fixed, a server with no Agent log (P2):** AppVeyor's certification starts only the database engine, so `--agent` there is unmeasured; the live tests (`errorlog_reads_the_servers_and_the_agents_log`, `mcp_tools::the_errorlog_tool_reads_the_log`) required a non-empty Agent log. They now take rows, `-- no rows: agent_log` or exit 1 `this server lists no SQL Server Agent log archives`, and fail on anything else, exit 10 included, so the next certification run measures it (schema-commands.md §6.9).
- **Fixed, `--search`'s count (P3):** the limit counted UTF-16 units while the refusal and MCP's `maxLength: 500` count characters; it now counts characters (code points), and `@search` is `nvarchar(1000)`, room for 500 of two units each (500 U+1F600 searched on both servers, exit 0).
- **Fixed, docs and comments (P3 ×3):** DESIGN.md §9 said the timeout's advice always names `--since`; `since_sql`'s comment and schema-commands.md §6.9 said a date or search reaches `INSERT ... EXEC` (only `@a` and `@type` do); mcp-mode.md §8's 312 KiB is now labeled an estimate on a stale base (309.0 KiB at `e60a1de`), to be measured for decision 87's proposal.
- **Fixed, `--help` (P3):** `--continue-on-error` again says `run later batches after one fails (default: stop)`, on the `-c` line, within the 62 lines.
- **Fixed, a test's race (P3):** `errorlog_finds_a_whole_entry_and_reads_back_through_archives` took the line above `Login failed` as its `Error: 18456` line; another test's failed login in the same instant can interleave there, so it now looks for the Error line among the lines of the same time and source above it.
