**Slice 2 follow-ups (2026-11-22, on `phase6-slice2` after `85b50f8`; decision 90 and the slice's internal reviews' P3s; for the same external review).**
- **Decision 90:** `errorlog --agent --search` (MCP `agent: true` with `search`) keeps only the lines that match; the server's log keeps decision 89's companions (`schema::errorlog_sql`, schema-commands.md §6.9). Unit row `errorlogs_lookup_passes_only_typed_values`; the live check in `errorlog_reads_the_servers_and_the_agents_log` now asserts every row of an Agent-log search matches.
- **`activity`'s ages and depths (final review P3s):** `idle_ms` and `tran_ms` are clamped at 0 (`GETDATE()` is fixed at the statement's start and the DMVs are read after it; `tran_ms` -3 was seen on 2016). A session waiting behind a blocking cycle without being in it got depth 100 (its walk looped to the hop bound, and only a walk back to itself was marked); `up` now carries the sessions a walk has passed and stops at a step back to one, and `tops.cycle` marks that or 100 hops, so it gets NULL. schema-commands.md §6.8's SQL is the constants word for word (unit test `the_docs_show_activitys_sql_as_built`), and the live `activitys_depth_is_null_in_a_cycle_and_behind_one` runs that step on made-up sessions (the old step gave 100 for both waiters behind the cycle, the new NULL, on both servers). By hand under `chain.ps1`'s three-session chain (`activity3.sql`, local) on both servers: depths 0, 1, 2, `blocking` 2 and 1, every age non-negative, the tables dropped.
- **Wording (final review P3s):** the `errorlog` tool's description says VIEW ANY ERROR LOG is SQL Server 2022 and later, as `activity`'s qualifies its permission; `--archive`'s parse error states the bound for `-1` and a non-number too (`--archive x: expected 0 to 99`, as for 100); the MCP refusal prefixes are one rule, now written in mcp-mode.md §4: the schema's refusals start `invalid arguments for TOOL:`, and a command's own refusal of a value the schema allows is worded as the CLI words it, as `tables`' and `find`'s pattern and the name commands' already were, so `errorlog`'s `since` and `search` keep theirs (no code change there).
- **`--help`'s SERVER line (final review P3):** `activity` names the idle sessions in an open transaction it shows too, and `--since` shows its date form, `2026-11-20T08:00`, which needs no quotes in any shell (the space form is two arguments unquoted), server-local like the times; still 62 lines. The `--continue-on-error` text was already restored in `c13f934` (`run later batches after one fails (default: stop)`), so nothing else on the page lost information.
- **Tests that could pass while wrong, or fail for the wrong reason (group C and group A review P3s):** `the_activity_tool_shows_a_busy_sqlh_mcp_session_and_not_an_idle_one` gave its blocked call 8 s while the looks before `activity` may take 20 s and a connect; the call now has 28 s (under the server's `-t 30`), the time before `activity` returns is asserted under it with a message that says so, and the call is cancelled once seen, not waited out. Its decision-83 check now proves an idle pooled session existed: both pooled sessions are seen from another session (`sqlh mcp`, no request, no transaction) before and after the next `activity`, and exactly one keeps its `login_time`, since the reset on reuse sets the taken session's anew (measured on both servers; the first version of this check compared both sessions whole and failed on that, a test error, not a flake). `activity_shows_the_tests_own_chain_to_who_may_see_it` checks `Blocked`'s holder is still running after each look (`Blocked::still_held`), so outrunning `hold_a_write`'s minute fails as that; and for each grant (VIEW SERVER STATE, and on 2022 and later VIEW SERVER PERFORMANCE STATE alone) the head's `input_buffer` is the owner's and the waiter's wait and resource are an `LCK_M_` wait on a `RID: `. `run::tests::a_step_of_one_select_after_a_lookup_has_no_later_statements` checks the steps `activity` and `errorlog` (server log, and Agent log with a search and `--since`) build from their lookups' rows, not stand-ins; `info_names_the_logins_server_permissions` asserts 2016's VIEW ANY ERROR LOG grant fails with error 102, as schema-commands.md §6.1 says.
- **Docs (group A, D and final review P3s):** U14 (docs/notes/upstream-issues.md) and DESIGN.md §7's S1 paragraph claim only what was measured: an archive that doesn't exist (through `sp_readerrorlog` as sysadmin, and on 2025 with VIEW SERVER STATE; through `xp_readerrorlog` as sysadmin) and a date string `xp_readerrorlog` can't read; a `varchar` search inside `INSERT ... EXEC` was never measured through `xp_readerrorlog`, and through `sp_readerrorlog` can't raise 22004 (its `@p3` is `nvarchar`; run again by hand, 0 rows, exit 0, on both servers); U14's workaround says only `@a` and `@type` reach `INSERT ... EXEC`. DESIGN.md §7's list of what of the categories design is built now names `activity` and `errorlog`. No tracked doc cites the slice's local build spec any more (schema-commands.md §6.1, U14, this record). The README's MCP cost is re-measured: on the release exe at `7784761` with one profile listed, `tools/list`'s result is 15,785 bytes and `initialize`'s answer 867 (decision 48's Why, §6, has both), so "about 16 KB of JSON, roughly 4,000–5,000 tokens" replaces "3,000–4,000 tokens". MCP's size reads the same everywhere: 298.0 KiB at `00377cb` on mcp-mode.md §8's narrower base, beside the 309.0 KiB over Phase 4 of `e60a1de` (mcp-mode.md §1 and §10, DESIGN.md §10 and §11, AGENTS.md's Stack); the exe's budget is 3 MiB in DESIGN.md §11 and AGENTS.md, and "about 3 MB" in the README.
- **For the external review's brief (decision 52):** `src/engine.rs` changed in slice 2 by a message only (`00377cb`): `wait_for_connection` takes an `activity` flag (`run::Prepared::lookup_label()`), and an `activity` call whose wait for a free pooled connection times out ends `; sqlh activity on the command line opens a connection of its own`, decision 88's advice. Decision 88's "the engine is unchanged in slice 2" holds for its behavior: nothing is reserved, no permit and no connection.
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` at the follow-ups' last commit, `f8c53d5` (2026-11-23), both servers:** `test` passed in 283 s and `test2016` in 203 s, each with 535 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests, 95 debug and 88 release live tests, none failed or retried; exe 3,136,000 bytes (9,728 under the budget) from `3433098` on, in-box imports. The fix round's own `-Live` run had been cut off when its agent ended, so this run, by hand, is the one that counts.

**Decision 92 and the last review's P3s (2026-11-23, on `phase6-slice2` after `f8c53d5`; for the same external review).**
- **Decision 92:** `activity`'s `statement` and `input_buffer` are on one line: each run of spaces, tabs, CR and LF one space, the ends trimmed, before the cut (`schema::ACTIVITY_STEP`; schema-commands.md §6.8, DESIGN.md §9, the tool's description, mcp-mode.md §4, the README's entry). In the step's SQL, exact: three `REPLACE`s make CR, LF and tab spaces, so a tab is then a marker no text holds, and `' '` → `' ' + tab`, drop `tab + ' '`, drop tab joins each run, in `Latin1_General_100_BIN2`; `LTRIM(RTRIM())`, since `TRIM` is SQL Server 2017 and later. The statement is cut from the batch in an `OUTER APPLY` (`r.s`), so the expression is written once per column. By hand on both servers (`scratch/d91/collapse.sql`, local): CR LF, tabs and runs collapse, a literal's double space too, `''`, spaces only, NULL, and a `NCHAR(1)` and `NCHAR(65535)` kept. Unit: `activitys_step_is_one_select_of_its_columns` holds both columns' expression; `the_docs_show_activitys_sql_as_built` the docs' copy. Live: `activity_shows_the_tests_own_chain_to_who_may_see_it`'s blocked read is sent over lines, with a tab and runs of spaces, and its `statement` must equal the read on one line; against the old step it failed with the raw text (`SELECT COUNT(*) AS n\r\n\t   FROM  …`), and passes on both servers.
- **The decision-90 live check tells the rules apart (fix review P3):** `errorlog_reads_the_servers_and_the_agents_log` searched the first short Agent line, which might share its second and level with no other line, and then companions and decision 90 give the same rows. It now prefers a line that shares them with a line it isn't in, and says on stderr when the log has none. Both servers had one; with the Agent log's search switched back to companions by hand, the check failed on `test`, and it passes as built.
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` with these changes (2026-11-23), both servers:** `test2016` passed in 205 s (535 unit tests, 6 ignored; 132 and 125 process tests; 95 and 88 live tests). `test` failed one release live test, `the_servers_read_ai_generate_embeddings_as_the_guard_does`, at a connect this computer refused with WSAEADDRINUSE (os error 10048), unrelated to the change and recorded under "Carried over from finished work"; its 535 unit, 132 and 125 process and 95 debug live tests passed, and 87 of 88 release live tests, the changed `activity` and `errorlog` ones among them. Exe 3,136,512 bytes (+512; 9,216 under the budget), in-box imports.
- **mcp-mode.md's decision 20 line (fix review P3):** "309.0 KiB as built" read as today's size; it now says it was 309.0 KiB over the end of Phase 4, at `e60a1de`, and is 298.0 KiB at `00377cb` on §8's narrower base.

**Decision 93 (2026-11-23, on `phase6-slice2` after `d007e02`; for the same external review; the output contract).**
- **The lookup** ends with two more columns, `from` (its oldest line, `CONVERT(varchar(16), ..., 120)`, taken before the window and the search delete lines) and `kept` (the lines left); `schema::errorlog_then` takes `further`, the words for what reads further (`--since or --archive`, in `sqlh mcp` `since or archive`), present only without `--since`, and with `kept` 0 gives the step an `empty_note`: `archive N, from T` (or `which has no lines`), then `; ... reads further back` before the last archive the server lists, or `, the oldest the server keeps` at it. Without `--since`, a row without the two columns is exit 10, as any malformed lookup row; with it they are not read.
- **The output** (the contract, decision 52): `run::Step` has `empty_note`, carried with its sections (`run::Shown`) to a new `Output::set_empty_note`, called with `set_sections` for every batch; `Tally::take_no_rows` takes the note with the empty sections at the batch's end, so it never outlives its batch. Text and csv put it in parentheses on the `-- no rows:` line (`text::no_rows_line`); JSON, which keeps the section's `rows: []` and had no `-- no rows:` line, now notes an empty section in its `end_rows` and adds the line without `-- ` to the document's `notes`, an existing field: contract 2.1 unchanged. A section with rows shows no note.
- **Tests:** unit `schema::tests::an_empty_errorlog_says_how_far_back_it_read` (each form, none with lines kept or with a window, a short row exit 10; and through the command's plan the CLI's and sqlh mcp's words, and none with `since`), `errorlogs_lookup_passes_only_typed_values` (the row's columns; `@from` set before any `DELETE`), `output::text::tests::an_empty_section_carries_its_note` (and a following batch without it), `output::csv::tests::an_empty_sections_note_is_on_the_metadata_stream`, `output::json::tests::an_empty_sections_note_is_a_note`, `run::tests::a_step_of_one_select_after_a_lookup_has_no_later_statements` (an empty default read); through the fake server, `engine::tests::errorlog_shows_its_section_or_says_why_not` (MCP's wording, exact); live, `errorlog_reads_the_servers_and_the_agents_log` (a search for a text of its own process: archive 0, a server-local minute, and either form of what follows; with `--since 1m`, no note). By hand on both servers: archive 0 for the server's and the Agent log, text and JSON, archive 2 (further back) and archive 6 on `test` (the oldest), and `--since 1m` plain.
- **Internal review (2026-11-23, not the external reviewer; three lenses, each finding verified against the code):** five P3s, all fixed. The tool's description and mcp-mode.md's summary said every empty read gives the span; they now say without `since`. schema-commands.md gave a false reason for no note on a section with rows (its oldest line is the oldest kept and shown, not where the read began); it now says decision 93 chose the empty result only. Two live checks of an empty Agent log (`agent_log_rows`, `mcp_tools::the_errorlog_tool_reads_the_log`) still took the plain `-- no rows: agent_log`; they take the noted line. Nothing outside the live tests checked `since` turning the note off or the CLI's words: the plan-level unit case above. mcp-mode.md §4's table of advice wordings gains the note's row. A sixth, that this record's exit-10 sentence held only without `--since`, was refuted as wrong output but is true of the words, which now say so.
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` after the review's fixes (2026-11-23), both servers:** `test` passed in 424 s and `test2016` in 204 s, each with 539 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests, 95 debug and 88 release live tests, none failed or retried; in-box imports. **Exe 3,141,632 bytes: decision 93 cost 5,120, and 4,096 bytes of the 3,145,728 budget are left** (decision 87: the measured budget proposal is due before slice 3, and a change that would cross the budget stops for the owner).

**Decision 94 (2026-11-26, on `phase6-slice2` after `e552862`; for the same external review).**
- **The search:** `schema::errorlog_sql`'s `CHARINDEX` takes `[Text] COLLATE Latin1_General_100_CI_AI` in both logs' rules, an explicit collation that outranks `@search`'s and tempdb's (schema-commands.md §6.9, DESIGN.md §9, the `search` argument's description and mcp-mode.md §4's copy of it, the README's entry). Both test servers' collation, `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS`, ignores case but not accents (measured: `CHARINDEX(N'NÓLOGIN', N'sqlh_nologin_1')` is 0 there, 6 with the collation), so the new live check tells the two apart on both.
- **Tests:** unit `errorlogs_lookup_passes_only_typed_values` holds both rules with the collation; live, `errorlog_finds_a_whole_entry_and_reads_back_through_archives` finds its failed login again by `SQLH_NÓLOGIN_<pid>`, in capitals and with an accent; with the collation taken out by hand it fails there on `test`, and passes as built on both servers.
- **Internal review (2026-11-26, not the external reviewer; two lenses, each finding verified):** three findings held, all docs, all fixed: mcp-mode.md §4 quoted the old `search` description as exact (filed as P2 and P3, the same finding), and the slice's decision lists (this section's heading, AGENTS.md's Status) left out 94. One was refuted: the Agent-log live check lowercases only, while the search now ignores accents too, but its probe is a whole line of the log itself, so no accent-only match can arise.
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` (2026-11-26), both servers:** the first run failed the new check on both, and tested the wrong binaries: `src/schema.rs`, put back by `Copy-Item` after the by-hand revert above, kept the backup's older time, so cargo did not rebuild and both exes still held the old search (its size unchanged; the check failed as it does against the old SQL). With the file touched and the release exe seen to hold both collated searches, the second run passed: `test` in 357 s, `test2016` in 200 s, each with 539 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests, 95 debug and 88 release live tests, none failed or retried; in-box imports. Exe 3,141,632 bytes, unchanged (the SQL's 70 bytes fit the section's padding; 4,096 under the budget).

**Decision 95 (2026-11-26, on `phase6-slice2` after `a069e19`; for the same external review: connect behavior).**
- **The connect:** `Session::connect_with` runs the driver's `create_client` through `once_more_if_port_held`, which makes a second attempt only when the first fails with WSAEADDRINUSE (`port_held`): `TdsError::Io` of raw OS error 10048, or under `-M` the driver's `ConnectionError` text `All parallel connection attempts failed ... Last error: ...`, which keeps only the last failure's text, ending `(os error 10048)` (mssql-tds 0.4.0 `parallel_connect.rs`). It bounds the second with `tokio::time::timeout_at` at the login's deadline (`login_deadline`: `-l` + 1 s from the first attempt's start; none with `-l 0`, and none rather than a panic for a limit past `Instant`'s range), past which it is the login timeout, exit 4. The driver's context is built anew for each attempt (`client_context`), not cloned, so sqlh keeps no extra copy of a password. `connect_error`'s new arm, ahead of the network errors', gives a second 10048 its hint. The driver's own `connect_retry_count` stays 0 (decision 41). `--help`'s `-l` says `one attempt (two on os error 10048)`.
- **Tests:** unit `db::tests::a_connect_refused_a_held_port_is_tried_once_more` (10048 then success: two attempts, at once; 10048 twice: the error; another IO error, 10061: one attempt; 10048 then 10061: that error; `-M`'s form of 10048 retried and of 10061 not, and other text holding the code not taken for it; a second attempt still running at the deadline: a timeout that `connect_error` words as the login timeout; each run bounded at 5 s, so a lost bound fails rather than hangs), `the_login_deadline_is_l_and_one_second` (none for 0, 2 s for 1, 16 s for 15, no panic for `u32::MAX`) and `a_held_port_is_this_computers_and_not_the_servers_name` (both forms' hint in the CLI's and sqlh mcp's words, no `server name`; 10061 keeps `check the server name`). Not end to end: no test can make Windows choose a held port, so every connect test covers the wrapper's first attempt only.
- **Internal review (2026-11-26, not the external reviewer; three lenses: correctness against the driver's source, tests and docs, the engine; each finding verified):** seven held, all fixed. P2 (two lenses): under `-M` the driver reports the failure as `ConnectionError` text, so neither the retry nor the hint applied there, while the docs said they did; `port_held` now reads that text. P3: "nothing reached the server" was false where an earlier hop did (SQL Browser's lookup, a routing listener's login, which the second attempt makes again), now qualified in the code, DESIGN.md and decision 95; the `-l` row was cited as DESIGN.md §3, but is §4.2 (also in the carried-over finding's text); the hint said this computer had run out of ports, where the cause is a collision with a held port, now `this computer twice chose a local port that a closed connection to that server still held (...)`, and the README's entry likewise; `--help`, DESIGN.md §11's "Failing fast" and connect-failures.md still said one attempt with no exception; the tests didn't pin the deadline's value or "at once", and a lost bound would hang them (the `login_deadline` table test and the bound above); DESIGN.md §11's exe size was stale. One was refuted: mcp-mode.md §5 needs no note of its own, since its statement that the engine's retry is the only reconnect stays true.
- **Size:** exe 3,144,192 bytes, +2,560, the same before and after the review's fixes; **1,536 under the 3 MiB budget** (decision 87).
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` after the review's fixes (2026-11-26), both servers:** `test` passed in 371 s and `test2016` in 218 s, each with 542 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests, 95 debug and 88 release live tests, none failed or retried; in-box imports. A run before the fixes passed on `test`; its `test2016` half stopped at `cargo fmt --check` on a file edited while it ran, so it counts for nothing.

**Decision 96 (2026-11-26, `8178ca9` and `b90c18e`; for the same external review).** No code after `7f22caf`: `8178ca9` changes only the version in `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` and documents, and `b90c18e` documents (`git diff --stat 7f22caf..b90c18e`), so the `-Live` runs above tested this code but for the version string. `scripts/check.ps1` (no `-Live`) on `b90c18e`'s code, version 0.2.0, passed in 99 s: 542 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests; exe 3,144,192 bytes; in-box imports (log `artifacts/slice2/check-d96.log`, local). Decision 50 now notes decision 96's amendment, and decision 96's launch-post date reads 2026-12-01, as decision 91 and Going public have it.

This is design work with the owner before any code:
1. List concrete DBA investigation workflows: blocking chains, wait stats, Query Store, plan cache and showplans, index usage, statistics, `sp_who2`, and common community procedures.
2. List their risks.
3. Derive categories (data read, metadata read, server-state read, session scratch, maintenance, data write, DDL, security, external or remote).
4. Map each category to a policy (allow, deny, require `--allow-write` or the `execute` tool).

Classifier v2 implements the result. Showplan support (`SET SHOWPLAN_XML`, summarized) probably belongs here as a first-class DBA read. (L, mostly design)

**Design (2026-10-23): [command-categories.md](command-categories.md).** Steps 1–4 are answered there as a design for owner decisions: the workflows and risks, a two-layer model (six kinds of change a person can grant, named in DBA words: `data`, `maintenance`, `tuning`, `kill`, `remote`, `unvetted`; and reason labels used only in refusals), a policy map per mode, Part 1 (read-only runs and `sqlh mcp`, buildable first) and Part 2 (changes: the deny tier, grants per kind, the `execute` tool), and decisions D1–D15 in the order to take them, all taken on 2026-10-23 and recorded in §6 as decisions 22–36. **Decided:** Part 1 (D2–D9) in full as recommended; Part 2 with the kinds (decisions 22, 32), the deny tier (31), `kill` (33), literal dynamic SQL and per-database runs (34) and trusted entries (35), but no `execute` tool in Phase 6 (36, against the recommendation). Open before building: the live checks in the design's §10, the MSDTC question for linked-server reads (25), and the SNAPSHOT test (24), which needs the owner's approval of a database-level change. It came from six workflow surveys probed against the guard, a draft, a safety critique and a DBA-practicality critique, and a revision checked for coverage and facts; every guard verdict in it was re-run on `f905385` (its Appendix A), and its Appendix B answers each critique issue.

**Interim step, not blocked on the taxonomy: extend the EXEC allowlist** (owner, 2026-10-10: "we will need to allow `xp_readerrorlog` and `xp_instance_regread`, and maybe more if the login permissions allow"). VISION.md keeps selecting individual permitted commands separate from designing the categories, so this can ship first. Today `xp_*` is blocked wholesale (DESIGN.md §7), which rules out reading the SQL Server error log, a basic investigation. The earlier sql-query-runner skill already allows these two, so they come from real use. (S)
- **Principle:** the allowlist says which commands are read-only. Whether a login may run one is the server's call, and database permissions stay the enforcement boundary (VISION.md). So a command belongs on the list because it only reads, not because this login happens to have the rights, and a login without the permission just gets the server's error.
- **First additions:** `xp_readerrorlog` and its wrapper `sp_readerrorlog`, and `xp_instance_regread`. Name them exactly: a prefix match on `xp_*` would also admit `xp_cmdshell` and `xp_instance_regwrite`, which stay blocked. `xp_instance_regread` reads registry values, which can disclose information; record that in the category notes.
- **Already allowed:** the skill's other diagnostics, `sp_who`, `sp_who2` and `DBCC TRACESTATUS`, pass sqlh's guard today (checked against the test server, 2026-10-12), so these two `xp_` procedures are all that sql-query-runner allows and sqlh refuses. The refusal then said the procedure "would write", which is wrong for an unknown procedure; decision 23's wording replaced it in slice 1 (above).
- **Candidates to evaluate in the same pass:** `xp_fixeddrives`, `xp_msver`, and `sp_configure` in its listing form only (with arguments it changes settings, so it needs the argument check `sp_spaceused` gets).
- **To verify on SQL Server 2025 Standard:** which permission each one needs for a non-sysadmin login (newer versions have finer-grained server permissions for the error log), so the blocked/allowed advice and the docs can say what to grant.
- **Acceptance:** table-driven guard tests (allowed bare and qualified as `sys.`/`master.`; `xp_instance_regwrite` and `xp_cmdshell` still blocked; stacked statements still refused); a live read of the error log; DESIGN.md §7 updated in the same change.
- **Built (2026-10-23).** `PROCS` gains `xp_readerrorlog`, `sp_readerrorlog`, `sp_enumerrorlogs` (it lists the log archives that `sp_readerrorlog`'s first argument numbers; added in the same pass, as a read-only companion), `xp_instance_regread`, `xp_fixeddrives`, `xp_msver` and `sp_configure`. `sp_configure` joins `sp_spaceused` in `ARG_CHECKS`, a table of the allowlisted procedures that write when given one argument: its value (`@configvalue`, named or second) is refused, with `sys.configurations` as the alternative, since `sp_configure` hides the advanced options until `show advanced options`, itself a setting, is on (seen live: `sp_configure 'max degree of parallelism'` failed with error 15123 on the test server). Left out: `xp_regread` (any key, unmapped), `xp_enumerrorlogs` (`sp_enumerrorlogs` covers it), and the writers. Tests: `guard::tests::diagnostic_extended_procedures_are_allowed_by_exact_name` (bare, `sys.`, `master.sys.`, `master.dbo.`, `master..`, bracketed and implicit; `INSERT #t EXEC`; the writing neighbours and prefix-sharing names blocked; other qualifiers blocked; stacked statements refused at their own line) and `sp_configure_with_a_value_is_blocked`; live, `the_error_log_and_server_reads_run_read_only_and_the_server_decides_who_may` reads the error log as the test profile and measures SQL Server 2025's permissions with a `Reader` login and each server permission alone, the table in DESIGN.md §7, on the test server, which is Standard Developer (plan-history.md D9: confirming it on Standard stays on the release checklist): `xp_readerrorlog` needs sysadmin or an explicit `EXECUTE` (not `VIEW SERVER STATE` or `VIEW ANY ERROR LOG`), the `sp_` log procedures need `VIEW SERVER STATE`, `VIEW ANY ERROR LOG` or securityadmin (the last from their source, read with `OBJECT_DEFINITION`, which checks this and only reads), `xp_fixeddrives` returns no rows without sysadmin, and `xp_instance_regread` runs with no server permission and, in a one-off probe, read even the service account's key (a disclosure for the category notes). None of the additions takes an app lock, sets session context or waits (mcp-mode.md §5; checked by reading). **Review (2026-10-23, an internal three-lens review with a verifier per finding; not the external reviewer):** two ways past the argument check, both older than this change in `sp_spaceused`'s, were fixed with tests: a procedure number (`EXEC sp_configure;1 'show advanced options', 1`, which the server takes, checked live) ended the scan at its `;`, and a bare value spelled like an unreserved statement keyword (`EXEC sp_configure GET, 1`, `MOVE`, `THROW`) ended it at the word (`UNRESERVED_STARTS`); the docs' overclaims (unmeasured cells, the edition, a source claim for `sp_configure`) were corrected, the unmeasured cells now measured. The exe grew 3,072 bytes (2,808,320). The "would write" wording for an unknown procedure, left open then, went with decision 23 in slice 1 (above).

### 0.1.0 external review (November 7, 2026)

Reviewed `7feae17..899591a` on main, HEAD `899591a` and a clean starting tree verified; origin/main is `6df4269`. Git counts 59 commits in the range (the brief says 58). **Original verdict: R51-R54 [P2] required changes; R55 [P3] was a documentation correction. R51-R55 are now closed by the fix verification below.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-0.1.0-slice1.md. No reviewer commit or push. The review covers slice 1, the guard rewrite, decisions 46-69 and the other work since R50; it does not reopen the explicitly accepted collation/string/opaque-module limits as new findings.

- **R51 [P2], config.rs:349-447: lock the source of a profile migration.** Only the new .sqlh/profiles.toml.lock is taken; an old-path editor or a current SQLH_CONFIG editor holds a different lock. The release exe migrated and deleted a synthetic AppData source while its no-sharing edit-lock handle was held, completing in 31 ms. A simulated editor then published removal of its profile; the next migration kept that removed profile from the new file, losing the completed removal. This is concurrent-operation loss, distinct from the documented conflict policy for preexisting copies. Requested: source and destination synchronization in consistent order, rechecking under the locks, for every consumed/uncovered source and the earlier rename path. Acceptance: a held source lock causes bounded waiting/refusal without copying/deleting it; completed additions, policy edits and removals survive with destination missing or present; concurrent first loads and R20 identity binding remain correct. Evidence: artifacts/review-010-migration.json and scratch/review-010-migration.py; synthetic Windows-auth profiles only, no credentials.
  - **Fixed (2026-11-07).** `bring_across` takes each earlier file's own lock after the new file's, in one order (`%APPDATA%\sqlh`, then `%APPDATA%\sqlharness` for the move from before the rename), checks again under them, and reads and removes the file only while it holds them; `tidy` removes the lock files and empty folders after letting go, which can't take a lock another process holds. `absorb` and `migrate_from_sqlharness` no longer remove the lock or the folder. `load_waiting` is `load_at` with a wait of its own, for the tests. Tests: `a_move_leaves_an_earlier_file_alone_while_its_editor_holds_it` (an addition, a policy change and a removal, the new file absent and present: a held lock is the usual error within the wait, both files untouched, and the editor's change is what arrives), `a_move_from_before_the_rename_waits_for_that_files_editor` (the same for `%APPDATA%\sqlharness`, no password moved while held), `a_move_waits_for_the_editor_of_an_earlier_file_and_takes_its_edit` (within the wait, the move waits and takes the removal); `two_first_loads_at_once_move_once` and R21's tests pass unchanged, and R20's binding is untouched (passwords move only in `migrate_from_sqlharness`, as before). The reviewer's scenario with the release exe, a scratch `USERPROFILE` and `APPDATA` and an exclusive handle on the source's `.lock`: `profiles list` waited 10.0 s and gave the lock error (exit 3) with the old file in place and no new one; after the editor's removal and release, the next run brought the empty file across, so the removal stands, and the old folder was gone. Not covered, stated in DESIGN.md §8: under a packaged app a lock file may be the package's private copy, so it may not exclude an editor outside the package (unmeasured).
- **R52 [P2], db/mod.rs:1475-1502: keep the TCP endpoint's SPN identity.** With a slow lookup, host_and_spn(db01\SALES) installs MSSQLSvc/db01:SALES. The actual mssql-tds 0.3.0 Windows path supplies a transport host/numeric port and calls make_spn_canonicalized(server, None, port); an explicit context SPN overrides it unchanged. Exact extracted sqlh/driver functions independently produced the instance-name override versus MSSQLSvc/db01:1544 for a synthetic endpoint at port 1544; an explicit-port control matched. Microsoft's SPN documentation likewise uses port-based SPNs for TCP named instances. The timeout can therefore alter authentication identity, causing Kerberos failure/NTLM fallback; no actual domain login was attempted. Requested: construct the override from the actual TCP endpoint, not an instance name before resolution, and correct the test/spec equivalence claim. Acceptance: slow lookup, named/default instance and explicit-port controls preserve the automatic TCP SPN; check routed endpoint identity as part of that correction, retaining bounded wait/cancel. Evidence: artifacts/review-010-spn.log and scratch/make-review-010-spn.py. Driver source read: handler/handler_factory.rs, security/windows/sspi_context.rs:98-102 and ClientContext::integrated_auth_config. Reference: [TCP SPNs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/database-engine/configure-windows/register-a-service-principal-name-for-kerberos-connections?view=sql-server-ver17).
  - **Fixed (2026-11-07).** `host_and_spn` gives no SPN for a named instance without a port, so the driver builds its own from the port SQL Browser gives, as its TCP path does; with a port, or none and no instance, the fallback is `MSSQLSvc/<host>:<port>` as before, the driver's own when its lookup fails. `Session::connect` makes no lookup of its own under `-K ReadOnly`, where a routing redirect is expected and a fixed SPN would carry over to the routed host (the driver keeps its `ClientContext` across a redirect); a redirect the server makes unasked after a slow lookup still keeps the first host's SPN. Windows authentication uses Negotiate, so the resulting connection may use Kerberos, use NTLM when Windows policy permits, or fail; the retained SPN does not establish a fail-closed routing guarantee. Routed Windows authentication remains unverified (R56, qualified during fix verification). Both cases keep the driver's unbounded lookup (upstream-issues.md U9, which now asks for a hook). The test that held `MSSQLSvc/db01:SALES` now holds `None` for three named-instance forms; `a_lookup_in_time_leaves_the_spn_to_the_driver` uses `db01,1500`. DESIGN.md §4.2 (`-E`) and notes/connect-latency.md are corrected. Not done: a domain login, or a routed Windows-auth login, which no test server offers.
- **R53 [P2], db/mod.rs:1443-1468: bound outstanding resolver workers.** outlasts spawns one OS thread per lookup and drops its receiver after timeout/cancel; the thread and its resource charge are not bounded by the async call's lifetime or MCP's pool/admission limits. An exact-helper probe returned from 32 sequential expired waits with 32 synthetic workers still active, then released every job and confirmed completion. This is a resource-bound demonstration, not a real DNS delay measurement. Requested: cap/coalesce actual resolver work, holding its reservation until the worker ends even when its caller leaves; queued waits must stay cancellable and shutdown bounded. Acceptance: a timeout/cancel burst stays within the chosen worker cap, remains responsive when full and returns slots only on completion. Evidence: artifacts/review-010-worker.json and scratch/make-review-010-worker.py. No DNS/SSPI/server used.
  - **Fixed (2026-11-07).** `db::outlasts` takes one of `LOOKUP_THREADS` (4) slots of a static `tokio::sync::Semaphore` before it starts a lookup, and the thread holds it until the lookup itself returns, whatever happened to its caller. The wait for a slot comes out of the same 500 ms deadline, so when all are busy the connect goes on with the fallback SPN at the budget, and dropping the wait (a cancel) ends it at once. Threads stay detached, so process exit waits for none. `lookups_past_their_budget_are_capped_and_hold_their_slot_until_they_end` (8 held lookups against a cap of 2: never more than 2 running, a full wait ending with its budget and on being dropped, the slots back only once the workers end); the existing fast, slow, pipe and cancel tests pass unchanged.
- **R54 [P2], guard.rs:2570-2588: do not case-fold master when granting system-procedure identity.** The new schema/procedure case checks leave the database qualifier case-insensitive. Exact-source and release CLI probes admit EXEC MASTER.dbo.xp_readerrorlog; the source probe also admits its quoted form. Other.dbo and master.DBO controls refuse. Database identifiers follow the instance collation, so on a case-sensitive instance a distinct MASTER database's dbo.xp_readerrorlog must not be trusted as master. The guard admission is independently reproduced; execution of a shadow procedure on a case-sensitive instance is an inference from Microsoft's identifier/system-procedure rules, not a live reproduction on the owner's case-insensitive servers. Requested: canonical master spelling before granting the allowlist identity, or independently established database identity; amend decision 62's any-case exception if needed. Acceptance: mixed/uppercase and quoted variants fail closed with usable advice, canonical forms remain supported, and a case-sensitive-instance fixture demonstrates the resolution distinction when available. Evidence: artifacts/review-010-guard-corpus.json and artifacts/review-010-cli-controls.json. References: [identifier collation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/databases/database-identifiers?view=sql-server-ver17), [system extended-procedure resolution](https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/sqlserver/name-resolution-default-schema-implicit-schema-part-i/383052/).
  - **Fixed (2026-11-07; decision 62 amended by the owner, §6).** `local_system_qualifier` gives `Cased` for `master` with an ASCII capital, bare or quoted, as for `sys` and `dbo`, so `EXEC MASTER.dbo.xp_readerrorlog` and `EXEC [MASTER].[dbo].[xp_readerrorlog]` are refused with `Write it as xp_readerrorlog`. `every_list_matches_in_any_case` holds each listed name lowercase under `master.sys.`, `master..` and `[master].[dbo].`, and refused under `MASTER.dbo.`, `[MASTER].[dbo].`, `Master.sys.` and `mAsTeR..`; `a_listed_procedure_in_another_case_never_connects` has the reviewer's two forms. No case-sensitive instance exists to show the resolution itself; DESIGN.md §7 states it as inferred.
- **R55 [P3], DESIGN.md:653: remove the stale numbered-RAISERROR exception.** The transaction-backstop paragraph still says a numbered event-logged RAISERROR is not refused, contradicting decision 59, the earlier specification and the implemented number/variable refusal. Requested/acceptance: make those sections agree while retaining the real limits concerning opaque modules and server-generated errors. Documentation only; self-verify and fold into the next brief under decision 52, without a standalone external round.
  - **Fixed (2026-11-07, docs only; for the next brief).** The paragraph now says `WITH LOG` and a `RAISERROR` that doesn't quote its message are refused, and that a module's body and an error the server logs by itself are not seen.

**Other judgments:** accept the measured reserved/unreserved comparison split and conservative name matching within their documented scope, and found no further concrete temp-alias/CTE-place bypass in the isolated controls. The procedure-argument rule refuses non-ASCII names, so parameter variation does not grant the checked write argument; DBCC names choose built-in commands. Direct quoted-message RAISERROR and WITH LOG checks are reasonable; FORMATMESSAGE builds text rather than raising/logging it, and unlisted event procedures remain refused. This cannot prevent arbitrary server faults or side effects hidden behind modules/describe strings. No outbound call or server-fault shape was executed; the path-taking readers in deferred D4b remain unfinished and no universal outbound-isolation claim is accepted. Decisions 54, 58, 60 and 63 and the enumerated false refusals remain accepted first-release limits. The CLI-first/offline direction, 2016 SP3 floor and thin slices fit the vision; hosted MCP/Entra remains planned. Application names are courtesy labels, not authorization evidence.

**Cost:** accept the reported decision-61 increment of 12.3% on temp-dense text. A separate independent seven-sample alternating benchmark of the exact old/current guard sources on 9.4 MB gave medians of 84.242/105.831 ms for ordinary SELECT text and 86.623/106.440 ms for temp lists. These compare the whole range and include helper startup/input transfer, not the isolated decision-61 increment or the developer's end-to-end CLI experiment. Evidence: artifacts/review-010-benchmark.json and scratch/review-010-benchmark.py. No dependency change or new per-row work.

**Independent checks:** the first standard non-live check passed without retry in **95 s**: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, **489 unit, 124 debug process and 118 release process tests**, locked release build, size and imports. Five unit, 86 debug live and 79 release live tests stayed ignored. Exe **2,996,736 bytes**, 148,992 below 3 MiB, 85,504 larger than the last reviewed exe; ten approved in-box imports. Log: artifacts/review-010-check.log. Fifty-nine exact-source guard cases and eight release CLI controls passed, distinguishing refusal exit 5 from admission followed by closed-loopback connect failure exit 2, using empty synthetic config. They do not validate admitted SQL on a server. The isolated migration, worker and SPN probes above supply their qualified evidence; earlier artifacts are preserved.

**Not independently rerun:** SQL Server live suites or parser sweeps, Windows-auth/real Kerberos logins, the case-sensitive-instance execution scenario, packaged MCP launch, hosted CI, signed publication or clean-workstation checks. The developer's 86 debug/79 release live passes on each server and the recorded live measurements remain supplied evidence. Automatic approval review rejected a proposed read-only SELECT 1 using the owner's live test profile before execution, citing the standing source/isolated-probe scope. No alternate live access was attempted. Only review/status Markdown and ignored synthetic probes/artifacts changed; no source/tests, Cargo, owner credentials/profiles/database, client or hosted configuration, commit or push changed.

### R51-R55 fix verification (November 7, 2026)

Reviewed `899591a..a86e0b8`, main, `sequeltom/sqlh`; HEAD and a clean starting tree verified. **R51-R55 closed; increment accepted with the first-release scope qualifications below. R56 [P3], documentation-only, corrected in the working tree during verification; no open new runtime finding.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-r51-r55-verification.md. No reviewer commit or push.

- **R51 acceptance met on shared physical source paths:** source locks are taken after the destination's, in one order, and files are rechecked before moving. tidy runs after releasing the source lock. Added addition/policy/removal, destination-missing/present, earlier-rename, wait-within-budget and existing concurrency tests passed. Independent release-exe replays in synthetic USERPROFILE/APPDATA trees: an exclusive source lock produced exit 3 after 10.062 s with no copy/removal, then the editor's removal survived; a destination-present removal waited and preserved the other profile; an earlier-rename addition waited and arrived intact. Evidence: artifacts/review-r51-r55-migration.json and scratch/review-r51-r55-migration.py. No credentials used. Accepted for 0.1.0: the documented, unmeasured packaged-app lock-redirection gap, given no released old-path editor. This does not establish serialization across different physical package copies or active explicit old-path editors, nor change destination-wins conflict policy.
- **R52 acceptance met with a narrower workaround:** exact-source probes show no override for three named-instance-without-port forms and numeric-port matches for both explicit-port spellings, a default instance and explicit IP/port. Source review confirms -K ReadOnly skips the prelookup/override. Accept preserving the driver's own endpoint identity instead of duplicating SQL Browser/routing resolution before 0.1.0. Those paths retain U9's blocking lookup: they are not covered by a universal 500 ms wait or prompt-cancel guarantee. Unasked redirects remain best-effort authentication under existing Negotiate/TLS policy, not proof of fail-closed behavior; R56 corrects that wording. Actual domain, named-instance resolution and routed Windows-auth logins were not independently run. Evidence: artifacts/review-r51-r55-spn.log and scratch/make-review-r51-r55-spn.py.
- **R53 acceptance met:** the actual worker owns the semaphore permit through completion, independent of its async caller. Slot acquisition and result waiting share a deadline; canceled queued waits drop their acquisition. An exact-helper replay of 32 expired waits peaked at four held jobs, retained all permits while work remained, canceled a queued wait, restored all four slots only after actual work ended and admitted a fast job. All held jobs were released. Evidence: artifacts/review-r51-r55-worker.json and scratch/make-review-r51-r55-worker.py. The cap of four and using the raw-host fallback when full are acceptable first-release policies, not a measured optimum or a guarantee of identical Kerberos selection under saturation. The driver's synchronous lookups are outside this worker cap.
- **R54 acceptance met:** the canonical database spelling joins the procedure/schema checks, with the owner's amendment to decision 62. Independent exact-source and release-CLI controls now refuse uppercase/quoted MASTER; canonical forms still admit only a closed-loopback connect attempt. The full list's cased/canonical unit matrix passed. This establishes the conservative classifier correction without claiming a live case-sensitive-instance execution test.
- **R55 acceptance met:** DESIGN §7's backstop now agrees with decision 59 and preserves the real opaque-module/server-generated-error limits.

**R56 [P3], corrected here:** the R52 fix record inferred a general fail-closed outcome from Kerberos ticket encryption, but the pinned driver defaults to Negotiate. Protocol selection may use NTLM when policy permits; a retained SPN alone does not establish the asserted outcome. The PLAN record now says Kerberos, NTLM or failure according to policy, with routed Windows authentication unverified. DESIGN §4.2's four-lookup statement now explicitly limits sqlh worker-thread lookups, not the separate synchronous driver path. These documentation qualifications change no authentication or worker behavior, and require no standalone fix-review round under decision 52. Source: SecurityPackage::default and ClientContext::integrated_auth_config; [Microsoft's Windows authentication architecture](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/security/windows-authentication/security-support-provider-interface-architecture). No routed NTLM exchange or actual downgrade was observed.

**Account rename/workflow accepted:** `github.repository_owner_id` is a documented string context value. Read-only GitHub API queries independently confirmed the owner-ID gate and immutable OIDC subject configuration; no OIDC token was minted. The main/tag gate and build/sign/verify/publish permission separation are unchanged; CI has no owner-name gate, and publish derives `GH_REPO` from `github.repository`. The Azure federated-subject update and a subsequent release dispatch remain the owner's §7 gate. Azure's current credential was not independently inspected, and no workflow/configuration/signing/publication action was performed. References: [GitHub context contract](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/contexts); local review artifacts.

**Independent checks:** first scripts/check.ps1 run passed without retry in **94 s**: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, **493 unit, 124 debug process and 118 release process tests**, locked release build, size and imports. Five unit, 86 debug live and 79 release live tests stayed ignored. Exe **2,999,808 bytes**, 145,920 below 3 MiB, 3,072 over 899591a; ten approved in-box imports, no dependency/lockfile change. Log: artifacts/review-r51-r55-check.log. Fifty-nine exact-source guard cases and nine release CLI controls passed; evidence in artifacts/review-r51-r55-guard-corpus.json and -cli-controls.json. Earlier artifacts preserved. The documentation-only R56 edits were checked with git diff --check; production/test implementation and Cargo were not edited during this verification.

**Not independently rerun:** live suites, real domain/Kerberos/named-instance/routed authentication, a case-sensitive instance, packaged locking/launch, hosted CI or signed release. Developer-reported 86 debug/79 release live passes on each server remain supplied evidence. The standing source/isolated-probe scope was respected; no live-profile attempt was made. Review/status/authentication wording and ignored synthetic artifacts only; no credentials, owner profiles/database, client/hosted configuration, commit or push changed. Other first-release gates stay open.

### 0.1.0 release checks (November 8, 2026)

§7's items that this machine and the test servers can settle, run against the release exe at the commits named. Each result is ticked in §7 with a pointer here.

- **The test servers updated (owner, 2026-11-07, reported 2026-11-08).** SQL Server 2025 to CU10, 17.0.5035.5 (KB5125471), Standard Developer, restarted 20:56 server time; SQL Server 2016 to SP3's last GDR, 13.0.6500.1 (KB5102340), Developer, restarted 20:35. Earlier measurements in this plan and DESIGN.md name the builds they ran on (2025 RTM, 17.0.1000.7). **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passed on both at `eeb7370`, after the updates:** 494 unit, 126 debug and 120 release process, 86 debug and 79 release live tests on each, among them the live sweeps that compare the guard's measured tables with what each server parses (`the_servers_ignore_what_the_guard_ignores`, the character and spelling sweeps), so the updates changed nothing the guard goes by. The two server-fault shapes (decision 57's `VECTOR_DISTANCE ... USE MODEL`, and deep `/*` nesting given to `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set`) are to be retried on the new builds when the work is otherwise idle (owner, 2026-11-08: "you may test those crashes, when we are otherwise idle. it will be good to know if the patching closed them").

- **Cancellation (at `c83a7fe`, release exe, 2025 server).** By test, each run in `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` on both servers: Ctrl+Break during connect (`ctrl_break_cancels_a_connect`, with and without `-M`) and during execution (`ctrl_break_cancels_a_running_query`, `..._a_run_waiting_for_its_reader`, `..._the_final_delivery_...`, and `sqlh mcp`'s), a killed run (`a_killed_run_leaves_no_work_on_the_server`), `-t` (`timed_run` and the timing tests). By hand, since no test makes sqlh a console of its own: a driver (`scratch`, not kept) started sqlh hidden in a new console, attached to it and sent Ctrl+C, or posted `WM_CLOSE` to its console window, or killed it.
  - Ctrl+C during connect (a listener that never answers): exit 6 in 0.32 s, `-- error: cancelled by Ctrl+C: cancelled while connecting`, the `-o` file published. The console closing: exit 6 at once, `cancelled by the console closing: cancelled while connecting`. A kill: gone, no `-o` file and no `.partial`.
  - During a 30 s `WAITFOR` (`--allow-write`, the test profile): Ctrl+C and the console closing both exit 6 at once, `the statement was cancelled; the connection stayed usable`, `-o` published; a kill leaves no `-o` file and no `.partial` (DESIGN.md §4.2's delete-on-close mark). After each, `sys.dm_exec_requests` had no `WAITFOR` left.
  - `-l 2` against a listener that never answers: exit 4 at 3.05 s (each attempt `-l`, the login one second more, DESIGN.md §4.2). `-t 2` against the `WAITFOR`: exit 4 at 2.36 s, `query timeout after 2 s in batch 1`, nothing left on the server. `-t` bounds a batch from when it is sent, so a connect is `-l`'s.
  - Not tried: logoff and shutdown (they would end the session; they take the console-close path with the same 1 s grace, `signals.rs`), and the two outside a console's own process group, which no harness does.
- **The response budget (at `c83a7fe`, release exe, 2025 server).** A sweep (`scratch`, not kept) of six read-only workloads (200,000 wide rows with `--max-rows 0`; one 3,000,000-character value with `--max-cell 0`; 300 result sets; 2,000 messages of 1,000 characters; 300 failing batches with `--continue-on-error`; rows, messages and `RAISERROR` mixed), each in text, json and csv at `--max-output` 4096, 20000 and the default 1,000,000: 54 runs, stdout and stderr together within the cap in all 54 (the closest, 999,346 of 1,000,000), and each ended as it must (text: the status line last on stdout; csv: last on stderr; json: one document that parses, with its summary). Exits were 0, or 1 for the failing batches. As DESIGN.md §4.4 has it, a piece that doesn't fit what is left stops the body: the 3 MB value shows `0 of 1 rows shown (--max-output)`, and at 4096 the mixed workload stopped at its first 3,000-character message and named what it didn't show.
- **cmd.exe and Windows PowerShell 5.1.** The tests from plan-history.md 2.13 are plain process tests, run against the debug and release exes in every `scripts/check.ps1`: `the_help_examples_run_as_written_in_every_shell`, `path_forms_work_for_input_output_and_config`, `a_path_from_copy_as_path_works_after_i_and_o_in_every_shell`, `the_documented_q_form_reaches_sqlh_intact_in_every_shell`, `sqlh_leaves_the_console_code_page_alone`. Piped non-ASCII, live with the release exe: cmd.exe after `chcp 65001`, intact; 5.1 with its default `$OutputEncoding`, `Caf? ? ??`, as documented.
  - **Found and fixed: 5.1 as `--help` says to set it up was refused when the command's output is captured.** `$OutputEncoding = [Text.Encoding]::UTF8; $sql | sqlh ... | Out-File` (and `$r = $sql | sqlh ...`) sends `EF BB BF EF BB BF` and the text; a BOM-less `$OutputEncoding` sends one `EF BB BF` there. `decode` took the first as the encoding's and left the second, which the guard refused as `<U+FEFF>SELECT starts the batch`, exit 5. Without the capture 5.1 sends one, which is what notes/windows-shells.md had measured. Not new: before `03eb5ec` the second mark was refused all the same, only unseen. `script::decode` now drops every BOM at the very start of a file or stdin, in each encoding; one later (a batch after `GO` that starts with one) is still refused and shown. `a_run_of_boms_at_the_start_is_dropped_and_one_later_kept`; `piped_sql_with_a_run_of_boms_reaches_the_server`, which also runs the 5.1 capture itself. DESIGN.md §4.1 and §7, notes/windows-shells.md, the README's entry. The input's decoding goes in the next brief (decision 52).
- **plan-history.md §1's findings, each fixed with a regression test (2026-11-08).** All fourteen have their fix recorded in plan-history.md (Phases 1–3), and a test that runs in every `scripts/check.ps1`, or `-Live` where named:
  - F01 `output_onto_an_input_file_is_refused`; F02 `undelivered_output_is_exit_7`, `closed_streams_give_exact_exit_codes`, `stalled_stdout_and_stderr_together_still_exit_7`; F03 `sequences_and_global_temp_writes_are_blocked`, `global_temp_tables_are_blocked`, `sp_spaceused_update_usage_is_blocked`, `diagnostic_extended_procedures_are_allowed_by_exact_name` (the qualifiers); F04 `check_never_executes` (live), `check_refuses_noexec`, `compile_only_refuses_noexec`; F05 `unterminated_quotes_do_not_panic`, `arbitrary_input_never_panics`, `splitting_arbitrary_input_never_panics`, `a_panic_with_closed_streams_still_exits_10`; F06 the collapsed `-- N statements, M rows affected` (csv.rs and text.rs tests), `GO 1001` and the 10 MB cap (script.rs), the `--max-output` tests and the sweep above; F07 `a_stalled_reader_never_blocks_the_writer_and_times_out`, `async_delivery_keeps_the_runs_exit_code`; F08 the Ctrl+Break tests, `connect_failures_end_the_same_way_every_time`, and the checks above; F09 `duplicate_and_contradictory_keys_are_errors`, `windows_profile_ignores_sqlcmduser`; F10 `rejects_odd_length_utf16`, `odd_length_utf16_is_rejected`, `substitutes_variables` (`-v sep=GO`), `multi_line_values_keep_line_numbers_right`; F11 text.rs's `-- forged` header test and live `hostile_column_names_are_escaped`; F12 `the_profiles_lock_is_held_by_one_edit_at_a_time`, `a_failed_removal_can_be_finished_by_running_it_again`, `a_failed_undo_says_how_to_repair`.
  - **F13 had only its manual acceptance (Git Bash), which had no record.** Now a test: `a_mintty_terminal_gets_no_password_prompt` has PowerShell make a pipe named as mintty's pty (`msys-<hex>-pty0-from-master`), which Rust's `IsTerminal` takes for a terminal and which has no console mode, as `profiles add`'s stdin; sqlh reaches the prompt, refuses with the `winpty` advice, exit 3, reads nothing of what the pipe offers and saves no profile. Run by hand first with the release exe, the same.
  - **A flake in the closing live run, diagnosed and fixed (test only).** `mcp::tests::the_last_log_lines_reach_stderr_within_a_bound` (a unit test, no server) failed once in the 2016 pass of `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` at `92fe538`: three of five lines written when `flush_log` returned. The log thread handles lines in order and signals only after the earlier ones are written, so the flush ran out its `SIDE_WAIT` (500 ms): six paced writes of 5 ms took over 500 ms. That unit step took 30.3 s against the usual 22–23 s, while cargo unpacked mssql-tds 0.4.0 and two crate trees were diffed alongside. The product kept its bound; the test stacked ten load-sensitive sleeps against it. It now holds the sink once, for 50 ms, from another thread, then lets every write through at once, and still fails if the flush doesn't wait for the thread's answer (nothing written) or the thread answers early (lines missing). **Then `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passed at `d4fb782` on both servers, with nothing else running:** 494 unit tests, 126 debug and 120 release process tests, 86 debug and 79 release live tests on each (the 2016 unit step 24.7 s); exe 2,999,808 bytes.
  - **F14 uses Secret; coverage requires ownership review, not a freed-memory test:** `Secret` (`zeroize::Zeroizing<String>`) wipes values owned through that wrapper. The type does not prove that every temporary String or byte-buffer copy is wrapped. The already-carried invalid-UTF8 Credential Manager error-buffer follow-up remains open (§4, Carried over from finished work); the driver and other wiping limits remain in DESIGN.md §8. No freed-memory read is required or performed. This release-record qualification is R57; it does not close the existing runtime follow-up.

### AppVeyor release certification (decision 75, built 2026-11-08, first green run 2026-11-09)

The owner's AppVeyor account runs the release's own signed exe, and the tests built with it, against SQL Servers the owner's servers don't cover. Nothing is compiled there.
- **GitHub:** `release.yml`'s build job uploads the release test binary that `scripts/check.ps1` built alongside the exe (`cargo test --release --test cli`, same `SQLH_VERSION` and `SQLH_BUILD`) as the workflow artifact `sqlh-live-tests`, kept 30 days and never a release asset. `--no-run` only names the file (checked locally: one binary, nothing rebuilt).
- **The tests on another machine:** `tests/cli.rs` takes `SQLH_TEST_EXE` (the exe under test, 30 sites through `exe()`), `SQLH_TEST_ROOT` (the smoke script, its golden files, the icon) and `SQLH_TEST_TMPDIR` (its files), each falling back to what cargo compiled in. Checked here: the release test binary and exe copied to a folder of their own and run from it through the three variables passed 121 process tests and, against the `test` profile, 80 live tests, and left `target/tmp` untouched.
- **AppVeyor:** `appveyor.yml` runs only for a build started by hand (a tag push would race the release run it needs) and builds nothing (`build: off`). `scripts/appveyor-live.ps1` (Windows PowerShell 5.1 or later) finds the commit's successful release run (waiting up to 30 minutes for one still going), downloads `sqlh-<version>` and `sqlh-live-tests` with `SQLH_GITHUB_TOKEN`, checks the exe's Authenticode signature, its `SHA256SUMS.txt` hash and its `--version`, runs the process tests once, then for each instance writes a Windows-auth profile for `COMPUTERNAME\INSTANCE` (a named instance through SQL Browser: real local SSPI through the per-hop provider, with SQL Browser's port; not the provider's name lookup, since the driver takes this computer's own name as loopback, which isn't looked up: R61), logs `@@VERSION`, the login, sysadmin, `auth_scheme` and `net_transport`, creates `sqlh_test` and runs the live tests. Jobs: Visual Studio 2026 image (Windows Server 2019; SQL Server 2025 Enterprise Developer and 2022), Visual Studio 2019 (Windows Server 2019; 2019), Visual Studio 2017 (Windows Server 2016; 2017; allowed to fail, as evidence for the candidate Windows Server 2016 client floor). SQL Server 2016 on the images is SP1, below decision 49's SP3, so it isn't used.
- **Covers:** SQL Server 2017, 2019 and 2022, never tested before; a named instance through SQL Browser and Windows authentication to it (driver-0.4.0.md §8 item 6: decision 72's provider dispatched with real local SSPI and SQL Browser's port); Windows Server 2016 and 2019 as hosts. **Doesn't:** the provider's capped name lookup or its 500 ms budget (this computer's name is loopback to the driver, R61; the fake-server and unit tests keep covering them), a remote canonical name, Windows 11 (no image), Standard edition (Developer only), a real network, SmartScreen, Kerberos in a domain, an AG route, LocalDB (not on the images). The live tests make SQL-auth logins of their own (`Reader`), so each instance also needs mixed mode and the build account login-creation rights.
- **The owner's steps:** add the project in AppVeyor (it needs access to the private repository); add `SQLH_GITHUB_TOKEN` as a secure variable in its settings: a fine-grained token for `sequeltom/sqlh` alone, Actions: read, with an expiry; then, after a release run, "New build" on its commit.
- **First run (owner, 2026-11-08, `2e8b135`, a build started by hand):** the `init` gate let it through and the checkout worked. The script stopped at its first API call: GitHub answered 403, "Resource not accessible by personal access token", listing the release workflow's runs. That is the token's access: a fine-grained token that doesn't include this private repository, or lacks Actions: read. Nothing was downloaded or run. **Second run, after the owner gave the token Actions: read:** the Visual Studio 2026 job started (so that image name is AppVeyor's), the API call succeeded, and the script stopped as designed: `no successful release run for 2e8b135... in sequeltom/sqlh: run the release workflow for it first`, since none had run since that commit.
- **Third run, the first whole one (AppVeyor build 4, `d7756bd`, release run 10, `0.1.0-dev.10`; read by an agent from the owner's signed-in browser pane):**
  - **The download path worked on all three jobs.** The Visual Studio 2026 job waited 9 minutes for release run 10, as designed. The digests matched; the signature was valid and had the expected signer; `--version` matched.
  - **Process tests:** 121 of 121 against the signed exe on each job, Windows Server 2016 included.
  - **The instances:**

    | Windows | SQL Server | Login | Live tests |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Server 2019 | 2025 Enterprise Developer RTM (17.0.1000.7) | `appveyor` as sysadmin, NTLM over TCP | 79 of 80 |
    | Server 2019 | 2022 Developer RTM (16.0.1000.6) | same | 79 of 80 |
    | Server 2019 | 2019 Developer RTM (15.0.2000.5) | same | 79 of 80 |
    | Server 2016 | 2017 | none: the connect failed | none ran |

    So the instance names `SQL2022` and `SQL2025`, Windows auth as sysadmin, mixed mode for `Reader`, and the R59 collection (SQL2022 ran after SQL2025 failed) are confirmed.
  - **The one live failure, the same on every instance:** `the_smoke_script_matches_its_golden_output`, whose golden JSON pinned `"auth": "sql"`, the profile's method. **Fixed:** `normalize_json` now checks that `auth` is `windows` or `sql`, then writes `<auth>`, as it does for the server and version; the JSON contract test still checks the value. The smoke test passes on both owner servers. This needs a new release run, since AppVeyor runs the test binary that run built.
  - **Windows Server 2016:** every TLS connect fails before the login is sent, `AcquireCredentialsHandleW(UNISP_NAME) failed ... 0x8009030D`. The driver builds only `SCH_CREDENTIALS` (Windows 10 1809 / Server 2019) and says it intentionally doesn't support Server 2016; 0.3.0 is the same, so this isn't a regression (upstream-issues.md U12). That blocks the candidate Windows 10 / Server 2016 client floor below 1809 (Phase 7) unless the driver changes. **sqlh's hint for it is wrong:** "a self-signed certificate needs -C; an old server without TLS 1.2 may need -No", though `-C` was in effect and `-No` can't help. **Fixed (owner, 2026-11-09; it changes a connection error's text, DESIGN.md §6):** an error with `AcquireCredentialsHandleW(UNISP_NAME)` in it, Schannel's credentials on this machine, gets `this machine couldn't set up TLS, so the login was never sent: the driver's TLS needs Windows 10 1809 or Windows Server 2019 or later (on a later Windows, check that this machine's Schannel settings allow TLS 1.2)`, in both hint forms and every encryption mode, in place of the strict hint too. The driver's SSPI login (`security/windows`) calls the same API without that name, so a Windows-auth failure keeps its own hint. `a_tls_setup_failure_on_this_machine_names_the_windows_it_needs` checks the three modes and both forms, with the error AppVeyor's Server 2016 job showed. Seen on Server 2016 in the fifth run (below). `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` then passed at `7e4f340` on both owner servers (510 unit, 127 and 121 process, 87 and 80 live tests on each), the first full live run there since `88ac335`. For the next external review brief (decision 52: the output contract).
  - **Added after it:** `appveyor-live.ps1` now also requires the signer's CN to match the release signer, as the review suggested once the subject was seen. The thumbprint isn't pinned, since the certificate is short-lived.
- **Fourth run, the first green one (AppVeyor build 6, 2026-11-09, `6b954a3`, release run 11, `0.1.0-dev.11`; read by an agent from the owner's signed-in browser pane):** the build passed, with the Windows Server 2016 job failing as allowed.
  - Downloads, digests, signature (`Valid`, the pinned CN) and `--version` passed on all three jobs; 121 of 121 process tests on each.
  - **80 of 80 live tests on SQL Server 2025, 2022 and 2019** (the same builds and login as the third run: `appveyor` as sysadmin, NTLM over TCP), each instance about 2 minutes; the script ended `sqlh 0.1.0-dev.11 certified on Visual Studio 2026 against SQL2025, SQL2022` and `... on Visual Studio 2019 against SQL2019`. The smoke golden's auth normalization is confirmed off the owner's servers.
  - **SQL Server 2017 on Windows Server 2016:** the same TLS failure as the third run (`0x8009030D`, U12), at the connect stage, exit 2; nothing else ran on it.
  - So every item the first runs left unconfirmed is now confirmed: the image name `Visual Studio 2026`, the instance names, Windows auth as sysadmin, the download from PowerShell 5.1, and that no live test needs a SQL login of the profile's. Still open: the `release.yml` change, the test overrides and the R59–R61 fixes go in the next external review brief (decision 52: release workflows) before a release relies on them.
- **Fifth run (AppVeyor build 9, 2026-11-13, `64b8b17`, release run 12, `0.1.0-dev.12`; read the same way):** green again, the same as the fourth. The download checks passed on every job; 121 of 121 process tests on each; 80 of 80 live tests on SQL Server 2025, 2022 and 2019. The Server 2016 job's connect error now ends with the new hint (the TLS setup fix above), `...(status=0x8009030D); this machine couldn't set up TLS, before anything reached the server: the driver's TLS needs Windows 10 1809 or Windows Server 2019 or later (...)`, with no `-C` or `-No`; exit 2, as allowed. (R62 has since reworded "before anything reached the server" to "so the login was never sent".)
- **Sixth run, 0.2.0's certification (AppVeyor build 93, 2026-11-27, `637c3ab` on the tag `v0.2.0`, a rebuild of the tag push's build, started by the owner; release run 18, `0.2.0`; read by an agent from the owner's signed-in browser pane): failed, two findings open.** On all three jobs the script took release run 18 (the tag's, not the dispatch's run 17: it takes the highest successful run number for the commit), the digests matched, the signature was `Valid` with the pinned CN, and `--version` was `sqlh 0.2.0 (json contract 2.1)`; 125 of 125 process tests on each. The first run of slice 2's live tests off the owner's 2025 and 2016 servers.
  - **SQL Server 2022 (Visual Studio 2026 image, Server 2019): 88 of 88 live tests.**
  - **SQL Server 2017 on Server 2016: the known TLS failure** (`0x8009030D`, U12) at the connect stage, exit 2, with the setup hint; allowed to fail, as in every run.
  - **Finding A, SQL Server 2025 (Visual Studio 2026 image), the first instance the script starts: the connect failed, so nothing ran on it.** `cannot connect to APPVEYOR-VM\SQL2025 (Windows auth, encrypt mandatory): ... SQL Server Browser did not respond within 999ms. Verify that the SQL Server Browser service is running on '::1'`, exit 2 in 1,018 ms. `appveyor-live.ps1` starts SQLBrowser, then the instance, and connects as soon as `Start-Service` returns; SQL2022, started next on the same VM, connected through Browser, and SQL2025 connected the same way in the fourth and fifth runs (same driver, 0.4.0). Most likely a readiness race on the VM, not sqlh: not yet shown, since one run can't tell a race from an image change. The fix to weigh is a bounded wait in the script, after an instance starts, until Browser reports it, before the first connect (a wait for the server, not a retry of a test; `appveyor-live.ps1` is a release workflow under decision 52).
  - **Finding B, SQL Server 2019 RTM (15.0.2000.5, Visual Studio 2019 image, Server 2019): 87 of 88 live tests; `live::mcp_tools::the_activity_tool_shows_a_busy_sqlh_mcp_session_and_not_an_idle_one` failed** at `tests/cli.rs:15161` (at `v0.2.0`), `assert_eq!(shown["open_tran"], 1)`: the busy `sqlh mcp` session, blocked inside its wrapper transaction, showed `open_tran` 0 with `tran_ms` 360, `blocked_by` 54, `LCK_M_S`, `status` `suspended` and the right statement. `activity` takes `open_tran` from `sys.dm_exec_sessions.open_transaction_count` (`schema.rs`, `ACTIVITY`), and `tran_ms` from `sys.dm_tran_session_transactions` and `sys.dm_tran_active_transactions`, which saw the transaction. So on this server the session's count read 0 for a session whose request runs in a transaction. The call ran on a reused connection: its setup carried the reset and ended in the wrapper's `BEGIN TRANSACTION`, and the blocked read was the next batch. Passes on 2025, 2022 and 2016. **A defect in 0.2.0's output on SQL Server 2019 until shown otherwise** (`open_tran` is what a DBA reads to find who holds a transaction open); whether an idle session's count is right, and whether 2019 or its RTM build is the cause, is unknown. Diagnosed next on AppVeyor's 2019, since neither owner server runs 2019, by `scripts/appveyor-diag.ps1` on the branch `appveyor-diag` (prepared 2026-11-27; a one-off, not for `main`): no sqlh, .NET Framework's SqlClient from Windows PowerShell 5.1, a blocker, a reader and an observer; it reads `sys.dm_exec_sessions.open_transaction_count`, `sys.dm_exec_requests.open_transaction_count` and the session's `sys.dm_tran_session_transactions` rows for a reader running blocked in a transaction (begun in its own batch on a new connection, the same on a reused connection whose batch carries the reset, and begun in the query's batch) and idle in one (new and reused), on every instance of the three images; it also times each instance's first connect through Browser after it starts (finding A). Its fix, a correction to released output, is the kind of real fix decision 100 names for 0.2.1.

### The exe's icon (owner, 2026-11-08)

The owner asked for an icon. Built: `assets/sqlh.ico` (a navy tile, a white database cylinder, an amber `>_`; 16, 24, 32, 48 and 256 px, palette PNGs, 5,980 bytes), drawn by `scripts/make-icon.py` (Pillow, a dev tool; the build reads only the committed .ico), and written by `build.rs` as `RT_ICON`s and an `RT_GROUP_ICON` in the version resource's .res file, still without rc.exe or a crate (DESIGN.md §4). The exe grows 6,656 bytes, to 3,105,280 of 3,145,728 (40,448 left). `the_exe_carries_its_icon` reads each image back out of the exe against the .ico, byte for byte, and has Windows draw it at 16 and 256 px; with the group misnamed it fails. Explorer showing it on a clean workstation goes with §7's clean-workstation check. The design is a first draft for the owner to accept or redirect; it is a build input only, so it can change without touching the output contract.

### 0.1.0 release-check external review (a86e0b8..92fe538)

Reviewed the supplied November 8, 2026 brief against main, HEAD `92fe538` and a clean starting tree verified. **The decoding change and F13 regression are accepted. No new runtime finding. R57 [P3], documentation-only, corrected in the working tree; the existing Credential Manager invalid-UTF8 wiping follow-up remains open.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-0.1.0-release-checks.md. No reviewer commit or push.

**Decode:** decode_once retains strict UTF-8/UTF-16 selection and malformed-input errors. The wrapper removes only a contiguous FEFF run at decoded offset zero, in place, without a second String allocation; shifting the remaining bytes is still linear work. The normalized text is both classified and sent. Marks after whitespace/newlines, inside literals/quoted identifiers and in later batches remain; line numbers do not move. Thirty independent exact-source cases passed across UTF-8 and UTF-16 LE/BE (one/two/three/32 leading marks, mark-only input, preserved interior content, malformed UTF-8/UTF-16 and UTF-32 rejection). Four release-CLI controls passed for stdin normalization, a marked later batch and unchanged -Q/positional scope. Additional process controls confirmed normalization of each of two files, MCP's marked SQL refusal before connect, and an actual captured Windows PowerShell 5.1 pipeline preserving SELECT N'Café … 日本' through the exact-source decoder helper. Evidence: artifacts/review-release-decode.json, -decode-cli.json and artifacts/review-release-scopes.json, with ignored scratch helpers. No real SQL Server listener or owner profile used.

**Judgments 1 and 2:** a second FEFF after an encoding signature could intentionally be part of an unquoted first identifier, so normalization can change that token/name resolution, especially with writes allowed. No decoder can distinguish that intention from the repeated shell preamble. Accept sqlh's documented leading-run-as-preamble policy for the demonstrated harness shape; do not claim universal Unicode/SQL semantic equivalence. A significant FEFF inside a quoted identifier or literal is preserved. Keep -Q, positional queries and MCP SQL unchanged, since they carry decoded text and no known transport adds marks there; their refusal already explains the correction. Reference: [Unicode BOM/content distinction](https://www.unicode.org/faq/utf_bom.html).

**F13 and judgment 3:** accept the named-pipe stand-in. It deliberately triggers Rust's Windows pseudo-terminal heuristic while lacking a Win32 console mode, and the expected winpty/error text distinguishes this from nonterminal-input refusal. Both debug/release suites passed. read_password obtains EchoOff before either prompting or reading; that source ordering plus the process assertions supports the no-read result. The absent canary alone is not independent read instrumentation. The outer run/finish watchdog bounds the PowerShell helper and its child tree, including the pipe wait. No unsafe code added; the toolchain is pinned. This is not a manual test of every mintty/winpty version. Reference: [Rust IsTerminal](https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/std/io/trait.IsTerminal.html).

**R57 [P3], corrected here:** the new F14 release record claimed every own password copy was a Secret and the compiler enforced it. Secret is a Zeroizing<String> alias; it protects those values but does not prevent unwrapped temporary copies. The already-known credman.rs invalid-UTF8 FromUtf8Error buffer remains unwiped and is still carried open; no new runtime issue was discovered or fixed. The release record/checklist now describe wrapper/ownership-review evidence and its limits, not a universal compiler proof or a runtime wipe test. No freed-memory probe or complete repeat of the F14 ownership audit was performed. Documentation-only, closed in this working tree; no standalone review round needed under decision 52.

**Hosted signing independently checked:** read-only GitHub API queries show workflow_dispatch run 42429985874 completed successfully at a86e0b8db71a7ea0423485802fd862f1c73ccfc2, build/sign/verify/publish all successful. This supports closing the rename-signing gate, but predates the decoding/F13 changes. No claim of a signed build or hosted test of 92fe538. The draft dev.9 and Azure credential update/removal remain supplied evidence; no token was minted, asset downloaded, Azure credential inspected, workflow dispatched or signing/publication action performed. Evidence: artifacts/review-release-checks-hosted-run.json and -hosted-jobs.json; [reported run](https://github.com/sequeltom/sqlh/actions/runs/42429985874).

**Independent standard check:** first scripts/check.ps1 passed without retry in **100 s**: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, **494 unit, 126 debug process and 120 release process tests**, locked release build, size/import checks. Five unit, 86 debug live and 79 release live tests remained ignored. Exe **2,999,808 bytes**, unchanged, 145,920 below 3 MiB; ten approved in-box imports. Log: artifacts/review-release-checks-check.log. Only review/status Markdown, the R57 evidence qualification and ignored probes changed during review; no production/tests, Cargo, credentials, owner profiles/database, client/hosted configuration, commit or push changed.

**Supplied, not independently rerun:** the live non-ASCII query, new-console cancellation/close experiments and server cleanup observations, 54-run real-server output-cap sweep and other live shell checks. The brief's LIVE verification line quantifies no full suite at this HEAD. Real mintty, live suites and the remaining clean-machine/edition/packaged-client gates were not independently checked or newly marked complete by this review.

### Driver upgrade to mssql-tds 0.4.0 (owner, 2026-11-08: "upstream released a new version 0.4.0 ... we need to review and take it in"; step 1 done; step 2 under way)

0.4.0 was released 2026-11-07 (MIT, not yanked). **Step 1, the analysis, is [docs/notes/driver-0.4.0.md](notes/driver-0.4.0.md)** (2026-11-08): the two crates compared file by file (51 files differ, 7 new), by six readers with an independent checker each, every claim re-read against both versions and sqlh; nothing built, nothing connected.
