Its findings in short: no compile break; U9 partly fixed upstream (the driver's SPN lookup and SSPI calls now run on tokio's blocking pool, inside `-l` and the cancel), so sqlh's 500 ms pre-lookup stays but its stated reasons change; 0.4.0's parser takes a protocol prefix before an IPv6 address, which opens `np:<IPv6>\INST` and the IPv6 UNC pipe forms past sqlh's remote-pipe refusal, so step 2 must widen it; LOGIN7 asks for vector feature version 2 by default; Schannel failures read `TLS handshake failed for '<host>'`; the packet dumps (password included) are unchanged, so AGENTS.md's Driver logging rule stands, with one new trap (a tracing dispatcher would silence `db::phases`); U10 and U11 unchanged; new public hooks (`SecurityContextProvider`, connect-stage spans, data classification) recorded as candidates, none for the bump. Its §7.1–§7.5 go to the owner one at a time; §7.1 is decision 70 (0.1.0 waits for 0.4.0, and takes no later driver), §7.2 decision 71 (vector version 2, set explicitly). Step 2 (§9: the bump on a branch, the refusal fix, the vector pin, tests, docs, the live checks in §8) starts with what no decision changes (the bump, the IPv6 refusal, the TLS fixtures, the encoder test), on the local branch `driver-0.4.0`; then the vector pin (decision 71), and a driver upgrade goes to external review before anything is built on it (decision 52).

**Step 2 so far (2026-11-08, branch `driver-0.4.0`, local):** `1352077` the bare bump (no code change; `scripts/check.ps1` passed; exe 2,999,808 → 3,099,648 bytes, `cargo bloat`: mssql_tds +60 KiB, tokio +20 KiB, std +10 KiB, of it about 29 KiB the data-classification parser sqlh never calls; 46 KB of the 3 MiB budget left). `15a0e87`: §9 items 2, 4, 5, 6 and 7's docs: `split_protocol` on 0.4.0's rule and IPv6 under `tcp:` in `data_source`, so `np:<IPv6>\INST` and the IPv6 UNC forms are refused (unit rows and a process row); the TLS fixtures in the `TlsHandshakeError` shape, one 0.3.0 case kept; `a_batch_reaches_the_server_unit_for_unit_across_packets` (packets checked at 4,096 bytes, a pair cut for odd `k`); the stale comments; DESIGN.md §4.2, §4.4, §14, mcp-mode.md §5, upstream-issues.md, connect-latency.md, m0-driver-spike.md, AGENTS.md. `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` at `15a0e87` passed on both servers: 495 unit tests, 126 debug and 120 release process tests, 86 debug and 79 release live tests on each; exe 3,099,136 bytes. Then §9 item 3 for decision 71: the pin, the LOGIN7 test of 0x0E and 0x09, float16 rendering with exhaustive unit tests, DESIGN.md §5.1. The live vector test and the fake-server vector test are built; an independent review before commit confirmed one P3 (the native-arrival claim needed its condition; fixed) and refuted three (driver-0.4.0.md §10). The exe is 3,103,744 bytes with decision 71: float16 rendering is done in integers, since a first version that formatted decimals to a precision cost 25,600 bytes. §8's by-hand checks 2–14 are done and recorded in the note's §10, all as predicted: among them, Ctrl+C during the driver's SPN lookup ends a run in 0.32 s (3.52 s on 0.3.0), and on the bare bump a pipe to a raw IPv6 address stalled 21 s past `-l 2`, which the fix refuses in 14 ms. §7.3 is decision 72: `db::spn::HopSpn`, the driver's per-hop `SecurityContextProvider`, replaces the pre-lookup (7 unit and 6 fake-server tests, among them each routed hop's own SPN and SQL Browser's port; exe 3,098,112 bytes). An independent review before commit confirmed eight findings, none in the provider's behaviour (three scheduling-dependent tests, a missing pin of the looked-up host, doc wording), all fixed. Live, by address on both servers: `-K ReadOnly -l 2` now connects in about 0.6 s (exit 4 at 3.02 s before), Ctrl+C inside the wait ends a run at once, and `sqlh mcp`'s first calls don't wait on each other (driver-0.4.0.md §10, Decision 72). §7.4 is decision 73 (the local-pipe check takes the driver's computer name), §7.5 decision 74 (the other new features are candidates after 0.1.0). Still to do: real SSPI through a named instance, a routed hop and Kerberos, none at hand; the live float16 check, which needs `PREVIEW_FEATURES` on in `sqlh_test` (a database-level change outside decision 21, the owner's call); §8 item 6 (no named instance) and 13 (no LocalDB); and the external review.

### mssql-tds 0.4.0 external review (92fe538..e8832d0)

November 8, 2026. Reviewed on local branch `driver-0.4.0`, HEAD `e8832d0`, with a clean starting tree. **The driver integration, IPv6 refusal, per-hop SPN design and decision 71's float16 rule are accepted. No P1/P2 or new authentication/output defect found. R58 [P3], a test-only scheduling race, remains open.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-driver-0.4.0.md. No reviewer merge, commit or push; the branch and the combined release gate in §7 remain pending the owner's go-ahead and the remaining checks. **Merged 2026-11-08** on the owner's go-ahead, after R58's fix: `f0e2465` (`--no-ff`, as `driver-0.3.0` was), its tree the branch tip's, which `scripts/check.ps1` passed. `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` on merged `main` passed on both servers (508 unit, 126 debug and 120 release process, 87 debug and 80 release live tests on each; exe 3,098,624 bytes), the first live run since decision 73's commit; pushed after it.

**R58 [P3], open:** `mcp::tests::the_last_log_lines_reach_stderr_within_a_bound` (`src/mcp/mod.rs:1428-1441`, `d4fb782`) starts the releaser's 50 ms sleep before starting `flush`'s stopwatch, then asserts `took >= hold`. A legal delay between spawning that thread and calling `flush` makes the lines arrive before that clock starts, so correct delivery fails the lower bound. An independent exact-source probe retained production `Log`/`start_log_to`/`flush_log`, the test Sink/Sunk and the real 500 ms SIDE_WAIT: the ordinary sequence took 50,673 microseconds; adding only a 100 ms test-thread delay before flush took 45 microseconds, all five lines correct, lower-bound assertion false. **Acceptance:** remove the unsupported lower timing bound or synchronize it with the work measured; preferably prove the flush stays pending while the sink is held, release it, then check complete output and bounded completion. Retain stalled/full/no-log cases and verify bypassing the acknowledgment wait still fails. No product bound changes. Self-verify under decision 52 and list the fix in the next brief; no standalone external round is needed. Evidence: scratch/review-driver040-probes.py and artifacts/review-driver040-log-clock.txt. The ordinary unsandboxed suite passed; this is a constructed legal-interleaving failure, not a random suite failure. **Fixed 2026-11-08, self-verified (decision 52):** the slow case now waits for the sink to be held in its first write, polls the flush once and asserts it is pending, then lets the sink go and checks the five lines and that the flush ended within `SIDE_WAIT` of the release; the lower bound and the releaser thread are gone, and the stalled, full-channel and no-log cases are unchanged. A 100 ms test-thread sleep before the flush, the review's counterexample, passed; `flush_log` returning without its acknowledgment wait failed at once ("the flush ended with the sink held"); both probes were reverted. `scripts/check.ps1` passed (exe 3,098,624 bytes, unchanged). List it in the next brief.

**Judgments 1 through 3:** every Windows-auth connect installs HopSpn; the driver's sole production SSPI-handler factory receives it and the current transport host/port. Cache/shared-memory/normal/Enhanced Routing paths retain it. The provider always supplies `Some(server_spn)` to the built-in context factory and changes no package, loopback or channel-binding field. Its in-time composition is the driver's own canonicalizer plus `make_spn(..., None, port)`, with no additional IPv6/bracket/scope parsing. The driver does not repeat canonicalization; its empty-SPN loopback retry and Negotiate limitations remain. Moving each lookup wait into the one login deadline is accepted, including a normal two-hop AG route's two waits and a healthy slow connection newly reaching exit 4 at `-l 1`. Two hops is not a universal maximum (the driver allows ten redirects). Keep the public canonicalizer rather than copying private is_fqdn: an FQDN can wait 500 ms under a full cap, but stays inside the stated budget and retains the same fallback name. Real Kerberos and Extended Protection interoperability remain unverified.

**Judgment 4:** float16's shortest decimal at its own precision, whole values whole, is accepted as the explicit output rule. An independent exact-source formatter probe checked all 63,488 finite encodings against exact Python Fraction midpoint intervals and shorter-decimal candidates, including signs, zero and 14,334 nonzero whole encodings; all passed. The wire-decoder and LOGIN7 feature tests passed in the suite. This is synthetic native-input evidence, not a real server's V2 acknowledgment/native float16 arrival; PREVIEW_FEATURES was not changed. Microsoft's vector documentation confirms the SQL Server 2025 preview requirement. Evidence: artifacts/review-driver040-half.txt and -half-check.json.

**Judgment 5:** the prefix/UNC order matches 0.4.0, including lpc:/admin: before an explicit UNC path; remote IPv6 names/instances stay on TCP and the cited pipe spellings are refused. LocalDB and ordinary remote lpc: behavior were traced to the driver's handling. Decision 73's normal computer-name source is aligned; its documented environment fallback when the driver has no name remains. No new remote-pipe bypass found; this does not add an endpoint allowlist to Browser/cache/routing. An isolated preexisting alias distinction is not an upgrade finding: literal UNC `\\(local)\pipe\...` returned OS error 64 immediately and did not reach a disposable local pipe, while `\\localhost\pipe\...` reached it. SQL's np:(local) alias does not make `(local)` a Windows UNC alias; use the documented `\\.\pipe\...`. No 21-second stall was demonstrated for that edge. Sources/probes and limits are in the full review.

**Independent verification:** scripts/check.ps1 passed outside the sandbox in **103 s**: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, **508 unit, 126 debug process and 120 release process tests**, locked release build, size/import checks; five unit and 87/80 live tests ignored. Exe **3,098,624 bytes**, **47,104 below 3 MiB**, 98,816 above the preceding reviewed 0.3.0 exe; the same ten approved in-box imports. cargo-about's locked/offline Windows-target generation passed without block2/objc2 entries. Logs: artifacts/review-driver-0.4.0-check-unsandboxed.log and artifacts/review-driver040-about.log. The initial sandbox run (433 passed, 75 failed) stopped at unit tests with denied temporary-file/loopback operations (Windows errors 5/10013 and dependent failures); this was diagnosed before running outside it, not retried until green. Exact-source probes and a disposable local pipe used no owner configuration or credentials.

**Supplied, not independently rerun:** both live SQL suites and the note's by-hand server/shell measurements. No owner live profile, SQL Server, Credential Manager item or preview setting was accessed or changed. Real SSPI through a named instance/AG route, domain Kerberos, Extended Protection, native float16, LocalDB startup and the other recorded environment-dependent checks retain their limits. Only the ignored report/probes and PLAN.md/AGENTS.md review status were written; no production/test implementation or dependency change.

### Post-0.4.0 changes external review (e8832d0..2e8b135)

November 8, 2026. Reviewed main at `2e8b1357029ed43d663d1bd03d174348430d3d3d`, clean starting tree. **Close R58. Accept the credential-wiping implementation, icon resources and test-path overrides. The added test artifact preserves R22's signing/write boundaries. R59 [P2] and R60/R61 [P3] remain open.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-post-0.4.0.md. No reviewer commit, push, service/cloud change or live SQL work.

| Finding | Evidence and required follow-up |
|---|---|
| **R59 [P2], open: per-instance PowerShell failures abort the remaining certification** | scripts/appveyor-live.ps1:135-159 starts the service outside try, has no catch for terminating body errors and lets stop errors escape finally. Unlike native sqlh/test exit codes, these errors skip later instances and the collected summary. A Windows PowerShell 5.1 probe executed the actual foreach AST with service/executable mocks: an injected SQLFAIL startup error produced only its start event, no SQLNEXT attempt and no failure-array entry. **Acceptance:** collect stage-specific startup/body/cleanup failures, preserve a nonzero final result and continue when isolation permits. After a failed stop, establish isolation or explicitly skip remaining conflicting servers; never certify a different server on the shared port. Verify startup/body/stop failures with mocks before relying on the script's continuation promise. Evidence: scratch/review-post040-script-mocks.ps1, artifacts/review-post040-service-failure.json. |
| **R60 [P3], open: qualify the inferred lost credential write** | src/credman.rs:123-126 and the carried concurrency record call write loss measured. The available scratch/credrace source maps every CredReadW failure to false without GetLastError, ignores delete errors and derives targets from tag/worker without PID. The supplied observation is one target unreadable through two seconds, not proof of a permanently lost write or its cause. **Acceptance:** qualify comment/record; a stronger experiment retains unique process/target/launch identities, immediate OS errors for every failed operation and a final observation before cleanup. No product retry is required to close this documentation finding. The in-process mutex serializes one test binary only, not other processes; fixed self-test target names can collide between test binaries. |
| **R61 [P3], open: qualify the local named-instance SPN coverage** | scripts/appveyor-live.ps1:18-20 and the AppVeyor coverage record say COMPUTERNAME instead of localhost takes the lookup path. When the computer name matches the driver's, parse_server maps this TCP/named-instance host to localhost; HopSpn runs with Browser's port but not_loopback skips the canonicalization worker. A differing NetBIOS/DNS spelling is not a guarantee either. **Acceptance:** state that the job covers local real SSPI, Browser/port resolution and provider dispatch, not the capped DNS worker/500 ms slow-lookup path, remote canonicalization or domain Kerberos. Keep the existing fake/unit worker checks; no profile change is required for 0.1.0. |

**Fixes, 2026-11-08 (for the next brief; R59 is P2, so that review covers it before a release relies on the script):**
- **R59:** `scripts/appveyor-live.ps1`'s per-instance work is `Test-Instance` and `Invoke-Instances`. The start, the profile, the connect, the scratch database and the live tests run inside one `try`, each error recorded with its stage (`SQLA (start): ...`, `SQLB (live tests): exit 1`). The stop is always tried, in its own `try`, and is confirmed by the service's status. After an instance hasn't stopped, every later one is recorded as skipped and never started (`SQLE (skipped): SQLC didn't stop, and every instance listens on 1433`). Executables' output goes to the host, not into the function's result. Dot-sourced, the script defines its functions and runs nothing. `scripts/appveyor-live.tests.ps1` drives `Invoke-Instances` with mocked `Start-Service`, `Stop-Service`, `Get-Service` and executables, through a startup exception, a live-test exit 1, a missing instance, a stop exception and the skip after it, checking the failures and the exact order of events. It passes in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and 7, and `scripts/check.ps1` runs it in 5.1 after fmt (so CI does). With `Start-Service` moved back outside the `try`, it fails with the exception escaping.
- **R60:** the `credman::serial` comment and the concurrency record (§4, Carried over) now state the observation and that its cause is unconfirmed, with what a conclusive probe would keep. They also note that the mutex orders one test binary only. No product change.
- **R61:** the script's description and the AppVeyor record now say the job covers real local SSPI through the provider with SQL Browser's port, not the provider's name lookup or its budget, since this computer's own name is loopback to the driver.
- **From the judgments:**
  - Each downloaded zip must match the `digest` GitHub records for the artifact. The field is present on run 9's artifacts (`sha256:...`).
  - The token is removed from the environment and the headers once the downloads are done, before any test or the exe runs.
  - Actions: read is now described as also reading runs and their logs (decision 75, `appveyor.yml`, the AppVeyor record).
  - The registry's instance list drops PowerShell's `PS*` properties.
  - The live tests' need for mixed mode is recorded.
  - Pinning the signer's subject waits for the first run, which logs it.

**R58 independently closed:** the current exact-source slow-case fixture passed with a 100 ms delay before its first flush poll; removing only the production acknowledgment wait failed immediately with "the flush ended with the sink held". The full suite passed too. Evidence: scratch/review-post040-r58.py, artifacts/review-post040-r58.json. No implementation was edited for these probes.

**Credential judgment:** CredReadW here requests only CRED_TYPE_GENERIC. Microsoft's return-buffer and CREDENTIALW contracts permit writing the caller's returned CredentialBlob, then freeing the one block; wiping it does not change persistence. The Zeroizing Vec owns the one copy, mem::take/from_utf8 transfer its allocation, success wraps it in Secret, and failure restores into_bytes before constructing the public error. The unit ownership check passed. This closes the carried invalid-UTF8 wiping implementation follow-up. User/comment metadata need not be wiped for F14. Drop-based wiping is not an arbitrary-panic/abort guarantee: sqlh's panic hook exits without unwinding. Real Credential Manager tests/churn were not independently rerun. R60 limits the separate concurrency inference; recording that reliability issue remains appropriate for 0.1.0 instead of building a blind retry on an unconfirmed cause.

**Release/security judgments:** Stage tests remains in build, not sign/publish, and paired test/unsigned-code execution gains no Azure or repository-write authority. AppVeyor does add a repository Actions-read credential to the build-code execution boundary under decision 75. That permission also reads run information/logs, not only artifacts; it cannot modify code/releases or reveal GitHub's stored secret values. A same-build hash detects damage/mismatch, not a malicious build; checking the REST artifact digest is useful integrity defense without signing the test program in the privileged job. A Valid exe signature plus the owner-controlled release-run provenance is acceptable here; an expected publisher subject is useful additional identity checking, whereas issuer-only or short-lived thumbprint pinning is not equivalent. Full-SHA selection and unique artifact names fit the current release workflow's owner/main/tag and dispatch/push model. No new release/AppVeyor run or actual signed asset was exercised by this review.

**Independent validation:** scripts/check.ps1 passed on its first permitted run in **104 s**: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, **509 unit, 127 debug process and 121 release process tests**, locked build, size/import checks; six unit and 87/80 live tests ignored. Exe **3,105,280 bytes**, **40,448 below 3 MiB**, same ten approved in-box imports. Stage tests' cargo query returned exactly one current cli binary, fresh, without rebuilding. Log: artifacts/review-post040-check.log. Read-only GitHub metadata confirms CI run **42532575197** passed at this exact SHA; artifacts/review-post040-hosted-runs.json.

**Additional isolated checks:** the copied release exe/test binary passed all **121 process tests** in **35.922 s**, with spaces in all three overrides, a separate fixture root and working directory outside it; target/tmp stayed unchanged. The fixtures included smoke/golden files but their live reads were not exercised. The current signature/sum/version segment, with mocked signature status, refused changed bytes. Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 parsed the script and, against two local HTTP servers, stripped a synthetic Bearer header on the storage redirect while preserving the downloaded bytes. The icon's five entries, offsets, PNG dimensions/headers and chunk CRCs passed an independent parser; the process tests also read its PE resources and rendered it on Windows. PNG icon resources are supported since Vista, within the candidate Windows 10/Server 2016 floor. Sources, artifact paths and primary documentation links are in the full report.

**Limits:** no owner profile, Credential Manager item, SQL Server or service was accessed/changed. No live suite, private artifact download, clean-workstation Explorer/SmartScreen, actual AppVeyor job or its YAML schema validation was independently run. Public AppVeyor image/manual-trigger documentation was checked; instance names, rights and image behavior still need the first hosted run. The live suite creates its own SQL-auth Reader logins even with a Windows-auth root profile, so it requires mixed mode as well as admin login rights. Developer live and Credential Manager results remain supplied evidence. Only ignored probes/report/artifacts and PLAN.md/AGENTS.md review records were written; no production/test/workflow/build implementation changed.

### R59 through R61 fix verification and subsequent changes (2e8b135..7e4f340)

November 13, 2026. Reviewed main at 7e4f340, clean starting tree. **R59, R60 and R61 independently closed. Certification refactor, artifact digests, token removal and signer check accepted within the existing trusted-release-run model. Two new P3 follow-ups, R62 and R63, remain open; no P1/P2 found.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-r59-r61-verification.md. No reviewer commit, push or hosted/live run.

- **R59 closed:** Test-Instance catches startup/body/native failures by stage, sends executable output to Out-Host, separately attempts the stop and checks Get-Service. Exceptions or non-Stopped status cause later instances to be explicitly skipped. The committed mock suite passed through check.ps1 under Windows PowerShell 5.1 and independently under PowerShell 7. Additional mocks in both shells verified Write-Error promotion under ErrorActionPreference Stop, a Running status after a nominal stop, and no subsequent start. No real service/profile was touched. SCM Stopped is an appropriate confirmation here, not a proof about arbitrary detached listeners; no delay is required merely for TIME_WAIT.
- **R60 closed:** comment/plan now qualify the unreadable credential observation and its unknown cause, and limit the mutex to one test binary. **R61 closed:** local named-instance coverage is described as SSPI/provider/Browser-port coverage, excluding the lookup worker; mixed mode for Reader logins is acknowledged.
- **R62 [P3], open:** connect_error's new Schannel hint says "before anything reached the server". Mandatory and optional/LoginOnly paths already completed TCP and TDS prelogin before the local TLS credential acquisition fails. This is before LOGIN7, not before all server contact. **Acceptance:** remove that broad claim or name the precise pre-login boundary, retaining exit 2, local OS guidance and priority over certificate advice; update test/comment/docs. Source evidence: pinned handler_factory::SessionHandler::execute, transport/ssl_handler.rs and win_tls/engine.rs. No Server 2016 reproduction was independently rerun.
- **R63 [P3], open:** normalize_json accepts either valid auth value before overwriting it with <auth>. A SQL-auth run falsely reported as Windows now passes the same golden; the other live contract assertion also accepts either, and formatter fixtures only serialize a method already supplied to them. **Acceptance:** retain the portable placeholder but check the expected controlled connection method first, or add equivalent execution-path coverage; reporting the opposite valid method must fail. This is lost regression coverage, not evidence of a current wrong auth report.

**Fixes, 2026-11-13 (P3; self-verified under decision 52, for the next brief):**
- **R62:** the hint now reads `this machine couldn't set up TLS, so the login was never sent: ...`, the rest unchanged: exit 2, the Windows guidance, and its priority over the certificate and strict hints. The source comment names the boundary (after TCP and, below strict, the prelogin; before LOGIN7). `a_tls_setup_failure_on_this_machine_names_the_windows_it_needs` asserts the new text and that `reached` is gone. DESIGN.md §6 and mcp-mode.md §4 have the new wording.
- **R63:** `server_auth` asks the server how the profile's login was made, in a run of its own (`sys.dm_exec_connections.auth_scheme`: `SQL` is `sql`; `NTLM` or `KERBEROS` is `windows`; anything else fails). `normalize_json` takes that value and requires the document's `auth` to equal it before writing `<auth>`, and `json_says_where_the_run_went_and_how_long_it_took` compares against it in place of accepting either. A report of the other valid method now fails both. Checked 2026-11-14: both tests passed on `test` and `test2016`, debug and release, and with `server_auth` mapping `SQL` to `windows` (the mutation R63's acceptance names) both failed (`left: "sql"`, `right: "windows"`); reverted. The full live suite since the fixes ran on the Azure VM (§7, real network), not yet on the owner's servers.

**Other judgments:** independent execution of the exact digest-check segment in PowerShell 5.1/7 accepted matching bytes and refused empty/wrong digests. The REST digest identifies the uploaded archive under trusted GitHub metadata, not benign code from a compromised build. Token/header removal occurs before executing either binary and removes normal child inheritance, not every possible same-VM memory access. Valid signature plus the expected publisher CN is accepted as additional checking within release-run provenance. The UNISP_NAME acquisition needle is specific to Schannel in the pinned driver and avoids the SQL SSPI failure path; recheck on upgrade. Optional negotiates LoginOnly/Mandatory with an encryption-capable server, or NoEncryption on NotSupported. The driver uses only SCH_CREDENTIALS, supporting the documented 1809-generation client floor; this is not loss of SQL Server 2016 server support. Checking newer-Windows Schannel policy is a troubleshooting suggestion, not a reproduced cause or universal remedy. The Copilot instructions pointer is accepted.

**Independent standard verification:** scripts/check.ps1 passed on its first permitted run in **100 s**: locked metadata, fmt, mocked AppVeyor step, Clippy default/timing, **510 unit, 127 debug process and 121 release process tests**, locked build and size/import checks. Exe **3,105,792 bytes**, **39,936 below 3 MiB**, same ten approved in-box imports. Log: artifacts/review-r59-r61-check.log. Additional mock source: scratch/review-r59-r61-extra.ps1. No production/test/workflow implementation was changed.

**Limits:** no signed-in AppVeyor browser/logs, hosted release assets/jobs, owner profile/credential, real service or SQL Server was accessed. AppVeyor builds 4/6/9 and release runs 10/11/12 remain supplied evidence. Neither owner live suite nor real Credential Manager tests were independently rerun. The brief's LIVE_RESULTS placeholder supplies no current owner-server result. Primary documentation links, detailed judgments and evidence boundaries are in the full review. ### R62 and R63 fix verification and release-environment review (7e4f340..e474ff6)

November 14, 2026. Reviewed main at e474ff6 with a clean starting tree. **R62 and R63 independently closed. Test adjustments accepted; one new P3 documentation finding, R64, remains open. No new runtime defect or P1/P2.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-r62-r63-verification.md. No reviewer commit, push, live SQL or hosted/client/cloud action.

**R62 closed:** the failed hop's Schannel acquisition error precedes its LOGIN7 in strict and non-strict modes; the new wording no longer claims no server contact. The error is not a plaintext-fallback instruction. Optional's NoEncryption negotiation is a different path, not recovery from this acquisition failure. The statement concerns that failed login, not every earlier AG hop, all Windows credential activity or the entire password lifetime. All six unit combinations passed. **R63 closed:** the separate session's CSV auth_scheme is independent of the connection metadata field, maps SQL/NTLM/KERBEROS to the supported categories and rejects everything else. With a stable profile it is a sound category oracle, not proof both sessions used identical tickets/routes. An independent exact-source probe with mocked CSV verified every mapping, rejection of an unknown scheme and failure on the opposite valid auth value before normalization. Source/evidence: scratch/review-r62-r63-oracle.py, artifacts/review-r62-r63-oracle.json. Developer real-server mutation runs remain supplied evidence.

**R64 [P3], open:** DESIGN.md §5.1's vector row, driver-0.4.0.md §10 item 8 and tests/cli.rs:13415 say Microsoft documents no float16 binary transport yet. Current primary documentation explicitly describes native support in JDBC 13.4 and ODBC 18.7.1: [vector compatibility](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/data-types/vector-data-type?view=sql-server-ver17), [half-precision driver support](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/data-types/vector-data-type-half-precision-float?view=sql-server-ver17#driver-support). The 17.0.1155/sqlh/mssql-tds 0.4.0 observation remains evidence of that tested combination, not universal transport absence; requested V2 does not establish the negotiated acknowledgment or the cause. **Acceptance:** correct/remove the external assertion in spec/note/comment, retain dated build-specific evidence and the contract for both native rendering and unchanged down-level server text/type. No driver change or new live investigation is required. **Fixed 2026-11-14 (P3, docs and a test comment; self-verified under decision 52, for the next brief):** the claim came from a search summary, not the page; the half-precision page (updated 2026-09-29) was read and confirms the review. DESIGN.md §5.1's row now says build 17.0.1155 sent float16 down-level to sqlh, which asks for version 2, and that the acknowledged version and the cause aren't visible outside the driver; driver-0.4.0.md §10 item 8 says the same, cites the Driver support section (JDBC 13.4, ODBC 18.7.1) and that an earlier wording said the opposite; the test comment drops the claim; decision 71's note and §7's mssql-tds line are scoped to that build too. The contract is unchanged: native float16 in sqlh's own form, a down-level form as the server wrote it.

**Remaining judgments:** accepting either exact vector form is appropriate for a contract permitting both; native decoder/half formatting remains pinned by fake-server/exhaustive tests, and no arbitrary header can pass the exact down-level checks. The smaller live output budget reduces the demonstrated WAN throughput requirement but is not literally throughput-free: about 500 rows must still arrive before the deadline. This case no longer tests the identical default-1-MB end-to-end scenario. Existing Tally/default limits and count/deadline/hidden-tail tests retain component coverage; a deterministic combined default-budget scenario, if needed, belongs against the fake server with a real sink and controlled timing. The older unrecorded 2016 flake properly remains open.

**Release evidence judgment:** the supplied fresh Windows 11 Sandbox run supports the no-install/import compatibility gate on that image, and the Azure VM supports the real remote-network/mandatory/strict/reuse gate. ### R64 verification and decision 77 review (e474ff6..7d89b4e)

November 14, 2026. Reviewed main at 7d89b4e, clean starting tree. **R64 independently closed; decision 77's product timing change accepted. R65 [P2] and R66 [P3] remain in the new process test.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-r64-decision77.md. No implementation changes, commit or push by the reviewer.

- **R65 [P2], open: test can delete a real sqlh:local credential.** The new the_connection_test_after_profiles_add_times_only_itself runs profiles add local with a scratch SQLH_CONFIG. A successful Windows-auth add unconditionally calls store.delete_password(name) (profile.rs:307-308), and System uses the actual Credential Manager target sqlh:local. The file override does not isolate that namespace. A real unrelated local profile can lose its saved password while its real profiles file stays untouched. **Acceptance:** use an unmistakably synthetic, process/test-unique profile name and constrain every credential operation to the reserved test namespace; verify delete targeting through a non-destructive fake Store/seam. Do not reproduce by creating/deleting a real local credential. No product deletion behavior change is requested.
- **Review-run disclosure:** the standard check was launched before the reviewer traced that side effect. Its log confirms the new test ran and passed in debug and release, thus reaching both deletion attempts. The reviewer attempted to stop the verified check process after identifying the issue, but it had already completed. Whether sqlh:local previously existed or was affected is unverified; no Credential Manager inspection, recreation or further local-name product probe was performed. The suite's passing result is not evidence of harmless user-state isolation.
- **R66 [P3], open: release sleep precedes the test stopwatch.** The three-second sleeper is spawned before started is captured. A parent scheduling pause can consume the hold before the measured child run begins, making elapsed >= hold fail on correct behavior; a release before the child reaches the lock also defeats the intended pre-test delay. **Acceptance:** synchronize a delay actually incurred by the child or use a controlled seam; parent delay must neither create a false failure nor make the old process-start clock pass. Moving only the outer stopwatch earlier does not prove the child waited. This is a source-proven legal interleaving; no additional product reproduction was run after finding R65.
- **R65 and R66 fixed 2026-11-14 (self-verified under decision 52, for the next brief; R65 is P2, so the brief asks for its check):**
  - **Was anything lost here?** `cmdkey /list:sqlh:*` (target names only, no secrets) shows only the owner's four, `sqlh:trial`, `sqlh:test2016`, `sqlh:test` and `sqlh:az`, and there was never a `local` profile, so the runs deleted nothing of the owner's; on another machine they could have.
  - **R65:** the test's profile is `sqlh-ut-add-timing-<pid>`, in the `sqlh-ut-` namespace the other process tests use, now a rule in AGENTS.md (Tests). A new unit test, `a_windows_auth_add_deletes_only_its_own_password`, has the fake Store record every delete and pins that a Windows-auth add of a new profile asks to delete only its own name's password, another profile's untouched. The other three process tests that pass a plain name to `profiles add` or `rm` (`dev`, `nothing`, `probe`) are refused before Credential Manager: an `-S` refusal, an `SQLH_CONFIG` refusal and the mintty prompt refusal, none reading or writing a password. After the fixed runs, `cmdkey` lists the same four targets.
  - **R66:** the lock and the releaser thread are gone. `SQLH_DEBUG_ADD_HOLD_MS`, debug builds only (`profile::run`'s Add arm, before `add`), makes the child wait inside its own process after its start, in place of the prompt, so no scheduling of the test process moves it and the old process-start clock always counts it; the test is `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` (AGENTS.md). With a 4 s hold, the fix passes; with `test(name, started)` restored it failed, `5025 ms counts the wait`. The lock wait is no longer exercised by a test: like the prompt, it comes before `add` returns, ahead of the new clock. AGENTS.md's and DESIGN.md §12's debug-variable lists name it. `scripts/check.ps1` passed: 511 unit, 128 debug and 121 release process tests (the test is debug-only now), exe unchanged at 3,105,792 bytes.

**Judgments:** timing after the successful save and output flush/drop correctly measures the automatic connection test alone; immediately after prompting would still include credential/file writes. Failed adds should keep process time, since they report the edit's failure and no connection test ran. A post-save connection failure properly uses the new test time. List/rm/plain profiles test remain unchanged. The changed Instant feeds reporting/closing only, not login/query deadlines or connect N ms. A controlled lock wait is a valid stand-in for the excluded prompt path, but the present fixture needs R65/R66 fixed before further runs on a user account. R64's spec/note/comment now scope the vector observation to build 17.0.1155 and mssql-tds 0.4.0, distinguish requested from acknowledged V2 and remove the general no-binary-transport claim; the output contract is unchanged.

### Decision 79 external review (b95ba91..a4a764c)

November 14, 2026. Independently reviewed main at `a4a764c`, clean starting tree. **One new finding, R67 [P2], open; the forward-name selection and bounded provider design are otherwise accepted.** R65 and R66 remain closed; the next finding is R68. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-decision79.md`. No release gate checkbox changed.

- **R67 [P2]: canonical-name decoding can exit the whole process.** The new `forward_canonical` iteration (`src/db/spn.rs:150-153`) reaches dns-lookup 2.1.1's `AddrInfo::from_ptr`, which calls `CStr::to_str().unwrap()` on the ANSI WinSock `ai_canonname`. A non-UTF-8 result panics before `.flatten()` or the original-host fallback can handle it. sqlh's panic hook calls `process::exit(10)` on any thread; a late lookup can terminate an MCP server even after its caller already timed out. The Windows [ANSI API/result contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/ws2def/ns-ws2def-addrinfoa) does not promise UTF-8. **Independent probe:** copied the cached dependency unchanged into ignored scratch and appended a synthetic `AddrInfo::from_ptr` test with valid socket metadata: ASCII succeeds; `caf\xe9.example` reproduces its Utf8Error panic at line 156. The normal dependency test harness catches that expected panic; sqlh's process-exit consequence is source-traced. No actual DNS result with those bytes or end-to-end sqlh crash was reproduced. An initial pointer-mutability harness compile error was corrected before the successful probe. **Acceptance:** a correctly or fallibly decoded Windows canonical-name boundary, with an undecodable answer returning the original-host fallback instead of panicking; deterministic conversion/fallback coverage including invalid bytes, while retaining the budget, cap, late-worker ownership and SPN/port/channel-binding behavior. `catch_unwind` around this call does not bypass sqlh's exit hook. Non-ASCII support may be deferred; graceful boundary failure must still hold. No real DNS or owner credential probe is needed.
- **R67 fixed 2026-11-14 (P2; for the next brief, which must check it):** the forward lookup no longer goes through dns-lookup. `src/dns.rs` (new, an FFI module: AGENTS.md's `unsafe` list names it) calls WinSock's `GetAddrInfoW`, the wide API, with `AI_CANONNAME`, frees the list through a guard, and decodes the first entry's `ai_canonname` with `String::from_utf16`, so an answer that isn't valid UTF-16 (an unpaired surrogate), a null or empty name, or one with no terminator within 1,024 units is `None`, and `db::spn::canonical_name` keeps the name as given; nothing in the path unwraps. Host names reach Windows as UTF-16 too, not through the ANSI code page. WinSock is started once (`WSAStartup` 2.2, never cleaned up) so the function stands on its own. `db::spn` keeps the selection rule, the 500 ms budget, the cap of 4, the late lookup's slot, loopback and `make_spn` unchanged; its lookup is now `crate::dns::canonical_name`. Tests: `dns::tests::a_canonical_name_decodes_or_is_no_answer` (ASCII and accented names decode; null, empty, a lone surrogate and an unterminated buffer are `None`) and `a_lookup_that_cannot_answer_is_no_answer` (a NUL in the name is refused before the call; `sqlh-no-such-host.invalid` gives `None`), beside the eight `db::spn` tests. The direct `dns-lookup` dependency is gone (the driver still uses it); `windows-sys` 0.59 gains `Win32_Networking_WinSock`. `scripts/check.ps1` passed: 514 unit, 128 debug and 121 release process tests, exe 3,106,304 bytes (2,048 less than with dns-lookup), the same ten imports.

**Judgments:** CNAME canonicalization is accepted with DNS as an explicit identity-selection trust boundary; Kerberos proves the selected principal, not ownership of the user's alias. Source-traced checked TLS still validates the current hop's transport name, not the canonical SPN name: `network_transport` gives that name to `SslHandler`, which passes it to Schannel before the provider runs; sqlh sets no certificate-name override. The domain trial used `-C`, so it does not test that protection. Claim SqlClient parity only for the supplied domain/account/name results, not its unread internal mechanism. A dotted-name lookup can add up to the existing 500 ms per-hop wait under DNS/cap trouble even after TCP resolved that name, and the login deadline may expire; a free canonical cache hit is not guaranteed. Healthy reused MCP connections do not redo login lookups on each call. This bounded cost is acceptable. Keep the raw-host fallback, not another forward/PTR fallback that reintroduces U13. Keep injected policy and fake-server tests; add deterministic decode-boundary coverage for R67 rather than a required external-DNS unit test. Domain machine-account results do not establish interactive-user, domain named-instance/routed, hosts-only/NetBIOS-only or non-ASCII interoperability.

**Independent standard check:** `scripts/check.ps1` passed without retries in **98 s**: locked metadata, fmt, AppVeyor mocks, Clippy default/timing, **512 unit, 128 debug process and 121 release process tests**, locked build and size/import checks. Name-selection, capped workers and fake routing/cancel/runtime-progress tests passed. Exe **3,108,352 bytes**, **37,376 below 3 MiB**, same ten in-box imports; direct dns-lookup adds no locked package, and its MIT/Apache-2.0 license is compatible. Evidence: `artifacts/review-decision79-check.log`, `artifacts/review-decision79-dns-probe.log` and `.json`, synthetic source under `scratch/review-decision79-dns`. Domain/Kerberos/SqlClient/klist and signed/local-build results remain supplied evidence. No owner profiles/credentials, SQL live suite, DNS/domain/cloud configuration, interactive logon, hosted CI, workflow or signed artifact was independently accessed or changed. Only ignored review/probe/artifacts and PLAN.md/AGENTS.md status changed; no tracked implementation or dependency edit, commit or push.

### R67 fix verification (a4a764c..a4fec67)

November 14, 2026. Independently reviewed main at `a4fec67`; the starting tree had an existing AGENTS.md review-status edit, incorporated here. **R67 [P2] closed; no new findings.** The next is R68. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-r67-verification.md`. No release checklist checkbox changed.

**Acceptance:** the forward-name provider now calls `dns::canonical_name` through the wide Windows API. NUL input, lookup failure, absent/empty/overlong names and malformed UTF-16 return `None`, retaining the original-host fallback, without the old dependency conversion unwrap. Source-traced address/PTR conversion returns an error on invalid UTF-8 rather than unwrapping. The 500 ms shared deadline, four-worker cap, late-worker slot ownership, loopback rule, SPN/hop port, package and channel bindings are unchanged. Closure concerns resolver-encoding failure, not allocation failure or arbitrary broken OS pointers; complete internationalized-hostname interoperability is not claimed.

**Judgments:** the first entry is correct for `AI_CANONNAME`; the result remains owned until decoding ends and `List` frees the wide list once. The scan is sound under Windows' [NUL-terminated canonical-string contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/ws2tcpip/nf-ws2tcpip-getaddrinfow) and the unsafe caller's readable-buffer promise. Its 1,024-unit cap limits work; it cannot make a shorter unterminated allocation or invalid pointer safe. The deliberately readable synthetic no-NUL buffer meets the test contract. One process-lifetime WSAStartup reference is acceptable for this exe, with no per-lookup growth; Microsoft generally requires matched startup/cleanup, so this is an intentional lifetime policy, not a general exemption or a reusable-DLL design. Do not add per-lookup cleanup to shared cached initialization. Top-level `dns` is a suitable FFI boundary with no driver types, explicitly permitted by the reviewed AGENTS.md. No new crate or runtime DLL. The real `.invalid` negative test is resolver smoke, not hermetic timing evidence or a guarantee against every local name-provider override.

**Independent checks:** `scripts/check.ps1` passed without retries in **99 s**: locked metadata, fmt, AppVeyor mocks, Clippy default/timing, **514 unit, 128 debug process and 121 release process tests**, locked build and size/import checks. New decoder/negative tests and existing provider/routing/cancel/runtime-progress checks passed. An isolated probe with the new implementation unchanged survived null and invalid UTF-16 under an exit-10-on-panic hook, decoded ASCII/accented/surrogate-pair controls, checked the last allowed terminator and readable no-NUL bound, and completed **100 numeric 127.0.0.1 GetAddrInfoW/free cycles** without external DNS. This checks normal native allocation/free flow, not instrumented allocation failure. Exe **3,106,304 bytes**, **39,424 below 3 MiB**, same ten approved in-box imports. Evidence: `artifacts/review-r67-check.log`, `artifacts/review-r67-wide-probe.log` and `.json`; source `scratch/review-r67-wide`.

**Pending/supplied evidence:** the brief still marks this build's domain run pending; the reviewer requested its results and did not assume success. Earlier decision 79 domain/SqlClient/klist/profile results are supplied evidence for the earlier build, not this one. Final signed/domain checks and the other release gates remain open. No live SQL, owner profiles/passwords/Credential Manager, interactive user/domain login, DNS/hosts/cloud configuration, hosted CI, workflow or signed artifact was independently accessed or changed. Only ignored review/probe/artifacts and these status records changed; no tracked implementation/dependency edit, commit or push.

### Dependabot's PRs (from 2026-11-14)

The owner's review request, after `.github/dependabot.yml` arrived with PR #1. Open ones, and the upstream watch's open driver issues (below), are checked for at the start of a session and before a release or tag, and each PR is recorded here when it is decided (AGENTS.md, Repository housekeeping; owner, 2026-11-27, after #11 waited six days unseen). No issue is opened for one: the PR is its own record. Each upstream changelog was read; none changes sqlh's code, so each is self-verified under decision 52 and listed in the next brief.

- **#2 serde_json 1.0.151 → 1.0.154, merged.** 152–154: `-0` and an f32 comparison in `arbitrary_precision`, the first bad byte in an invalid-UTF-8 error, `preserve_order` under `no_std`; none touches what `sqlh mcp` uses (`Value`, `json!`). CI `check`, size budget included, passed.
- **#3 log 0.4.34 → 0.4.35, merged on its `check`.** Upstream says the release has no source changes (CI rework); the crate is built only with `--features timing`.
- **#4 windows-sys 0.59 → 0.61.2, closed.** It doesn't compile (`BOOL` left `Win32::Foundation`), and sqlh stays on the version mssql-tds uses (0.59; tokio, mio and socket2 use 0.61). `dependabot.yml` now ignores `windows-sys` and `mssql-tds`: both are bumped by hand, the driver after its changelog is read (Stack).
- **#5 tokio 1.53.2 → 1.54.1, held for the live suites, then merged (2026-11-14).** A broad runtime release: among its changes, `spawn_blocking` (decision 72's SPN lookups use the driver's blocking pool), `Notify` orderings, timer-wheel and interval fixes (`-t`, the 250 ms count), retries on `Interrupted`; 1.54.1 only reverts 1.54.0's timer-handle change. CI can't reach those paths against a server, so `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` ran on `main` (`fc029a7`) with exactly #5's lock entry, in a worktree: both servers passed, each 514 unit, 128 debug and 121 release process, 87 debug and 80 release live tests. Exe 3,110,912 bytes (4,608 more than at `bdf6b24`, serde_json's 1.0.154 included), 34,816 within the 3 MiB budget. A local `cargo update -p tokio` would also have moved four crates' `windows-sys` (errno, rustix, socket2, tempfile); Dependabot's PR changes tokio's entry alone, which is what was tested.
- **#11 zeroize 1.9.1 → 1.9.2 (opened 2026-11-21, found 2026-11-27), merged.** `Cargo.lock` only. zeroize wipes sqlh's copies of a password (`Secret`, `credman.rs`'s byte buffer; F14), so its changes were read in the source, not only the changelog. 1.9.2 (upstream PRs 1592, 1602–1604, 1607): on targets with stable `asm!`, x86_64 among them, a slice of `Copy` values is now wiped by `fill` followed by `optimization_barrier`, an `asm!` block given the slice's pointer with `readonly` (not `nomem`), so the compiler must assume it reads that memory and keeps the writes before it; 1.9.1 wrote each element volatile. A `Vec<u8>`'s elements, which `Zeroizing<String>` and `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` wipe, are still written one volatile write each (`IterMut`), its spare capacity through the new byte path; `credman::wipe` is sqlh's own. GHSA-pfv9-jv8r-j7rg, fixed by 1592, is in the fallback barrier for targets without `asm!`, not ours. MSRV 1.85. Brought up to `main` (`gh pr update-branch`, `f8e580b`) and checked on that tree in a scratch worktree: `scripts/check.ps1` passed in 438 s (542 unit, 132 debug and 125 release process tests), exe 3,139,072 bytes, 1,536 smaller than `main`'s, ten in-box imports; the four Credential Manager tests (`cargo test -- --ignored credman`: the round trip, a non-UTF-8 password, a legacy credential's move, another server's password not sent) passed. No live run: zeroize touches no connection or row path. Merged on the owner's word (2026-11-27) as `abc1e08`, after a last update onto `main` (`30dcc2c`, still `Cargo.lock` alone), whose CI `check` passed.

### Upstream watch (owner, 2026-11-27)

The owner asked for a nightly check of crates.io that files an issue when the driver has a new version, since Dependabot ignores `mssql-tds` (above, #4). GitHub has no other signal: upstream publishes no GitHub releases (its only tags are `archive/*`), and its `CHANGELOG.md` keeps one `[Unreleased]` section, not one per version (both read 2026-11-27), so a release shows only as a crates.io version and an upstream commit "Bump released TDS crates to x.y.z (#n)".

- **Built:** `.github/workflows/upstream-watch.yml`, daily at 06:23 UTC and by dispatch (`dry_run` prints instead of filing), on `ubuntu-latest` with `contents: read` and `issues: write`, no secret, nothing of sqlh's built or run, one run at a time. It runs `scripts/upstream-watch.ps1`: the exact pin from `Cargo.toml` (anything but one `mssql-tds = "=x.y.z"` fails the run), the version list from the crates.io sparse index (`index.crates.io/ms/sq/mssql-tds`), and then at most two issues, labelled `dependencies`: `mssql-tds <v> published` for the newest non-yanked release above the pin (prereleases ignored), and `mssql-tds <pin>, the pinned driver, was yanked`. A title already used by any issue, open or closed, is never filed again. A published issue links crates.io, the crate diff on diff.rs, upstream's compare between the two bump commits (or the driver's commit history if either isn't found) and the upgrade's steps as for 0.4.0. Versions and SHAs are checked against `^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$` and 40 hex digits before they reach an issue.
- **Tests:** `scripts/upstream-watch.tests.ps1`, run by `scripts/check.ps1`: the pin's parsing, the newest-release and yank decisions, the bump-commit match (no prefix match, no malformed SHA) and the bodies, against fixed inputs. A local `-DryRun` on 2026-11-27 read the real index and repository: it would file `mssql-tds 0.5.0 published` with the compare `a04e227...6a4b88d`.
- **Kept in mind:** GitHub disables a public repository's scheduled workflows after 60 days without activity and mails whoever last changed the cron line, as it does a failed run. Self-verified under decision 52 (a workflow that files issues, holding no release or signing secret); listed in the next brief.
- **0.5.0 itself** (crates.io 2026-11-25) waits for the issue the first run files; its analysis comes later, at the owner's word.

### Going public (decision 91; checks run 2026-11-22; public since 2026-11-27, decision 98)

The repository goes public with 0.2.0, slice 2, the release after 0.1.0 (decisions 91 and 96; planned for 2026-11-27). The owner asked for the checks before the launch post (planned for 2026-12-01). Each check was run on 2026-11-22 against the repository and its GitHub settings, without changing either.

- **History: passed.** 388 commits were scanned: `main`, every branch on GitHub, the 11 PR heads (`refs/pull/*`, which go public with the repository), `v0.1.0` and `phase6-slice2`. Both added and removed lines were searched for private network names and addresses, employer names, personal email domains, local user paths, and token and key shapes. Nothing was found. Every `PASSWORD` hit is test SQL or code. The deallocated Azure check VM's internal DNS names in the driver notes are left in place. One unit test in `src/output/mod.rs` uses a real user's profile path, which a synthetic `C:\Users\me` would replace (P3). This follows decision 80's 2026-11-14 audit, which covered 352 commits.
- **Refs: passed.** GitHub holds `main`, `public-readiness`, one Dependabot branch, PR heads 1–11 and `v0.1.0`. The local-only refs (`drain-prototype`, `driver-0.3.0`, `driver-0.4.0`, `decision-56`, the agent checkpoints, and `refs/codex/`, which still holds review files) never go there. Never push with `--mirror` or `--all`.
- **Workflows: passed.** `ci.yml` runs on `pull_request`, not `pull_request_target`, with read-only permissions, so an outside PR gets no secrets. `release.yml`'s sign, verify and publish jobs run only for the owner's immutable ID, in the `release` environment (R22).
- **Author email: the owner's call.** Every commit carries the owner's own email. Rewriting history isn't possible because `v0.1.0` can't move. The options are to keep the email, or to use GitHub's noreply address for later commits.
- **GitHub settings, to change at the flip.** Turn on private vulnerability reporting, which SECURITY.md relies on, plus secret scanning with push protection and Dependabot alerts. Decide whether issues come on. Require approval before workflows run for all outside collaborators, not only first-time ones. Optionally, require actions pinned to full SHAs (they already are), and move the three `AZURE_*` identifiers from repository secrets to the `release` environment.
- **Docs read by a stranger: failed, six blockers.**
  1. README says "pre-release".
  2. README says releases "will be" built and distributed.
  3. There are no install steps for the downloaded zip: `SHA256SUMS.txt`, PATH, SmartScreen.
  4. CONTRIBUTING.md refuses public issues "until after its first release".
  5. SECURITY.md says "pre-release" and points reporters at "an existing private contact method"; it also needs a supported-versions line.
  6. The README describes slice 2's commands, which 0.1.0 lacks. The README has to match the release a stranger downloads, so ship slice 2 first or mark those commands as next.

  Should fix:
  - Define "harness" and MCP where they first appear.
  - Put what it is, install and a first try on the first screen.
  - Cut "Built for agents" to a short list and move the full one to a doc. That changes this file's tally rule in AGENTS.md too.
  - Fold the guard's single-function refusals into one line.
  - "The tools only read" should say they run under the guard.
  - Keep one requirements list.
  - Move local housekeeping to CONTRIBUTING.
  - Rewrite `v0.1.0`'s release notes, which are internal PR titles.

  Agents fix the mechanical parts. The owner writes the README's opening and the release notes.

  **Fixed 2026-11-26, for 0.2.0 (decision 96), docs only:**
  - The README now has a status line instead of "pre-release", a requirements list, install steps (the zip, `SHA256SUMS.txt`, PATH, the signature, SmartScreen), and "Build from source" after the documents. "Will be built" is gone.

The tags, assets and signatures are unchanged. The README's opening: reviewed by the owner on 2026-11-27 and kept as it is ("I'm fine with the way the README opening is for now"). **Done 2026-11-27, docs only:**
