### Phase 6 slice 2 external review (682f035..b852ce6)

November 26, 2026. Independently reviewed local `phase6-slice2` at `b852ce6`, clean starting tree. Range includes main's post-0.1.0 dependency/doc changes. **R69 [P2] should be fixed before 0.2.0; R70 [P3] can wait for 0.2.1.** Next finding: R71. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-phase6-slice2.md`. No implementation fix, budget exception, commit, push, PR, merge, tag or public-switch action was performed.

- **R69 [P2], open: an expired second-connect deadline can return success.** `db::once_more_if_port_held` (`src/db/mod.rs:859-869`) constructs/polls the retry in `timeout_at` without testing the wall clock first. Pinned Tokio polls the inner future before the timeout. An immediately ready result can win after the shared deadline. Independent replay of the exact production helper/recognizer with minimal equivalent driver error enums and pinned Tokio 1.54.1: deadline one second past, first IO 10048, second ready `Ok(7)` gives `Ok(7); attempts 2`. No socket/real late login was used. Existing pending-second-attempt coverage misses it; the driver's first-attempt timer does not prove completion before sqlh's separate deadline. **Acceptance:** reject expiry before constructing/polling the retry and reject a successful second completion past the clock boundary, using the existing login timeout/exit 4. Add expired ready-success/ready-error, boundary-crossing completion and in-time controls; retain `-l 0`, cancellation and the one-retry rule. Reuse the existing timeout construction and a clock predicate rather than adding a crate/variant. No fix size was measured; remeasure, and follow decision 87 if it exceeds the remaining 1,536 bytes.
- **R70 [P3], open, eligible for 0.2.1: 10048's hint overstates its cause.** `connect_error` (`src/db/mod.rs:1704-1709`) and the corresponding specs say Windows twice chose a port held by a closed connection, with a two-minute lifetime. Microsoft's [connect contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winsock2/nf-winsock2-connect) establishes local address already in use, not a uniquely identified TIME_WAIT cause. The 1,411 TIME_WAIT ports counted after the run do not establish the conflicting tuple/state at failure or exhaustion of the 16,384-port range. **Acceptance:** word the hint as a local socket-address conflict on both attempts, with wait/retry/local TCP investigation advice; retain TIME_WAIT as a possible explanation or hypothesis in the records. A shorter replacement can save string bytes. The narrow retry itself remains acceptable; this is not a security/replay correctness blocker for the tag.

**Judgments:** generated search quoting is sound for valid Rust strings: doubled ASCII quotes in an N-literal, C0/NUL refused, up to 500 scalar values fitting 1,000 UTF-16 units. DEL/C1 remain data, so the phrase "no control characters" means the implemented C0 policy. CLI/JSON cannot supply a Rust string with unpaired surrogate code points. Variable-looking text is not substituted. The checked since parser emits only its own numeric/date expressions. Only the declared integer archive/type go to `sp_readerrorlog`; `sp_enumerrorlogs` separately takes its integer type. Independently tested 432 admitted hostile/Unicode searches against unchanged extracted builder functions: their nonliteral SQL shape stayed fixed, and C0/cap/date controls passed. This was a lexical probe, not SQL execution.

Tests of the actual built one-SELECT steps suffice for this construction invariant; a blanket runtime assertion would wrongly reject legitimate multi-section generated commands. Brief-count stopping can omit hidden rows but no later statement's output here, so ordinary truncation/count information is sufficient without GO/partial advice. JSON's existing optional free-form notes array can receive the empty-section note under contract 2.1; rows stay `[]`. Per-batch set/take clears note and empty-section state, and MCP creates a new sink per call. The release's 0.2.0 bump is appropriate for new flags/tools/columns.

The activity walk's seen-session path, 100-hop stop and DISTINCT descendant count handle the documented cycle/depth policy; its 1,000-byte path fits the bounded positive smallint IDs. Zero-clamping computed ages handles racing DMV observations. Materialization is not an atomic server snapshot, and a hop bound does not bound total sessions/APPLY work. Many-session performance remains unmeasured. Whole error-log archives still cost tempdb/CPU even with search/row limits; since narrows archives only. Client timeout/retirement is not proof every server-side extended-procedure operation instantly stops. Keep the large-log measurement gap visible. Permission checks/refusals match the scoped versions (errorlog: VIEW SERVER STATE/securityadmin before 2022, also VIEW ANY ERROR LOG after; activity: VIEW SERVER PERFORMANCE STATE after 2022, else STATE). `HAS_PERMS_BY_NAME` reports effective permissions, not merely explicit grants; server_permissions is a selected summary, not an exhaustive authorization certificate under every DENY/configuration combination.

The engine activity flag changes pool-wait wording only; no reserved or prioritized connection is introduced. CLI fallback is an acceptable decision-88 tradeoff, not a promise of MCP-only emergency availability. Dead-idle replacement remains before call work; its new connection can use decision 95's bounded pair, without replaying the user's query. R69 is the remaining deadline issue. The -M text match is appropriately narrow for the pinned driver, but wording-dependent and describes the last address error. Client contexts/password Strings are built serially per attempt without a retained cloned context; failed-driver strings remain unwiped under the disclosed F14 limitation. No new secret formatting/logging was found. U14/U15 distinguish measured/source claims and retain their limitations. Public docs appropriately state the guard is not a security boundary and distinguish server versions from the validated Windows client. Prefer Authenticode verification before running --version; no policy-bypass instruction was found. Public issues/security settings and final certification remain the owner's actual release checks.

**Unverified follow-up, not a numbered finding:** activity slices st.text using byte-offset/2 positions before applying BIN2 to the whitespace normalization. A source-derived model under SC semantics shifts `/* 🙂 */ SELECT 123 AS marker;` to `ELECT...`, since SC SUBSTRING counts pairs once. The actual DMV result collation/reachability was not established, so no product reproduction or tag blocker is claimed. Check with synthetic SQL without changing an owner's server collation; if reachable, collate the SUBSTRING operand to non-SC BIN2 before slicing. Large-archive metadata capacity is also unverified; no overflow claim is made from secondary examples.

**Independent checks:** `scripts/check.ps1` passed first run, no retries, in **100 s**: locked metadata, fmt, AppVeyor mocks, Clippy default/timing, **542 unit, 132 debug process and 125 release process tests**, locked build, size and ten in-box imports. Exe **3,144,192 bytes**, **1,536 below 3 MiB**. New command/note/MCP tests and numeric/scanning regressions passed; six unit and live tests remain ignored. Exact-helper replay proves R69; the 432-case extracted-function probe proves its stated lexical controls; the SC model is not SQL execution. Evidence: `artifacts/review-slice2-check.log`, `review-slice2-deadline.log`, `review-slice2-inputs.log`, `review-slice2-boundaries.json`; source under ignored `scratch/review-slice2-deadline` and `review-slice2-inputs`. Developer live passes, internal reviews/mutations, server measurements and the TIME_WAIT observation remain supplied evidence. No branch CI exists. No independent owner live/credential tests, actual 10048 connect, SQL/domain/Agent-log run, AppVeyor, signing or publication. Only ignored review/probes/artifacts and PLAN.md/AGENTS.md review records changed.

**R69 and R70 fixes and the follow-up (2026-11-26, on `phase6-slice2` after `f338129`; for the reviewer's fix verification).**
- **R69, fixed:** `db::once_more_if_port_held` checks the clock before the second attempt, which isn't made once the deadline has passed, and after it ends, so an attempt that ends past the deadline, a connection included (dropped, which closes it), is the login timeout, exit 4; the timer still cuts one that is running. Both checks take the deadline as reached at `at` (`>=`), as tokio's timer does. Unit `db::tests::a_connect_refused_a_held_port_is_tried_once_more` adds the reviewer's cases, with deadlines 500 ms off so a pause of the test's thread can't reach them first: still running at the deadline (the timer); ending in its first poll once the deadline has passed (it sleeps until just past it, so `Timeout` sees it ready before its timer), as a success, a 10061 and a 10048: each a timeout; in time, a success and a 10061 each stand; already past, a ready success and a ready 10061: a timeout with no second attempt. Against the timer-only version (the fix taken out by hand) the late success failed, and with the late check narrowed to successes the late 10061 did; with the fix, all pass. `-l 0` (no deadline) keeps its cases above; cancellation is unchanged, outside the helper (`Session::connect_with`'s `select!`). DESIGN.md §4.2's `-l` row says it.
- **R70, fixed now rather than in 0.2.1:** the hint is `this computer reported its local address in use, twice: wait a minute and try again; if it persists, check this computer's TCP connections` (`connect_error`; unit `a_held_port_is_this_computers_and_not_the_servers_name`; DESIGN.md §4.2, mcp-mode.md §4). The code's `WSAEADDRINUSE` comment and DESIGN.md §4.2 keep TIME_WAIT as the likely cause when it was seen, a hypothesis the error doesn't prove, and `client_context`'s comment no longer states it; the README's entry keeps its hedged "can pick a local port a closed connection still holds". Decision 95's text keeps the hint it was decided with.
- **The review's unverified follow-up, measured and fixed (for the reviewer to number):** `activity` cut the statement from `st.text` in its own collation, and `sys.dm_exec_describe_first_result_set` describes `st.text` as `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS` on both test servers, whose defaults are that collation too, so whether it follows a default or is fixed isn't told apart (R71's verification). By hand on both servers (`artifacts/slice2/sc-substring.log`, local: the SQL and its output): `/* 🙂 */ SELECT 1 AS marker;` cut at byte 18 gave `ELECT 1 AS marker;` under an explicit `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_SC`, and `SELECT 1 AS marker;` under `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS` and BIN2, and under `_SC` then BIN2. So under an `_SC` or `_UTF8` default, the server's or the database's, a statement after a surrogate pair began a character late; that is inferred, since no test server or database has such a default. `ACTIVITY_STEP` now cuts `st.text COLLATE Latin1_General_100_BIN2` (schema-commands.md §6.8, its SQL block and a bullet); unit `activitys_step_is_one_select_of_its_columns` asserts it, and `the_docs_show_activitys_sql_as_built` the docs' copy. No live test tells the two apart.
- **README:** the install steps check the signature before the first run of the exe (the review's judgment 9).
- **Internal review of these fixes (2026-11-26, not the reviewer; two lenses, each finding verified):** five P3s held, all fixed above: the 50 ms deadlines a paused test thread could reach (now 500 ms, and the late case sleeps past its own deadline); no late failure tested (now 10061 and 10048 as well as a success); `st.text`'s collation stated as the server's, where the measurement can't tell it from the database's; the measurement's record pointing at no file (now `artifacts/slice2/sc-substring.log`); `client_context`'s comment still stating the held port. Refused: the `>` / `>=` mismatch, unreachable but made `>=` anyway; the already-past cases' `checked_sub`, which can't skip on Windows; the README's hedged wording.
- **Size:** exe 3,145,216 bytes, +1,024 for all of it; **512 under the 3 MiB budget** (decision 87).
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` with every fix above (2026-11-26), both servers:** `test` passed in 413 s and `test2016` in 209 s, each with 542 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests, 95 debug and 88 release live tests, none failed or retried; in-box imports. A run before the internal review's fixes passed on both too.

### R69/R70 fix verification and the collation follow-up (b852ce6..cbab052)

November 26, 2026. Reviewed local `phase6-slice2`. The brief contained RANGE_END/CHECK_RESULT placeholders and testing began with uncommitted fixes over `f338129`. The developer committed `cbab052` during the review; implementation/Cargo hashes captured before testing match that commit's working tree. **R69's late-result defect and R70's hint are fixed. R71 [P3], the collation-safety follow-up, is accepted as fixed defensive offset handling, with deployment reachability still unverified. R72 [P2] is new and should be fixed before 0.2.0: a late retry can turn an observed cancellation into timeout.** Next finding: R73. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-r69-r70-verification.md`.

- **R69 accepted:** the clock is checked before retry construction and after completion, with the pending timer retained. Already expired work takes no second attempt; late success and ordinary late errors return the login timeout. A late owned success is dropped and never enters Session/engine. Exact-helper replay using pinned Tokio 1.54.1 and minimal equivalent driver error/result enums independently verified expiry with one attempt and late owned success dropped once. Real socket closure was source-traced, not separately exercised. The no-deadline/error-selection cases are unchanged. The developer's timer-only/success-only mutations are supplied evidence. The deadline is a classification boundary, not proof that a blocking poll/native call or scheduling pause physically returns by that instant; first-attempt driver behavior and pool/probe/setup scopes remain as before.
- **R70 independently closed:** the hint names the observed local-address conflict twice, gives wait/retry/local TCP investigation advice, and no longer asserts an owning closed connection or TIME_WAIT duration. The spec/comment label the historical cause as a hypothesis. Exact hint tests passed in both hint modes.
- **R71 [P3], accepted/fixed with qualified evidence:** applying `Latin1_General_100_BIN2` to st.text before SUBSTRING aligns its indexing with byte offsets divided by two and DATALENGTH. SQL-shape and docs/code-block tests passed. The sanitized supplied SC log was inspected: both servers describe DMF text as SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS; explicit SC gives ELECT after the emoji prefix, while non-SC/BIN2 controls give SELECT. No SQL was independently rerun. Those observations demonstrate expression sensitivity; they do not distinguish a fixed metadata rule from server/database-default inheritance on differently configured instances. Phrase that reachability/default-dependence claim as inference, not established fact. The defensive fix does not need that question resolved. No other new activity/errorlog byte-offset mismatch was found: input_buffer is unsliced, errorlog's CRLF removal uses character functions together, and the seen-path is ASCII numeric text. An SC-default deployment remains untested.
- **R72 [P2], open: preserve a returned driver cancellation at the late boundary.** `src/db/mod.rs:877` matches `Ok(_)`, including `Ok(Err(OperationCancelledError(...)))`, and replaces it with TimeoutError once the deadline passes. Cancellation maps to exit 6, timeout to exit 4. Session::connect_with's unbiased select at lines 707 onward can choose the connector result and immediately map it with `?`; its other cancel branch is not guaranteed to restore the discarded cancellation. **Independent regression probe:** first IO 10048, second future returns OperationCancelledError in its first poll after the deadline: exact old helper returns OperationCancelledError; exact new helper returns TimeoutError. Minimal equivalent enums were used, not a real Windows 10048 or Ctrl+Break login. Source tracing establishes possible exit 4; this is not a claim that every unrelated simultaneous cancel/timer race previously guaranteed exit 6. **Acceptance:** preserve structured driver cancellation before the generic late guard or enforce an observed user-cancel flag at the connect boundary. Add a controlled second-attempt cancellation crossing the deadline and the outer-race/policy check. Ordinary late success/10061/10048 must remain timeout; retain pending cancel, -l 0 and one retry. A small cancellation arm can reuse the error without new strings/crates/variants, but was not implemented or sized. With 512 bytes left, remeasure and follow decision 87 if it crosses the cap; no exception is granted here.

**Test/wording judgment:** 500 ms is practical headroom and passed this parallel run, not synchronization. A long enough pause after soon() can expire it before retry two, yielding a correct timeout with tries 1 where the test expects 2. The comment claiming a pause cannot consume it is too strong. No such failure was observed, so do not retry or loosen by reflex; deterministic clock/connector control is preferable for maintenance. R72 is the immediate missing acceptance case, since existing cancellation tests never take retry two. README now verifies Authenticode before executing the downloaded exe.

**Independent checks:** `scripts/check.ps1` passed first run, without retries, in **102 s**: locked metadata, fmt, AppVeyor mocks, Clippy default/timing, **542 unit, 132 debug process and 125 release process tests**, locked build, size and ten in-box imports. Six unit and live tests stayed ignored. Relevant R69/R70/SQL/docs tests, connect cancellation variants and fake SPN cancellation passed, with the retry limitation above. Exe **3,145,216 bytes**, **512 below 3 MiB**, up 1,024. Evidence: `artifacts/review-r69-r70-start.json`, `review-r69-r70-check.log`, `review-r69-r70-probe.log`; source `scratch/review-r69-r70-probe`. The supplied SC execution log and developer live results (95 debug/88 release on each owner server) remain supplied evidence. No independent live SQL, owner profiles/passwords/Credential Manager, actual 10048, OS cancellation during retry two, SC-default/domain/routed setup, hosted CI, signing or publication. Only ignored report/probe/artifacts and PLAN.md/AGENTS.md records changed by the reviewer; no production logic/test/dependency fix, commit, push, PR, merge, tag or visibility change.

**R72 fix and the verification's wording points (2026-11-26, on `phase6-slice2` after `92ebd97`; for the reviewer's fix verification).**
- **R72, fixed:** `once_more_if_port_held` returns a second attempt's `TdsError::OperationCancelledError` as it is, in an arm ahead of the late check, so a cancel stays a cancel, exit 6 (`connect_error` maps it to `DbError::Cancelled`), however late it ended; a late success, 10061 or 10048 is still the login timeout. The fix is in the helper, so the outer `select!` in `Session::connect_with` gives exit 6 whichever branch it takes: its cancel branch is `DbError::Cancelled`, and its connector branch now carries the driver's cancellation to `connect_error`. Unit `a_connect_refused_a_held_port_is_tried_once_more` adds a second attempt that ends cancelled once the deadline has passed, asserted through `connect_error` as `DbError::Cancelled` after two attempts; without the arm (taken out by hand) that case failed, and with it all pass. A pending second attempt's cancel, `-l 0` and the one retry are unchanged. Exe 3,145,216 bytes, unchanged (the arm fit the padding); 512 under the budget.
- **R71's wording:** schema-commands.md §6.8, `ACTIVITY_STEP`'s comment and the fix record above no longer say `st.text` takes a default collation: it is described as `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS` on both test servers, whose defaults are that collation too, so whether it follows a default or is fixed isn't told apart, and a real server reaching the shift is inference; the BIN2 cut is defensive.
- **The tests' margin:** the comment on the 500 ms deadlines says they are headroom, not synchronization, as the verification put it; no failure was seen, so the bound is unchanged.
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` with these changes (2026-11-26), both servers:** `test` passed in 407 s and `test2016` in 191 s, each with 542 unit tests (6 ignored), 132 debug and 125 release process tests, 95 debug and 88 release live tests, none failed or retried; exe 3,145,216 bytes; in-box imports.

### R72 fix verification (cbab052..4e19fcc)

November 26, 2026. Reviewed local `phase6-slice2`, clean at the start: `92ebd97` is the preceding review record and `4e19fcc` the fix. **R72 is fixed and closed. No new findings; next is R73.** R69/R70 remain accepted; R71's wording qualification is accepted. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-r72-verification.md`. This closes R72's review gate, not the separate release/publication checks.

- **R72 independently verified:** the helper preserves a returned `OperationCancelledError` before the late-result guard. The new repository case reaches retry two after IO 10048, returns cancellation after the deadline and asserts the real `connect_error` maps it to `DbError::Cancelled`. That and source inspection of Session's two select branches suffice for this narrow fix: the connector branch maps the returned cancellation, and the cancel branch directly returns Cancelled, which the run maps to exit 6. No new production connector seam is required. Ordinary late success/10061/10048 still time out; the pre-check, pending timeout, `-l 0` and one retry are unchanged. An offline exact-helper comparison with pinned Tokio 1.54.1 and minimal equivalent driver enums confirms `cbab052` returns timeout for the late cancellation while `4e19fcc` preserves it. Its R69 controls also pass: an expired deadline takes one attempt; a late owned success times out and drops the value exactly once. This is isolated helper evidence, not real Windows 10048, a real second connect or execution of Session's race. The developer's deletion-of-arm mutation remains supplied evidence.
- **Scope of the cancellation claim:** preserving a returned driver cancellation does not guarantee exit 6 for every cancellation during or after retry two. If the timer/pre-check supplies timeout before the driver returns cancellation, the unbiased select can choose that connector timeout even when its cancel branch is ready. A later cancel cannot retroactively change a terminal result. Those existing races are outside R72's specific loss of an already returned cancellation; no new global precedence guarantee is introduced.
- **Wording accepted:** schema-commands.md §6.8, ACTIVITY_STEP's comment and the fix record no longer claim the supplied measurements establish default inheritance. A real deployment reaching the SC shift remains inference; BIN2 is defensive and an SC-default instance remains untested. The 500 ms comment now says headroom, not synchronization; no load failure was observed and the bound is unchanged.
- **Independent checks:** `scripts/check.ps1` passed first run in **96 s**, no retries: locked metadata, fmt, AppVeyor mocks, Clippy default/timing, **542 unit, 132 debug process and 125 release process tests**, locked build, size and imports. Six unit and live tests stayed ignored. Exe **3,145,216 bytes**, unchanged, **512 below 3 MiB**, same ten in-box imports. This verifies artifact size, not the developer's linker-padding explanation. Evidence: `artifacts/review-r72-check.log`, `review-r72-probe.log`, `review-r72-snapshot.json`; probe source `scratch/review-r72-probe`. Implementation/Cargo hashes rechecked after testing. Developer live runs (95 debug/88 release on each owner server, 407/191 s) remain supplied evidence. No independent live SQL, owner credentials/Credential Manager, actual 10048, OS cancellation during retry two, SC-default deployment, hosted CI, signing or publication. Only ignored review/probe/artifacts and PLAN.md/AGENTS.md records changed by the reviewer; no implementation change, commit or push.

### Decision 97: the registry path and the CI size (2026-11-26, PR #12)

- **What failed:** PR #12's first CI run (`check`, run 45898217553, at `280c737`) failed at the size budget: 3,146,752 bytes, 1,024 over, where `scripts/check.ps1` passed locally at 3,145,216 on the same commit. AppVeyor's PR and branch statuses passed. The difference is CI's own: at `ede2980` CI measured 3,112,448 and the local build 3,111,424.
- **The cause:** the exe embedded 166 `.rs` paths, 17,795 bytes, most under the cargo registry (`<CARGO_HOME>\registry\src\index.crates.io-...\<crate>\src\...`), the panic locations of dependencies; the runner's user folder is longer. rustup's `std` paths are already remapped, and sqlh's own are relative.
- **The fix (decision 97):** `check.ps1` sets `CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_WINDOWS_MSVC_RUSTFLAGS` to `--remap-path-prefix=<registry>=crates` before its first cargo command, writes it to `GITHUB_ENV` when that is set, and after the size step fails if the exe holds the registry's path. Cargo merges it with `.cargo/config.toml`'s `+crt-static` (measured: no CRT DLL imported). `trim-paths` would do this in `Cargo.toml`, but isn't stable in cargo 1.99. `release.yml`, `ci.yml` and `appveyor.yml` are unchanged. A path with a space would be split by cargo, so the check refuses one; GitHub's runner has none.
- **The budget:** 3 MiB, for the unsigned exe `check.ps1` builds (DESIGN.md §11, `check.ps1`, AGENTS.md).
- **Measured, before the change:** a scratch build with the remap was 3,140,608 bytes, with the ten in-box imports and the user folder gone from it. The check's own results are below.
- **Internal review (2026-11-26, not the external reviewer; two lenses, each finding verified):** six held, fixed: the check first required `<CARGO_HOME>\registry\src` to exist, which a fresh GitHub runner fills only at step 1 (its image empties the registry), so CI, the release build and every Dependabot PR would have failed before any cargo command (P1, found by both lenses; the check is gone, since the remap doesn't need the folder); the leftover-path check decoded the exe as ASCII and matched only `check.ps1`'s own spelling of the path, so a non-ASCII user folder would pass it (now UTF-8, and it fails on any `\registry\src\index.crates.io-` and requires `crates\index.crates.io-`; by hand, the remapped exe passes it and an unremapped one fails); bare cargo commands now build with other flags (AGENTS.md says so). Refused: an existing `CARGO_TARGET_..._RUSTFLAGS` overwritten (no workflow sets one); `release.yml` relying on `GITHUB_ENV` unannounced (`check.ps1` says it); the size figure as one machine's (CI's run will show it).
- **Checks (2026-11-26):** `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` with the remap, before the review's two script fixes, passed on both servers (`test` 597 s, `test2016` 195 s; 542 unit tests, 6 ignored, 132 and 125 process tests, 95 and 88 live tests, none failed or retried), exe 3,140,608 bytes, 5,120 under the budget, ten in-box imports. The final script, without `-Live`, passed in 98 s with the same exe. A fresh GitHub runner, which the review's P1 was about, is CI's next run.
- **For the next brief (decision 52):** this changes how the shipped exe is built, through `check.ps1`, which the release workflow runs; listed there rather than given a round of its own, as offered with decision 97.

### Decision 97 external review (4e19fcc..a04a41c)

November 26, 2026. The local checkout was `phase6-slice2` at `49acea2`, clean at the start; its tree was independently checked as identical to the requested main merge `a04a41c`. **The registry-remap mechanism and unsigned budget definition are accepted. No new numbered findings; next is R73.** R69 through R72 remain closed. Ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-decision97.md`.

- **Flag handoff:** setting the target Rustflags variable covers check.ps1's child Cargo commands; writing the same assignment to GITHUB_ENV carries it to later job steps, independently of the child process's environment. This follows [GitHub's environment-file contract](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-commands#setting-an-environment-variable). Explicit workflow documentation would help discoverability, but duplicating the calculation in YAML is not required. With the exported assignment imported into a fresh local command process, the exact Stage tests Cargo command reported **225 fresh compiler artifacts, zero rebuilt**, and the exe's SHA-256 and last-write timestamp were unchanged. The fingerprint records both static CRT and remap flags, consistent with [Cargo's flag precedence](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html#buildrustflags); imports remain the same ten in-box DLLs. The other workflow steps build/install cargo-about, generate notices or copy/sign/package data, not rebuild sqlh with different flags. Notices/install were source-reviewed, not rerun.
- **Output and claim limits:** public panic paths naming `crates`, index hash, crate/version and source/line are acceptable; local debugger source mapping can need adjustment. Remapping also affects diagnostics and file! expansions, so it is path normalization, not proof of invisible-metadata-only behavior. No dependency/license/template change occurred. It removes the measured registry-prefix size difference, not every reproducibility variable or possible private path. Prefer the measured claim that local and CI size match to the universal statement that every machine yields the same size. [Rust remapping limitations](https://doc.rust-lang.org/rustc/remap-source-paths.html).
- **Checks:** the marker scan is a regression check for this Windows/crates.io layout, not an exhaustive privacy proof. Its exact predicate passed remapped, original ASCII, original Unicode, mixed and absent-positive-marker fixtures. The current exe has **0 original markers and 170 remapped markers**; the current USERPROFILE's UTF-8/UTF-16 byte sequences are absent. Other separator styles, registries, Git/generated paths and unrelated absolute paths require separate consideration if introduced. Whitespace refusal is reasonable for this space-separated flags variable and was independently verified to occur before Cargo. No preexisting registry-folder requirement remains. The unsigned artifact built by check.ps1 is a precise budget definition; signed size is a separate distribution measurement.
- **Pre-tag recommendation, still pending evidence:** require a successful release workflow dispatch at the intended tag commit before the immutable v0.2.0 tag. Inspect its unsigned size/imports/markers, confirm Stage tests freshness, then confirm the signed exe retains the remap and passes signature/sum/version checks; record signed size separately. The development version can change artifact size, so the tag's Check must still pass. This is a recommendation to close release evidence, not a new code defect, completed hosted test or permission to dispatch. Existing certification/public-release gates remain separate.
- **Independent verification:** non-live check.ps1 passed first run in **112 s**, no retries: locked metadata, fmt, mocked AppVeyor checks, Clippy default/timing, **542 unit, 132 debug process and 125 release process tests**, locked build, marker/size/import checks. Six unit and live tests remained ignored. Exe **3,140,608 bytes**, **5,120 below 3 MiB**. Independently read [PR CI 45903345845](https://github.com/sequeltom/sqlh/actions/runs/45903345845): successful at `49acea2`, 762 s, same size and ten imports. [Main CI 45905705501](https://github.com/sequeltom/sqlh/actions/runs/45905705501) at `a04a41c` was in progress when inspected. Evidence: `artifacts/review-decision97-*` (snapshot, check log, exported environment, Stage tests hash/timestamp/freshness, marker/space probes, binary/fingerprint scan and CI log/status). Build-input hashes rechecked after testing. Developer live runs remain supplied evidence. No independent live SQL, owner credentials/Credential Manager, fresh-cache build, release dispatch, cargo-about install/generation, signed-artifact inspection, AppVeyor certification or publication. Reviewer changes only ignored report/evidence and PLAN.md/AGENTS.md records; no implementation change, commit, push or external mutation.
- **Its wording point, done (2026-11-26, branch `review-d97-records`):** `check.ps1`'s comment and DESIGN.md §11 no longer say the exe is the same size on any machine: the local and CI builds measured the same size, and other inputs (the version string, the toolchain) can still make two builds differ. The release dispatch the review recommends before the tag is the owner's to start.

- **The dispatch the review asked for:** `release.yml` by hand on `main` at `637c3ab`, run 45910194290, version `0.2.0-dev.17`; all four jobs passed. Build: `check.ps1` in 925 s (542 unit, 132 and 125 process tests); unsigned exe **3,140,608 bytes**, ten in-box imports, the remap check passed; the remap flag in the environment of every later build step (Version check, Stage tests, Upload tests, Third-party notices, Stage, Upload unsigned); "Stage tests" finished in 0.26 s, compiling nothing; the notices step compiled only `cargo-about`. ### Phase 7: evidence-based evaluations (VISION priority 5)

Exact row counts (from plan-history.md 2.6 and real sessions), the Windows 10 / Windows Server 2016 client floor (the SQL Server 2016 floor is decided: decision 49; measured 2026-11-08 on AppVeyor: Server 2016 runs every process test but can't connect over TLS, since the driver needs Windows 10 1809 / Server 2019 for it, upstream-issues.md U12), the md format, and what SmartScreen and antivirus make of the signed exe on locked-down workstations (the signing itself is plan-history.md 3.6). Agent trial 3 ran on 2026-10-21 (plan-history.md 5.6) and covered the row and value limits; its timeout trial is still open. Whether the count budgets (250 ms, and decision 45's 5 s) are right now comes from real sessions as well as trials: the first real-environment feedback (2026-10-31, MCP client feedback above) produced decision 45.

### Phase 8: delivery to agents (skill, connector, plugin)

Raised 2026-10-10. Not a VISION priority: the exe is the product, and these are wrappers that get it to agents. Nothing is decided or started; the owner deferred drafting the skill. In order:

1. **Skill** (S, no code). A `SKILL.md` for Bash-driven agents. `--help` already carries the syntax, so the skill covers workflow: describe before querying, how to read the output format, the limits and exit codes, and when to reach for sqlh. It can be drafted at any time, and is more useful once Phase 4's schema commands exist. From agent trial 2: never add `--allow-write` without the user's explicit go-ahead for that statement; narrow a query before raising `--max-rows`, `--max-cell` or `-t` or using `--exact-count`; compute derived figures (percentages, totals) in SQL or leave them out. From agent trial 1: tell the agent to state only what the output shows (the trial's answer named tables it never queried), to pass `-d` or check `DB_NAME()` first, and to look up column names before writing a query.
2. **Connector = local stdio MCP server** (built in Phase 5 as its option (a), plan-history.md; not here). It is not a hosted, remote connector: credentials come from Credential Manager and SSPI on the workstation, and the workstation reaches the SQL Server, and neither survives a hosted deployment. Packaging it as a Claude Desktop extension (`.mcpb`) is a possible later step and is unresearched.
3. **Plugin** (S, after 2). Bundles the skill, the MCP config and the exe location so installing is one step. Open: whether the plugin carries the exe or points at an installed path, which ties into the code-signing decision in Phase 7.

Phase 5 chose MCP first (decision 14), so step 2 is built (plan-history.md 5.3); the local daemon, Phase 5's option (b), stays deferred (decision 48), and would be added beside it on the same engine, not instead of it.

### Azure SQL support and testing (owner, 2026-10-31; planned, not started)

The owner put Azure SQL on the roadmap after the DNS-cache check: the on-prem test server answers `allowed=false`, so decision 42's residual risk is likely Azure's alone, and nothing about Azure has been tested. VISION.md lists Azure as "not a first-release requirement", to be assessed before any commitment. This task is that assessment, then the testing; it changes no first-release scope until the owner decides (step 1). (M–L)

1. **Scope, an owner decision for §6:**
   - which targets: Azure SQL Database, Azure SQL Managed Instance, or both (Managed Instance is much closer to on-prem);
   - which authentication: SQL logins first, or Microsoft Entra ID too (none is built: sqlh has SQL and Windows SSPI only, and the driver's federated-auth support is unverified for sqlh);
   - the promise: supported, or "tested opportunistically" (DESIGN.md §12).
2. **A test target, the owner's:** provisioned by the owner, its cost the owner's call, and reached through a profile made with `sqlh profiles add` on the owner's machine. Its server name and credentials never go in the repo, as for `test`.
3. **Analysis, no code:** each difference with its evidence and its effect on sqlh, in `docs/notes/azure-sql.md`, before any change. Watch points known now (unverified on Azure unless marked):
   - **`USE` is unsupported in Azure SQL Database** (from Microsoft's documentation, not tested). This affects `sqlh mcp`'s per-call `database` (a `USE`, decision 15, mcp-mode.md §5), `dbs` and other schema commands, decision 34's per-database runs, and three-part names. `-d` is the login's database, so it should still work.
   - **Gateway routing:** Azure's redirect connection policy routes the login (ENVCHANGE 20, or 21 with a database). Decision 43 follows it with the same credentials. R20's binding is by the profile's name, and the certificate is checked against each hop's own name (R46). Check what `info`, the status line and JSON's `connection` report after a route.
   - **The DNS cache** (decision 42, U7): likely acknowledged by Azure. Rerun the event-only recorder (driver-0.3.0.md §8, DNS acknowledgement) against the target.
   - **The reset:** on Azure SQL Database, `sp_reset_connection` also resets the isolation level (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3, built 2026-10-17, the isolation-level finding). The session setup sets the level on every call anyway, so `db::spike::spike_reset`'s SERIALIZABLE assertion needs a per-edition expectation.
   - **Server-level features** absent from Azure SQL Database: the error log and the other `xp_` allowlist entries (DESIGN.md §7), `sp_configure`, `KILL` of other sessions (decision 33), server permissions (`VIEW SERVER STATE` becomes `VIEW DATABASE STATE`). The refusals and server errors an agent gets should stay clear.
   - **Transient faults and latency:** Azure's transient login errors (for example 40613) and an internet round trip. Check them against connect-failures.md's bounds and the engine's one silent retry (decision 19).
   - **Encryption:** Azure requires it, and supports strict (TDS 8). `-No` should still end in full TLS there, checked (R46).
   - **The live tests' own needs:** a scratch database (`sqlh_test`, made by the fixtures), `CREATE LOGIN` for `Reader` (a contained user in Azure SQL Database), `KILL` in the MCP retry test, and setting the compatibility level (decision 21). These decide which tests can run there.
4. **Live run:** the smoke script and the live suite against the target. Classify each failure as expected (a missing feature), a sqlh defect, or a documentation gap, and record the results in the note.
5. **Changes:** catalog SQL with Azure-specific paths needs a supported-target decision and a maintenance justification (AGENTS.md, Catalog SQL). Fixes land with tests, and with DESIGN.md (§1, §12) and, if support is promised, the README.

### Hosted MCP with Entra pass-through (decision 47, owner, 2026-11-01; planned, not started)

A colleague of the owner asked for Cowork-style access: claude.ai's remote connectors, which connect from Anthropic's cloud whichever Claude client is used ([Anthropic](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11503834-building-custom-integrations-via-remote-mcp-servers)), so a local `sqlh mcp` can't serve them. (In the owner's Cowork session of 2026-10-31 the desktop app reached a local `sqlh mcp` through its bridge; that keeps working where Claude Desktop runs on a Windows workstation that reaches the server.) Decision 47 puts a hosted MCP server on the roadmap with one identity model: each caller signs in with Entra ID and every query runs as that person. (L)

- **The first environment:** the colleague's SQL Server 2025 Standard on an Azure VM, and perhaps soon an Azure SQL Managed Instance in the same environment. The owner prefers PaaS to IaaS for the hosting: Windows App Service, say, with its built-in Entra sign-in (Easy Auth), access restrictions for Anthropic's IP ranges, and virtual network integration to reach the VM privately. Containers were an example, not a requirement.
- **The flow:**
  - **The caller to sqlh:** OAuth 2.1 with Entra as the authorization server; sqlh is the resource server and never issues tokens. MCP over Streamable HTTP, the 2026-07-28 revision only (a POST per message, no sessions, a closed stream is the cancel), so the older revisions' sessions and server-held streams are not built.
  - **sqlh to SQL Server:** sqlh exchanges the caller's token for one scoped to SQL (Entra's on-behalf-of flow, which also checks the caller's token), and logs in with it (TDS federated authentication). sqlh's own credential for the exchange can be the host's managed identity, so the host stores no secret and no SQL password.
  - **The driver:** mssql-tds 0.3.0 has a pre-acquired token method (`TdsAuthenticationMethod::AccessToken`) and token sources the application supplies (`ActiveDirectoryTokenCredential` with an `EntraIdTokenFactory`), plus managed identity and the other Entra methods (read from its source, 2026-11-01; not yet tried against a server).
- **Targets:** servers that take Entra logins: SQL Server 2022 and later with Entra authentication set up (on an Azure VM, enabled for the VM; to verify on the colleague's), Azure SQL Managed Instance, and Azure SQL Database. Other servers keep the CLI and the local `sqlh mcp` on a workstation.
- **What sqlh would build:**
  - MCP in an exe of its own first (decision 46's question (b), settled for this tier by decision 47), so `sqlh.exe` never listens on a network;
  - Streamable HTTP on the host's interface, behind the platform's TLS;
  - the token exchange, over Windows' own HTTP stack (WinHTTP is in-box), and the token handed to the driver;
  - a connection pool per person and profile, since a connection carries one person's identity, with the reset between calls as today (decision 15);
  - profiles that name a server and database but no login or password, the caller's identity supplying the rest.
- **Setup on the customer's side:** an Entra app registration for sqlh, admin consent for it to act for users on SQL, SQL users made from Entra, and the claude.ai connector configured with the app (to check: that claude.ai's connector can use a pre-registered Entra app, since Entra offers no dynamic client registration; and how App Service runs sqlh, as a process it forwards to or a Windows container).
- **Ties in with:** Azure SQL support and testing (above), whose first target becomes Managed Instance, and the CLI's own Entra login (DESIGN.md §13, "Entra token pass-in"), which the same driver path makes possible.
- **Alternatives:**
  - **Backup, open for reconsideration (decision 47):** (a) a shared read-only login per profile on the host (a group-managed service account with Windows auth, or a SQL login whose password comes from the host's secrets; sqlh maps callers to the profiles they may use and records who ran what). It works on any version, SQL Server 2016 included, but every caller has that login's permissions, which are then the boundary. Not built; revisit if a server without Entra needs hosting.
  - **Not planned:** (b) a SQL login per caller stored on the host (every password entered and rotated on a shared server), and (d) Kerberos delegation from the token's user to an Active Directory account (domain-administrator delegation settings).

### SQL Server 2016 support (decision 49, owner, 2026-11-01; first run passed 2026-11-01)

**The first run:** on 2016 SP3 (13.0.6300) Developer, with a sysadmin SQL login: `tests/fixtures/sqlh_test.sql` ran as written, the smoke script matched its golden files, and the live suite's two failures were both tests that assumed 2025, now fixed; 61 live tests pass on debug and 55 on release, and the full `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` still passes on 2025. No catalog SQL needed a change. The two:

- `the_error_log_and_server_reads_...`: `VIEW ANY ERROR LOG` is SQL Server 2022's, so the grant doesn't parse on 2016 (error 102). The rest of DESIGN.md §7's permissions table holds there, error numbers included.
- `describe_shows_any_name_from_compatibility_level_110`: 2016 has no `STRING_AGG`, so a column name XML can't carry (U+001B) makes `describe` fail after its columns, with the server's error 6841. It's a known version difference, documented in schema-commands.md §3, not fixed: such a name is rare, and any fix needs a third way to aggregate on 2016. An ordinary name is described in full.

By hand on `StackOverflow2013`, read-only: `info`, `tables`, `describe` (table and DMV, text and JSON), `find`, `def`, `deps`, capped and aggregate queries, csv, `--check`, the guard, `--read-uncommitted`, `--exact-count`, a DMV read, the error log, and `sqlh mcp`'s `query`, `describe` and `info`. A `STRING_AGG` query gets the server's error 195. `-Ns` fails with a message that says strict encryption needs SQL Server 2022 or later. A first read of `dbo.Posts` (37 GB) hit the 60 s timeout, exit 4, then took about 200 ms: consistent with a cold cache, not diagnosed further.

Not checked on 2016: Windows authentication (the instance refuses this workstation's login, error 18452), a login short of sysadmin beyond the `Reader` tests, and an availability group here (the owner's benchmark ran on one, notes/mcp-client-feedback.md).

1. **The instance, the owner's:** SQL Server 2016 SP3 (Developer edition is free) on a machine the workstation reaches, with mixed-mode authentication (the `Reader` tests make SQL logins) and a login with the rights the 2025 `test` profile has. The owner makes its profile with `sqlh profiles add` (a name such as `test2016`); its server name and credentials stay out of the repo, as for `test`.
2. **The scratch database:** `tests/fixtures/sqlh_test.sql` creates `sqlh_test` there; check that the script itself runs on 2016.
3. **First run:** the smoke script and the live suite with `SQLH_TEST_PROFILE` naming the 2016 profile, debug and release. Classify each failure: a sqlh defect (catalog SQL that needs a later version), a test that assumes 2025 (strict encryption needs 2022 or later; 2016's highest compatibility level is 130, which decision 21's level test must use; error text or catalog columns that differ), or a known version difference to document.
4. **Fixes:** defects fixed in sqlh with tests; a test that depends on the version asks the server (`SERVERPROPERTY('ProductMajorVersion')`) and states its 2016 expectation, never skips silently; the smoke golden files normalize the server's version already, so a difference in them is a finding.
5. **Running it:** the 2016 run stays a separate step before a release, with no change to the script: `$env:SQLH_TEST_PROFILE='test2016'; pwsh scripts/check.ps1 -Live`. CI can't reach either server, so both stay the owner's live runs.

## 5. Sequencing rationale

Why Phases 1–3 came in the order they did; their tasks are in plan-history.md. The order of the work since is set by decisions 46–51 (§6).

- **1.1 (F04) first.** It is the only finding where the tool states something false while possibly executing writes.
- **The test harness comes before the other fixes,** so every fix lands with a process-level regression test.
- **2.1 (connect latency) early, but time-boxed.** It is cheap to measure and could change Phase 5 entirely. Building pooling to hide a fixable 150 ms would be the wrong order. Bounded output and cancellation under a stalled reader (plan-history.md 2.2–2.4) are release requirements, so they come before pooling or more discovery features, whatever 2.1 finds.
- **The Runner refactor (plan-history.md 3.1) after the correctness fixes and before schema/MCP work.** Fixing defects first keeps the refactor behavior-neutral and easy to verify; schema commands and MCP both need its policies.

## 6. Owner decisions

Decided 2026-10-10:

1. **Where sqlh runs: workstations first.** The SQL Server is assumed to be remote in about 90% of use cases. The first release is validated on Windows 11 x64 workstations against a remote SQL Server 2025 Standard. Running on the database server is not a target. **Amended 2026-11-01 (owner):** "workstations only" was too narrow. A workstation is the CLI's primary target; an MCP server may also run on an application server, as the hosted MCP server of decision 47 does.
2. **Exit code 7: output could not be delivered.** DESIGN.md §6 defines exactly what "undeliverable" means.
3. **Substitution order: split first, then substitute per batch** (today's behavior). Variables can't create batch separators. DESIGN.md §4.3 is corrected to match.
4. **Global temp tables (`##`) are blocked in read-only mode.**
5. **A scratch database and a low-privilege test login on the dev/test server are approved.** The scratch database is `sqlh_test`. The test login waits until its password can be stored without a console (planned with plan-history.md 3.4), so it never passes through a command line or a file. **How (owner, 2026-10-12):** a live-test fixture, connected through the `test` profile, creates the login with only `db_datareader` on `sqlh_test` (and StackOverflow2010 while it is still used) and `VIEW DEFINITION`; its password is generated at random in the process and never printed or saved to a file. As built (plan-history.md 3.4), it isn't stored at all: the SQL that sets it reaches sqlh on stdin, and runs as the login get it from `SQLCMDPASSWORD` in their own environment; the test profile's stored password is for its own login only, so sqlh takes the variable. The login is dropped after each test. The guard and permission tests then run as that login. Also with plan-history.md 3.4: synthetic tables in `sqlh_test` replace StackOverflow2010 in the live tests, and the fixtures drop what they create and shrink `sqlh_test` when they finish, since the drain and memory tests grew it and tempdb on the dev server.

Decided 2026-10-11, except where an item gives a later date:

6. **The drain default: a bounded count for read-only runs (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** Following [notes/drain-experiment.md](notes/drain-experiment.md): in read-only runs, sqlh counts hidden rows for about 250 ms and then stops (an attention), reporting a lower bound floored to one significant digit (`100 of ≥800000 rows shown (counting stopped)`), so a result that ends inside the budget stays exact and the same query gives the same output. The exact count stays under `--allow-write` (a stopped count skips later statements, which can include a `COMMIT`), for input that mentions `XACT_ABORT`, and on request (a flag such as `--exact-count`). Independently, `-t` expiring while only hidden rows are being counted reports `≥N` with the run's own exit code, not exit 4, since the rows shown are complete. A stopped count is visible to machines: the status line says so (`ok: 2 batches, counting stopped in 1`) and each JSON batch gets a flag. Accepted cost: "limits never change what runs" no longer holds strictly, because a stopped count cancels the rest of that batch; in a read-only run nothing it skips outlives the run, and the output says it happened. plan-history.md 2.15, built 2026-10-11. **Clarified in building it, confirmed by the owner 2026-10-11:** `-t` expiring during the count stays exit 4 (i) with writes allowed, because a stop would skip a later `COMMIT` and the run would report success, and (ii) with `XACT_ABORT` in the input, because the attention rolls the read-only wrapper back, so the run would end with exit 1, or exit 0 with the wrapper gone, in place of the timeout. The 250 ms start at the first hidden row, so a stall before it is bounded by `-t` (default 60 s). Each JSON result also says whether its `row_count` is exact, and the contract is 2.0 (R16).
7. **Agent session defaults (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** Every connection gets `SET DEADLOCK_PRIORITY LOW` (the agent is the deadlock victim, error 1205) and `SET LOCK_TIMEOUT 10000` in both modes, so a blocked query fails after 10 s with error 1222 instead of waiting out `-t`; the lock timeout can be changed by a flag and a profile setting. The isolation level stays the database default (`READ COMMITTED`, which honors read-committed snapshot where the DBA turned it on): `READ UNCOMMITTED` is opt-in only (a flag such as `--read-uncommitted` and a profile setting), for read-only runs only, never under `--allow-write`, and the output notes it, because dirty reads can silently return uncommitted, duplicated or missing rows. plan-history.md 2.8, built 2026-10-11. The flag in a run whose profile allows writes (`write_policy = "allow"`) is a usage error too, since that run can write.
8. **A Git remote and CI: GitHub, with Actions (owner direction, 2026-10-10).** 3.3 becomes CI (plan-history.md 3.5), and 3.6 adds a signed release workflow like ssms-executor's. **Decided 2026-10-12:** the repository starts private (GitHub Pro) and goes public when the owner is ready to present it. Before it goes public, the history is rewritten with `git filter-repo` to drop the review files (12 commits hold them, one with a local path), keeping the rest of the history; nothing else private was found in the history. **Done 2026-10-12, before the first push** (owner): with `git filter-branch`, since filter-repo needs Python, which this machine doesn't have. `code-review.md`, `code-review-phase1.md` and `code-review-phase2.md` were removed from every commit of `main` and `drain-prototype`; the nine commits that only added a review were dropped (114 commits became 105, the 13 merges kept), and every other commit kept its tree minus those files, its author, dates and message. Commit ids cited in messages were rewritten to the new ids in the same pass, and those in the docs in the commit after it (a dropped commit maps to its parent's rewrite, which has the same code). `main`'s tree is unchanged. Every commit's ids changed except the first. A scan of every blob and message of the new history found no local user path but the synthetic `/c/Users/me`, no address outside loopback, RFC 5737's documentation ranges and one test value, no email address, and none of the owner's server names. The old history is in `artifacts/sqlh-history-before-rewrite-2026-10-12.bundle`, with the old-to-new map in `artifacts/history-rewrite-commit-map.txt` (local, ignored). Not rewritten: Codex's two local checkpoint refs under `refs/codex/`, which point at trees, not commits; one still holds two review files. They are never pushed unless the whole repository is mirrored. Releases build on `workflow_dispatch` and version tags, with the version taken from the tag (plan-history.md 3.6). **Name, decided 2026-10-12: sqlh, repository `tkwj/sqlh`** (now `sequeltom/sqlh`: the owner's account was renamed on 2026-11-07). "sqlharness" collides with `rgonek/sqlharness` on GitHub (an active C# project, July 2026: "safety-focused SQL benchmarking and optimization harness for coding agents" for SQL Server, Azure SQL and PostgreSQL, with MCP), and "harness" is close to Harness Inc.'s Database DevOps product; the exe name `sqlh` is free on crates.io and clashes only with small, unrelated Go libraries. The profiles folder and the Credential Manager names moved from `sqlharness` to `sqlh`, with a one-time migration of existing profiles and passwords (DESIGN.md §8). The local checkout folder can keep its name. **License, decided 2026-10-12: MIT** (owner: one license; MIT for its familiarity, and it matches the mssql-tds driver; every dependency is under a permissive license: MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD, Unlicense or Unicode-3.0). `LICENSE` and `Cargo.toml`'s `license` field say so. The release zip must also carry the dependencies' notices, which MIT and BSD require in binary distributions (plan-history.md 3.6).
9. **Ask upstream to redact the password in packet dumps?** At debug level, mssql-tds hex-dumps every TDS packet before TLS encrypts it (`io/packet_writer.rs`), including the login packet ([notes/connect-latency.md](notes/connect-latency.md)). The password in it carries only the scramble the TDS protocol requires (nibble swap and XOR 0xA5), which anyone can reverse. It's a logging-hygiene issue (CWE-532), not a remote vulnerability: nothing leaks unless an application turns on debug logging for the driver. Other drivers' traces usually mask the password. The channel is the owner's choice: a GitHub issue, or Microsoft's security process. Check `main` first; this was read in 0.2.0. **Decided 2026-10-12 (owner): no, sqlh doesn't ask.** Its own rule stays the defense: never install a general logger while mssql-tds runs (AGENTS.md, Driver logging). Re-check the dump when bumping the driver.
10. **Provenance in the text output: always, on the status line (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** Every text and csv run that connected ends its status line with the server, the database the run started in and the connect time, for about 10 to 15 tokens a call: `-- ok: 1 batch, 241 ms; db01/master, connect 195 ms` (csv: on stderr with the rest of the metadata). Left out when nothing connected (usage error, guard refusal, failed connect). Names are escaped and cut like other metadata, within the ending's reserve. Auth method and encryption stay in JSON and `--verbose`. Shown every time, not only when `-d` was missing, so the shape of the status line doesn't depend on how the run was invoked. Rest of plan-history.md 2.11, built 2026-10-11.
11. **Paths in metadata lines keep the escape rule (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** A backslash in a path inside a `-- ` line is written `\\`, like any other escaped field (DESIGN.md §5.1), and a reader decodes it like any other field. Input is never escaped: paths, SQL text and `-v` values are taken exactly as typed, so a path from Explorer's "Copy as path" (double-quoted, single backslashes) works as is after `-i` or `-o` in cmd.exe, PowerShell and Git Bash; only Git Bash eats an unquoted backslash, which is the shell's rule. Rejected: forward slashes (rewrites what was typed, and some cmd built-ins refuse them) and escaping a backslash only before an escape letter (changes the reader rule, and `C:\new` would still be doubled). Done 2026-10-11 in plan-history.md 2.13's follow-up: DESIGN.md §5.1 says paths follow the rule, the `--help` FORMAT lines say so without growing, and a process-level test runs a quoted "Copy as path" spelling, spaces included.
12. **Agents and the write switch (owner decision, 2026-10-12, from agent trial 2): new profiles refuse writes.** `sqlh profile add` saves `write_policy = "deny"` for a new profile unless `--write-policy` is given; existing profiles keep their policy, and a profile saved without the key is still `flag` (DESIGN.md §8). Built with plan-history.md 2.16. **Decided 2026-10-12 (owner): `profile list` keeps showing the server's address and the login.** Every run's status line already names the server, and a login isn't a secret; hiding them would make choosing a profile harder for an agent without protecting anything. A control over what agents may see or change in profiles would be a separate design. The background: An agent asked to clean up a table added `--allow-write` on its own and ran a 927,269-row `DELETE`; only the profile's `deny` stopped it. Options: make `deny` the default for new profiles (so writes take an explicit change to the profile's policy), keep `flag` and rely on agent guidance (the skill, Phase 8), or both. Also: `sqlh profile list` showed the agent the server's address and the login; whether it should, in agent hands, is part of the same question. Precedent (comparison with the owner's earlier sql-query-runner skill, 2026-10-12): that skill gives agents no write path at all (a query must start with `SELECT` or `WITH`, or be one allowed `EXEC` or `DBCC TRACESTATUS`), so trial 2's cleanup would have stopped at its refusal. Its check is weaker than sqlh's, though: it looks only at the first word and runs with no rollback, so `WITH x AS (...) DELETE ...`, `SELECT 1; DELETE ...` and `SELECT ... INTO` pass and write. The nearest sqlh equivalent of "no write path for agents" is `deny` as the default for new profiles, which keeps the stronger guard and makes writing an explicit change to a profile's policy. That change can run unattended, so an agent that can run `profile add` can make it (R19): `deny` stops an agent reaching for `--allow-write`, it doesn't make writing a person's decision. A human-only control over profiles, if wanted, is a separate design.
13. **`profile list` is text only (owner decision, 2026-10-13, from R25).** Profiles are ancillary to the product: listing them is setup an agent does once per session, and text is the cheapest format to read. A json listing would need a document shape of its own (R25 found that the anonymous batch it used breaks contract 2.0's batch metadata), which isn't worth its cost now, so `--format` is refused for `profile list` and the JSON contract is unchanged. Search (`--search`) stays. Revisit only if a consumer that can't read text needs the list (Phase 8's MCP connector might).
14. **Connection reuse comes through MCP first (owner decision, 2026-10-15, Phase 5).** The reuse engine (connections kept open, reset between calls, the cleanup and clean-cancel rules) is built once as a library module, and `sqlh mcp` (docs/mcp-mode.md) is its first front end. A local per-profile daemon behind the CLI, which would speed up Bash-driven use too, is deferred: it would be added on the same engine only if evidence (an agent trial with MCP) shows Bash-driven use stays the main path. Why: VISION.md asks that reuse preserve execution policy, isolation between calls and reliable cleanup, and under MCP the harness owns the lifecycle and cancellation is part of the protocol, where a daemon would make sqlh build auto-start, stale-process, idle-exit, pipe-security and cross-process cancellation itself; MCP also gives native tools and separate read and write tools a harness can gate, and Phase 8 already plans the connector. The cost, accepted: until a daemon exists, a CLI call still pays its connect (about 195 ms on the dev LAN, plan-history.md 2.1). **The deferral stands (decision 48, 2026-11-01):** the connect is about 0.5–1.5% of a small model's turn.
15. **MCP calls are stateless, for now (owner decision, 2026-10-15, Phase 5).** Before every call the reused connection is reset (TDS RESETCONNECTION: login defaults restored, temp tables dropped, a stray transaction rolled back, the profile's database restored), then the session setup and, for reads, the per-call wrapper transaction run: each call behaves exactly like one CLI run, without the connect. No `session_open` handles, so docs/mcp-mode.md §5's sessions, and the hour-long read-only transaction they needed, are not built. Why: isolation between calls and reliable cleanup (VISION.md) come from the reset itself, a cancelled call can leave nothing behind but the connection, and agents and sqlh have no handles, expiry or long transaction to manage. Sessions can be added later on the same engine if an agent trial shows agents need state across calls. The cost, accepted: work that needs state across calls isn't possible read-only (a wait-stats delta is two snapshots the agent compares itself); within one call, a script's batches still share one connection and its temp tables. **As built (plan-history.md 5.2, 2026-10-17; the decision is unchanged):** on SQL Server (all but Azure SQL Database) the reset doesn't restore the transaction isolation level, so a call's `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL` would outlive it on its pooled connection; each call is still like one CLI run because the session setup sets the level on every call, a reused connection's included (`READ COMMITTED`, or `READ UNCOMMITTED` for a profile that reads that way; `run::session_setup`). Found by the self-review (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 1, P1); docs/notes/mcp-reuse-spike.md §1, DESIGN.md §4.3.
16. **MCP is read-only, for now (owner decision, 2026-10-16, Phase 5).** The first `sqlh mcp` offers only read tools (`query`, the schema commands, `info`), each in the read-only wrapper with the guard, whatever the profile's `write_policy`; there is no `execute` tool, so docs/mcp-mode.md's write tool and write sessions are not built. A write goes through the CLI, with its safeguards: the profile's policy (`deny` by default for new profiles, decision 12), `--allow-write` and the guard. Why: the speed win matters most for the many repeated reads of an investigation; agent trial 2's agent added `--allow-write` on its own and only the profile's `deny` stopped a 927,269-row `DELETE`, and under an `execute` tool the last check would be the harness's permission prompt, which users often set to always allow; and Phase 6's command categorization is where which statements need explicit approval, and how, gets decided, so `execute` is added with it rather than designed twice. The cost, accepted: a write during an MCP investigation pays the CLI's connect.
17. **A connection is reused after a cancel only when the server confirmed it (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** A call that ends with a TDS attention (a cancel from the client or harness, `-t`, or the bounded count of hidden rows stopping at its budget, decision 6) keeps its connection when the server acknowledged the attention within the settle bound (R15 B); the reset before the next call (decision 15) clears what the cancelled call left: an open wrapper transaction, temp tables, `SET`s (as built, all but the isolation level, which the session setup sets on every call: decision 15's as-built note). Anything less discards the connection and the next call reconnects: no acknowledgement (today's `COUNT_STOP_UNCONFIRMED`), the settle grace running out, a protocol error, a lost connection. Why: count stops end any query with many hidden rows, so cancels are routine, and always discarding would make the calls agents make most pay the login again; the reset is the mechanism connection pooling relies on, and the unclear cases are the ones discarded. Required before it ships: a live test that a confirmed cancel leaves the next call clean (`@@TRANCOUNT` 0, the cancelled call's temp table gone, the agent session's `SET`s restored), and fake-server tests that an unacknowledged or stalled attention discards and reconnects. The cost, accepted: more code and tests than always reconnecting, and a residual risk of driver state the reset doesn't clear, which those tests are there to catch (the driver can be swapped behind `src/db/`). **Amended 2026-10-17 (owner), after spike 5.1:** mssql-tds 0.2.0 gives no receipt for an acknowledged attention, so "confirmed by the server" can't be checked; a connection the driver kept (`is_connection_dead()` false, which covers an acknowledged attention, a cancel before the request was sent and a half-sent request withdrawn with EOM|IGNORE, all in step with the server) is reused, and one it marked dead, or one past sqlh's settle bound, is discarded. The check that a reused session is clean is the next call's reset, whose acknowledgement the driver verifies (the server's ResetConnection ENVCHANGE must come first, or the request fails with `ConnectionResetNotAcknowledged` and the connection is marked dead); then the connection is discarded and the call retried once on a new one (decision 19). Why: what matters is whether the session is clean and in step, which the verified reset answers on every reuse after any kind of cancel; the spike found every kind clean after it (docs/notes/mcp-reuse-spike.md §2). The missing receipt is U4 in docs/notes/upstream-issues.md, for the owner to raise; the rule can tighten if upstream adds one. **The live test it requires is built (plan-history.md 5.4, 2026-10-18):** `mcp_a_cancel_the_driver_kept_leaves_the_next_call_clean`: the idle session holds no transaction, and the next call on the same session gets a fresh call's `@@TRANCOUNT` (its own wrapper's 1), `SET`s, language and level, with the cancelled call's temp table gone (plan-history.md, Phase 5.4).
18. **An MCP server reaches the profiles listed at its launch (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** `sqlh mcp --profile test --profile reporting` serves those profiles and no others; each tool takes an optional `profile`, which must be one of them (with one listed, it is the default). The server's instructions name the allowed profiles with their descriptions (plan-history.md 4.1), so the agent picks the right server in one call; a profile's connection opens on its first use. No tool argument names a server, a login or a password (docs/mcp-mode.md §3 stands for those). Why: the owner sets the reach once, in the harness config, and the agent never gets more; profile descriptions keep their job under MCP; one copy of the tool definitions keeps the agent's context cost flat however many profiles are listed; and with read-only tools (decision 16) the risk within the list is reads from servers the owner chose. Strict separation stays possible: a profile can get its own server entry, listed alone, so the harness gates it separately. The cost, accepted: within one server's list, the harness can't prompt per profile. **As built (plan-history.md 5.3, 2026-10-17; the decision is unchanged):** `profile` is optional only with one profile listed (the default); with several, every tool's schema lists it in `required` and `tools::plan` refuses a call without one (`missing required property profile: one of A, B`, exit 3; plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 1). A call's `profile` matches in any ASCII case, as the launch's `--profile` does (since `becb56f`; until then it had to match the profiles file's spelling exactly), and the schemas' enum and the instructions show the file's spelling (mcp-mode.md §3).
19. **MCP connection limits (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** Per listed profile, up to 2 connections by default (`--pool N`, 1–8), opened only when a call needs one, so a second exists only when calls overlap. A connection idle for 15 minutes is closed (`--idle MINUTES`; 0 keeps connections open while the server runs). A reused connection found dead (a network drop, a killed session) is reconnected and the call retried once, silently; if the retry fails, the call gets the real error. Why: harnesses make several tool calls at once (parallel discovery), and two connections cover that without loading the server; 15 minutes covers a human pause mid-investigation and ends idle sessions on production servers well before firewalls drop them; and a retry is safe because every call is a read in a rolled-back wrapper (decisions 15, 16). The cost, accepted: the first call after a long pause pays the login again, and a busy session holds up to two server sessions per listed profile. **As built (plan-history.md 5.2, 2026-10-17, `becb56f`, `6e7a0d8` and `4519341`, its boundary stated in `dc24d4b`): the decision's implementation, recorded for the owner's code review; the decision is unchanged, and the implementation raises no question of its own, since only a dead connection is replaced. Accepted as built by the external review (2026-10-18, its question 1), its ten-second boundary explicit, with the regressions it asked for kept since `c3c4a63`.** A reused connection shows it is dead at its first requests, the session setup (`run::setup`, which carries the reset) and the call's `USE` when it names a database (`run::use_database`), before any of the call's own SQL, so the call is retried on a new connection when either fails with the connection dead or retired (`engine::dead_before_work`): closed while idle (`Lost`), a reset the server didn't acknowledge (`Protocol`), or gone silent (a timeout whose attention went unanswered within the driver's 5 s). Not retried: a SQL error (a `database` that doesn't exist), a cancel (nor anything once the call's cancel is set), and a timeout at the call's own limit on a connection the driver kept, which ends `query timeout after N s in the session setup` (or `... changing to the call's database`). **The probe** (`REUSE_PROBE`): a reused connection's setup gets at most 5 s when the call's timeout is longer or 0; the `USE` keeps the call's timeout and runs once on its connection, after the setup has worked. Why: a connection gone silent while idle (a firewall's idle cut, a half-open socket) would otherwise hold the call for its whole timeout and the driver's 5 s attention wait and then fail it; with the probe it is found dead in about 10 s and the call runs again on a new connection, which is what the decision asks of a dead connection. **A setup the probe cuts on a live connection** (the server acknowledged the attention within the driver's 5 s attention wait, so within about 10 s of the setup being sent: slow, not dead) is reset and run again, the setup alone, on that same connection with the call's own timeout, not replaced. Why: a slow server is most likely one under load, when a DBA is investigating, and there a new login is the costly step and the one most likely to fail; replacing a live connection would also drop the profile's healthy idle ones. The probe costs a slow live setup its 5 s and the attention's acknowledgement, which the call's connect time reports (DESIGN.md §5.1). **The boundary** (round 5's second half, finding 1; stated in `REUSE_PROBE`'s doc in `dc24d4b`, whose `dead_before_work` doc names the 5 s condition, the code as built): a live server that doesn't acknowledge the attention within those 5 s, stalled more than about 10 s on the setup, can't be told from a half-open connection. The driver marks the connection dead, so it can't be kept, and it is replaced like a dead one: the profile's other idle connections are dropped, healthy as they are, and the call depends on a new login, which costs the call nothing more when it works (reproduced: ok at about 10 s on a new connection) and fails it when it doesn't (exit 2). Kept as built: taking such a connection for dead is inherent to the probe, and dropping the peers is right for the case the probe is for, a firewall's idle cut, where they most likely died the same way; it needs a stall of more than about 10 s on a trivial request against a server that still answers. History: until `becb56f` no timeout was retried, so a silent reused connection failed its call with a bare `query timeout` after the call's timeout plus 5 s, which round 3 found departs from this decision (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 3, finding 5). `becb56f` added the probe, over the setup and the `USE`, and retried a setup it cut on a live connection on a new one, beyond "found dead", recorded then for the owner to confirm; round 4 found that costly (a failed login could fail a call that would have succeeded, finding 1), and `6e7a0d8` narrowed it to the above. `6e7a0d8`'s loop around the cut setup also caught a `USE` that ran past the call's own timeout on a live connection, so the `USE` ran twice and the call took about twice its timeout; round 5 confirmed it (P2) and `4519341` fixed it: the engine calls `run::setup` and `run::use_database` apart, and the loop runs the setup alone (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 5, first half). Round 5's second half found the boundary above undocumented, the code's comments and the docs saying a slow server keeps its connection without the driver's 5 s, and `dc24d4b` recorded it (round 5, second half, finding 1); mcp-mode.md §5.
20. **MCP ships in the one exe, behind a size gate (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** `sqlh mcp` is a subcommand of `sqlh.exe`, not a separate `sqlh-mcp.exe`. Its code runs only when a harness launches `sqlh mcp`, so CLI-only use pays nothing for it at run time: no startup time, no memory, no open connections; the cost is disk, estimated at about 0.2 MB (295 KB as built, below). **The gate:** MCP's measured cost (`cargo bloat --release --crates` and the exe size before and after, in plan-history.md 5.3) must stay within its 300 KB budget, and the exe within the 3 MB size budget; past that, this decision is reopened. Why: one exe is a hard constraint (AGENTS.md); a split would double the release work (two files to sign, version, ship and document) and let the CLI and MCP drift apart in version while they share the profiles and the output contract; and "only as much tool as you need" is about what runs and what can surprise a user, which dormant code does neither. Splitting later stays cheap, because the reuse engine is a library module (decision 14). Measured: JSON handling (`serde_json`) alone costs 65 KB (spike 5.1). All of Phase 5, measured 2026-10-17 (plan-history.md 5.3) at `dc24d4b`: the release exe is 2,789,888 bytes against 2,487,808 at the end of Phase 4, so 302,080 bytes, 295 KB (at 1,024 bytes a KB, like the 65 KB), for the engine, the run's split, `src/mcp`, `serde_json` and the `describe` fix found while building it; 268 KB as first built, before the self-review's fixes, 274.5 KB at `0183d25`, 287 KB at `e8175d4`, 290 KB at `05032c0`, 293 KB at `becb56f`, 294 KB at `6e7a0d8` and 295.5 KB at `4519341`. Within the gate, with 5 KB to spare (read as 300,000 bytes, it would be 2,080 bytes over; the gate's figures have been 1,024-byte KB throughout, and which reading holds is the owner's), and the exe 355,840 bytes under the 3 MB budget. Checked by the exe's size alone: `cargo bloat` isn't installed on the dev machine, so the split by crate is unmeasured. **Reopened 2026-10-18:** the external review accepted 295 KB at `a8c5390` and asked for a remeasure after its fixes, with no silent raise; at `367f8b0` the exe is 2,805,248 bytes, 317,440 (310.0 KiB) over Phase 4, past the 300 KiB (307,200-byte) gate by 10,240 bytes (17,440 over 300,000, so past it under either reading of a KB), and 340,480 under the 3 MB budget. Options put to the owner: (A) raise the MCP gate to 350 KiB; (C) build `serde_json` with `opt-level = "z"` (measured 2,795,520 bytes, 300.5 KiB) and raise the gate to 350 KiB; (B), `opt-level = "z"` alone, no longer fits (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, Size). **Amended by the owner, 2026-10-18,** after the R35–R39 fixes took MCP to 310.0 KiB (plan-history.md, Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, Size): the gate is **350 KiB**, counted in 1,024-byte KB, and `serde_json` is built for size (`[profile.release.package.serde_json] opt-level = "z"`), which saves 9,728 bytes and doesn't matter to MCP's speed at its message sizes.
