85. **`activity --plans` waits for the showplan work (owner decision, 2026-11-21; amends decision 28).** Plans measured 2,900 to over 200,000 characters (slice 2 design review, 2026-11-21): cut at `max_cell`'s 500 they are an XML header, and raising `max_cell` raises it for every row's `statement` and `input_buffer` too, so 50 sessions' plans can fill `--max-output`. Offered (a) deferring `--plans` to command-categories.md §8 (recommended), (b) one session's plan in a section of its own (`--plan N`), or (c) head blockers' plans with a larger limit, the owner: "a". So `activity` has no plan column and no `plans` argument in slice 2; an agent reads one session's plan with a read-only query of `sys.dm_exec_text_query_plan`, which the tool description names.
86. **`activity` without the view permission fails, exit 1 (owner decision, 2026-11-21; amends decision 28).** Without `VIEW SERVER PERFORMANCE STATE` (`VIEW SERVER STATE` before SQL Server 2022) SQL Server shows a login only its own session (live check 10, both servers), and `activity` leaves its own session out, so decision 28's note would always come with an empty list, which an agent can read as "nothing is running". Offered (a) failing as `deps` does without VIEW DEFINITION (recommended), or (b) the note and the empty list, as decided, the owner: "a". So the error says which permission the login lacks, what SQL Server shows without it, and that a DBA can grant it, naming `VIEW SERVER STATE` on a server older than 2022.
87. **Slice 2 is built within the 3 MiB budget; the budget comes back before slice 3 (owner decision, 2026-11-21).** The exe was 3,111,424 bytes (34,304 left) for all of Phase 6 Part 1, and slice 2 was estimated at 12–18 KB after decisions 83 and 85. Offered (a) building within the budget, measuring each commit, and bringing a measured proposal (`cargo bloat`: what is left, what Part 1 still needs, what could shrink without changing behavior) before slice 3 (recommended), (b) raising the budget now, or (c) looking for savings first, the owner: "a". If slice 2 itself would cross 3 MiB, the work stops for the owner. Decision 20's MCP gate (350 KiB, 310.0 KiB used) is measured with it. Settled by decision 99 (2026-11-27): 3.25 MiB for the rest of Part 1, held conservatively.
88. **`activity` in `sqlh mcp` gets no connection of its own (owner decision, 2026-11-21).** A call takes a pooled connection like any other (decision 19), so with the pool's connections (2 by default, `--pool`) held by calls stuck behind a lock, an `activity` call waits for one until its timeout and fails "waiting for a free connection", and with `--pool 1` one stuck call is enough. Offered (a) documenting it, with the timeout's advice naming the CLI's `sqlh activity`, which opens a connection of its own (recommended), or (b) a reserved connection or permit for `activity` and `errorlog` (an engine change, so an external review under decision 52, and size), the owner: "a". The engine is unchanged in slice 2; reopen it if real use shows the wait.
89. **`errorlog` reads through `sp_readerrorlog` alone and searches in SQL, with each match's companion lines (owner decision, 2026-11-21; amends decision 28).** Decision 28 had a login that may run `xp_readerrorlog` read with a server-side window and everyone else through `sp_readerrorlog`. Live check 10 measured the same time for both (210 ms for a 455 KB archive): the server reads the whole file either way, and the window only spares copying rows into tempdb. Decision 84 needs each archive's oldest line, which a window hides; and the server's search returns only the matching line of a multi-line entry, so `Login failed` loses its `Error: 18456, Severity: 14, State: 8.` line and `18456` loses the user and reason (slice 2 design review). Offered (a) one path, every archive read whole by `sp_readerrorlog` into a temp table, filtered by time and searched in SQL with each match returned together with the lines of the same time and source (recommended), or (b) both paths and the server's search, as decided, the owner: "a". So `errorlog` needs no `xp_readerrorlog` permission check and runs one batch; a search no longer narrows what is copied, so a very large log takes as long with `--search` as without, bounded by `-t`, which the timeout's advice says.
90. **An Agent-log search returns the matching lines alone (owner decision, 2026-11-22; narrows decision 89).** Decision 89's companions are the lines of the same time and source, so a server-log `Error: 18456` line comes back with its `Login failed …` line. The Agent log has no source column (its second column is the level) and whole-second times, and no Agent message spans lines, so there the rule returned every line of that level in that second: 15 unrelated info lines for `Waiting for SQL Server to recover` on `test` (the errorlog review's P2, `c13f934`). Offered (a) companions for the server log only (recommended), or (b) companions for both, as built, the owner: "a". So `errorlog --agent --search` keeps a line only when it matches.
91. **The repository can go public at a `0.1.x`, after at least one more release; the `0.2` or `1.0` label still waits for Phase 6 Part 1 (owner decision, 2026-11-23; amends decision 50).** Decision 50 tied the public release to Phase 6 Part 1 being in use and the README read by strangers, and left making the repository public to the owner. A launch post is planned for 2026-12-01. The go-public checks of 2026-11-22 (§4, Going public) found the history, the refs on GitHub and the workflows ready, and the README, SECURITY.md and CONTRIBUTING.md not written for a stranger. Asked whether to amend decision 50 so the repository can go public with 0.1.x, the owner said: "yes, amend it but we'll make at least one more minor revision release first". So the repository goes public at a `0.1.x` release (decision 51's slice releases), once at least one release after 0.1.0 is out, and the README it goes public with describes the release a stranger can download. Part 1's slices keep their order and their releases, and the `0.2` or `1.0` label still comes after Part 1 is in use. Decision 81 makes the managed-laptop check the first of the public release's checks; that check and the rest of §4's list stay open until the flip. Amended by decision 96 (2026-11-26): the release is 0.2.0, slice 2.
92. **`activity`'s `statement` and `input_buffer` are on one line (owner decision, 2026-11-23; the slice 2 final review's open question).** Both come as the client sent them, with line breaks, tabs and indentation, and `max_cell`'s 300 characters cut them by count, so a laid-out statement could spend much of its cell on indentation and, in the text format, on `\r\n` and `\t` escapes. Offered (a) each run of spaces, tabs, CR and LF made one space before the cut (recommended), or (b) the text as it is, the owner: "a". So both are collapsed in the step's SQL, exactly, and trimmed at both ends (schema-commands.md §6.8); runs of spaces inside a string literal collapse too, as the text is for reading, and `sys.dm_exec_sql_text` keeps the original.
93. **An empty `errorlog` read without `--since` says how far back it went (owner decision, 2026-11-23; the slice 2 final review's open question).** Without `--since` the read is one archive, archive 0 by default, which starts at the last restart or log cycle, so a search that finds nothing answered `-- no rows: server_log`, which reads as "it never happened". Offered (a) the span on the empty result only, `-- no rows: server_log (archive 0, from 2026-11-20 08:14; --since or --archive reads further back)` (recommended), (b) a line in `--help`, the tool description and the docs only, or (c) the span on every result, the owner: "a". So the lookup returns its oldest line and the lines it kept, and an empty read without `--since` puts the archive, its start and what reads further back (or `the oldest the server keeps`) on its `-- no rows:` line; MCP names `since or archive`; in JSON it is one of the document's notes, so contract 2.1 is unchanged (schema-commands.md §6.9, DESIGN.md §5.1).
94. **`errorlog`'s search ignores case and accents on every server (owner decision, 2026-11-26; the slice 2 final review's open question).** `CHARINDEX` compared in tempdb's collation, so on a case-sensitive server `--search "login failed"` missed `Login failed`, while `find` and `tables` match in `Latin1_General_100_CI_AI` whatever the collation. Offered (a) `Latin1_General_100_CI_AI`, as `find` (recommended), (b) `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS`, case but not accents ignored, or (c) the server's collation, as built, the owner: "a". So both logs' searches put `COLLATE Latin1_General_100_CI_AI` on `[Text]` (schema-commands.md §6.9, DESIGN.md §9).
95. **A connect this computer refuses with WSAEADDRINUSE is made once more (owner decision, 2026-11-26; the carried-over 10048 finding, §4).** Every sqlh run opens a connection, Windows holds a closed one's local port in TIME_WAIT for 120 s, and after that live run 1,411 were in TIME_WAIT (of a 16,384-port dynamic range); a release live test's connect had failed in 4 ms with os error 10048, exit 2, and the hint `check the server name`: the cause was this computer, and an agent making many quick calls would take it for a server that is down. DESIGN.md §4.2 said of `-l` "nothing is retried". Offered (a) one more attempt, at once, within the same login deadline, and on a second 10048 a hint that names this computer (recommended), (b) the hint only, or (c) neither, the owner: "a". So `db::once_more_if_port_held` makes the second attempt, only for this error, in `-M`'s form too; the TCP connect that failed sent nothing, though an earlier hop (SQL Browser, a routing listener's login) is made again. A second 10048 is exit 2 with `this computer twice chose a local port that a closed connection to that server still held (Windows holds one about 2 minutes after it closes): wait a minute and try again` (DESIGN.md §4.2, mcp-mode.md §4). It is the one exception to "nothing is retried", and no reconnect (decision 41): it is a new connect, made before sqlh had a session. R69 (2026-11-26): the deadline is checked on the clock before and after the second attempt. R70 (2026-11-26): the hint is `this computer reported its local address in use, twice: wait a minute and try again; if it persists, check this computer's TCP connections`, since the error names no cause.
96. **Slice 2 ships as 0.2.0, the repository goes public with it, and later slices take their versions from the semver rule (owner decision, 2026-11-26; amends decisions 50, 51 and 91).** Slice 2 adds commands and changes the output (decision 93's note), so AGENTS.md's rule ("before 1.0 the minor for a release that changes the output contract or flags") makes it 0.2.0, while decision 51 said each slice ships in a `0.1.x` and decision 91 had the repository go public at one. Asked whether slice 2 releases as 0.2.0, as the semver rule says, or as 0.1.1, the owner chose "0.2.0", then: "I want to ship slice 2 as 0.2.0 by tomorrow. for the production schedule this can be v0.2 or just use 0.2.0. let's focus on finishing slice two and preparing for public visibility". So `Cargo.toml` is 0.2.0, `v0.2.0` is the release after its external review (decision 52), and it is the "one more release" decision 91 asked for before the repository goes public (planned for 2026-11-27; the launch post follows, planned for 2026-12-01, decision 91). Decision 50's "public release (`0.2` or `1.0`) after Phase 6 Part 1 is in use" no longer names a later version: Part 1's later slices keep their order and their "released and used before the next" rule (decision 51), each versioned by the semver rule, and §4's "Going public" list still applies to the flip.
97. **The release build remaps the cargo registry's path, and the size budget is the unsigned exe's (owner decision, 2026-11-26; PR #12's first CI run).** CI's `check` failed the budget: GitHub's runner built the exe at 3,146,752 bytes, 1,024 over 3 MiB, where the same commit builds locally at 3,145,216. CI had always built 1,024 bytes larger (`main` at `ede2980`: 3,112,448 there, 3,111,424 here): the exe embeds 166 source paths, 17,795 bytes, most under the builder's cargo registry, whose path names the user's folder (`C:\Users\runneradmin\...` on the runner). The release workflow builds through `scripts/check.ps1` too, so the signed 0.2.0 would have been over. Offered (a) remapping the registry's path in `check.ps1`'s builds (`--remap-path-prefix`; measured 3,140,608 bytes, 5,120 under, the static CRT and the ten in-box imports kept, no user folder in the exe) (recommended), (b) raising the budget now, for example to 3.25 MiB until decision 87's proposal, or (c) both, the owner: "a", adding "in addition we can state that the budget is only for the unsigned exe". So `check.ps1` sets `CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_WINDOWS_MSVC_RUSTFLAGS` to the remap for every cargo command it runs and, on GitHub, passes it to the job's later steps (`GITHUB_ENV`), so `release.yml`'s own `cargo test --release --no-run` builds the staged exe with the same flags; it fails if the exe still holds a path under the registry. The budget stays 3 MiB, for the unsigned exe `check.ps1` builds; signing appends a signature. Decision 87's proposal is still due before slice 3.
98. **The repository went public with 0.2.0 before the managed-laptop check (owner decision, 2026-11-27; amends decision 81).** Decision 81 made the managed laptop (corporate antivirus or EDR, AppLocker or WDAC) the first check for the public release. With `v0.2.0` published, the history rescanned and the settings listed, the owner was asked to go public with that check moved to after the release, and the issues turned on, and answered "yes". So `sequeltom/sqlh` is public since 2026-11-27, and the managed-laptop check is a check after the release, its steps unchanged (§4, Going public). The commits keep the owner's email as their author: the tags `v0.1.0` and `v0.2.0` can't move, so the history can't be rewritten; GitHub's noreply address for later commits stays the owner's option.
99. **The size budget is 3.25 MiB for the rest of Phase 6 Part 1, and the room under it is not an allowance (owner decision, 2026-11-27; decision 87's proposal).** Measured at `336c1c0`, `v0.2.0`'s code, built as `check.ps1` builds it: 3,140,608 bytes, 5,120 under 3 MiB. `cargo bloat --release --crates`: `mssql_tds` 931.8 KiB, sqlh 619.4 KiB, std 497.3 KiB, tokio 132.5 KiB, `x509_parser` 56.4 KiB, the rest about 250 KiB of the 2.5 MiB `.text`. MCP's share, by mcp-mode.md §8's method: 306,176 bytes, **299.0 KiB of decision 20's 350 KiB** (51.0 KiB left; 298.0 KiB at `00377cb`). The release profile already has full LTO, one codegen unit, `panic = "abort"`, stripped symbols and `serde_json` at `opt-level = "z"`. Two shrinks were measured and reverted, kept for later: the certificate parsers (`x509-parser`, `asn1-rs`, `der-parser`, `nom`) at `opt-level = "z"`, 4,096 bytes smaller, code that runs once per connect; and tokio, `mio`, `parking_lot` and `parking_lot_core` at `opt-level = "s"` as well, 26,624 smaller still (30,720 in all), but on every packet's path, so only after a throughput measurement. What Part 1 still needs: slice 2 cost about 34 KB with its reviews' fixes (3,111,424 to 3,145,216 bytes, before decision 97's remap); slice 3, decision 26's reads, was estimated at 10–25 KB and slices 4–6 at 40–80 KB in all, so slice 3 doesn't fit in 5,120 bytes. Offered (a) 3.25 MiB (3,407,872 bytes) for the rest of Part 1, each commit's size still recorded and the MCP gate unchanged (recommended), (b) shrinking instead and keeping 3 MiB (about 30 KB, enough for slice 3 at most, the tokio part only after a throughput measurement), or (c) both, with the certificate parsers' 4 KB now, the owner: "i agree, A it is but lets stay conservative and not get lax with bloat". So `check.ps1`'s budget is 3,407,872 bytes, still for the unsigned exe (decision 97), and the room is held as if it weren't there:
    - each slice states its size estimate before it is built (in its plan entry and its brief), records the exe after each commit, and justifies growth with `cargo bloat` (`--crates`, and `--filter sqlh` for its own functions);
    - a slice whose growth passes its estimate goes back to the owner, with what grew, before it is merged (slice 2 passed its 12–18 KB estimate, at 23,040 bytes, without that);
    - the rest of Lean and fast is unchanged (AGENTS.md, Hard constraints): every crate needs a reason, a few lines of our own code before a dependency, no copies in the hot paths;
    - MCP's share is measured at each slice's end against decision 20's 350 KiB gate;
    - the budget comes back to the owner at Part 1's end, with the measurements and the shrinks above among the options.
100. **No 0.2.1 until there is a real fix (owner decision, 2026-11-27).** The owner proposed cutting 0.2.1 once PR #19 (the Dependabot routine) and #11 (zeroize 1.9.2) were merged. Since `v0.2.0` (`637c3ab`), only zeroize changes the exe: on x86_64 it wipes as 1.9.1 did (§4, Dependabot's PRs, #11), and its advisory, GHSA-pfv9-jv8r-j7rg, is in code sqlh doesn't build, so the exe would be the same tool, about 1.5 KB smaller; the rest is the release check's budget (decision 99), the upstream watch (PR #18) and docs. Decision 52 would still need an external review first, since the range holds a credentials crate, the release check and a workflow that writes issues in the public repository. Recommended holding it, the owner: "I agree. we will hold off on 0.2.1 until there is a real fix". So these changes ride in the next release that carries something users gain (0.3.0 with slice 3, or the driver, issue #20), or in a 0.2.1 when a real fix lands; that release's external review covers everything since `637c3ab`. The release pipeline after the public switch can be checked meanwhile by a release dispatch on `main`, whose draft release is never published and is deleted after.
101. **The full "Built for agents" list moves out of the README; the README keeps a short version (owner decision, 2026-11-27; the going-public read-through's should-fix, §4).** The section was lines 143–223 of the README's 247: 57 built and 2 planned entries, each one to four sentences with a tag, kept as a running tally by AGENTS.md's rule. A stranger from the launch post met 80 lines of detail before the build and contributing sections. Offered (a) the full list, unchanged, to `docs/agent-features.md`, the tally rule moving with it, and about ten one-line headlines in the README with a link, changed only when a headline does (recommended), (b) leaving it as it is, or (c) all 57 entries kept in the README at one line each, the owner: "a". So `docs/agent-features.md` is the running tally, updated in the same change as its code; README's "Built for agents" has ten headlines (output sized for a model's context, the status line, errors, read-only by default, no secrets, never stuck, the schema commands, `errorlog` and `activity`, `sqlh mcp`, fitting how models work) and changes only when a headline does.

## 7. First-release checklist (replaces the spike's "still to verify")

- [x] mssql-tds 0.4.0 (decision 70): the bump, the IPv6 pipe refusal and decision 72's SPN provider built on a branch, `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` on both servers and driver-0.4.0.md §8's live checks, the external review, then merged to `main`; the cancellation, shell and budget checks below rechecked on 0.4.0 where §8 says so. No later driver in 0.1.0. *Built, reviewed and merged 2026-11-08 (`f0e2465`, §4). §8 item 6, real SSPI to a named instance through SQL Browser, is covered by AppVeyor's runs since build 4 (§4, AppVeyor; not the provider's name lookup, R61). The live float16 check ran against SQL Server 2025, 2026-11-14: the server sent float16 down-level as JSON text and sqlh rendered it as specified (driver-0.4.0.md §10, item 8; DESIGN.md §5.1); the live test's never-run native branch was fixed to take either form. Still open: §8 item 13 (LocalDB) and a routed hop, neither at hand. **Kerberos (decisions 78–79), 2026-11-14:** a disposable Active Directory test environment, with the client running as SYSTEM and authenticating as its computer account, confirmed the original short-name NTLM fallback against `System.Data.SqlClient`; after the SPN fix, sqlh and SqlClient both used Kerberos by fully qualified, short and CNAME names, and NTLM by address. Decision 79 was reviewed (R67 found and fixed, both closed, §4). **Signed build, 2026-11-14:** release run 14's `0.1.0-dev.14` (`bdf6b24`; signature valid and timestamped, hash matching `SHA256SUMS.txt` and checked on the client) gave the same Kerberos results, with sqlh matching SqlClient by every name. With the SQL Server's certificate naming its domain FQDN but not the CNAME alias, and trusted on the client, connections without `-C` checked the certificate against the name dialled: by the FQDN, mandatory and strict both connected by Kerberos; by the alias, both were refused with `SEC_E_WRONG_PRINCIPAL` and the certificate hint (strict's says `-C` doesn't apply). That measures the review's source trace that TLS names the hop's transport name, not the SPN's. `06219db` (public-release preparation) changed no code, so these results stand for it. LocalDB and a routed hop remain untested; a person's interactive Kerberos session was not tested either. **Closed 2026-11-14 by decision 81:** 0.1.0 ships with §8 item 13 (LocalDB) and a routed hop untested, as recorded here and in driver-0.4.0.md §10.*
- [x] 0.1.0 external review: R51-R55 verified closed; R56 documentation qualifications corrected during verification (November 7, 2026, §4, R51-R55 fix verification). Other release gates below remain open.
- [x] Release signing: after the GitHub account rename, the release environment's OIDC configuration was updated and a dispatch on `main` passed build, sign, verify and draft publication (run 42429985874; §4). The workflow retains its owner-ID condition and the signing action's required Azure configuration.
- [x] SQL Server 2016 SP3 (decision 49): the smoke script and live tests pass against a SQL Server 2016 SP3 instance, with version-specific expectations (2026-11-01; rerun before release, §4, SQL Server 2016 support).
- [x] The profiles file under a packaged client: `sqlh mcp` finds a profile created outside the packaged app (decision 53; §4, The profiles file under a packaged app). *Done 2026-11-13: the signed release was configured as an MCP server in the packaged desktop app. After restarting the app, its schema and query tools connected using the existing user profile and Credential Manager entry. The app configuration was preserved; no credentials or user-specific configuration files are tracked.*
- [x] Clean Windows 11 x64 workstation (§6.1): runs with no installs, imports are in-box only, and size is within budget. *Done 2026-11-13 in a fresh Windows Sandbox: the downloaded release archive retained its download mark through extraction, the executable signature and hash verified, and `--version` and `--help` ran. A test profile connected successfully; `info` and a query completed. No domain-joined or managed workstation was used; that remains the colleague-check scope.*
- [x] Against a remote SQL Server 2025 over a real network: connect latency measured, encryption modes mandatory and strict. *Done 2026-11-13 against a disposable cloud VM, with network access restricted to the test client. SQL authentication and a trusted test certificate were used. Median connect time was 425 ms; all encryption modes connected, strict encryption validated over TDS 8.0, and MCP reuse reduced subsequent calls to 113–125 ms from 570 ms for the first. `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passed over the network after the test flake was fixed. Detailed timings are in [notes/connect-latency.md](notes/connect-latency.md). The VM was deallocated after testing; its cleanup remains an owner decision.*
- [x] SQL Server 2025 **Standard** (not only Standard Developer): SQL auth, and Windows auth (decision 50: NTLM through a local Windows user on the test server; Kerberos recorded as unchecked unless a domain is at hand). *Closed by decision 76 (owner, 2026-11-13): Standard Developer stands in for Standard.*
- [x] Every finding in plan-history.md §1 fixed or explicitly accepted, each with a regression test (2026-11-08: regression coverage reviewed; F13's test is new; F14's wrapper/ownership-review evidence and known wiping limits are qualified by R57, §4, 0.1.0 release-check external review)
- [x] Cancellation: Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Break, console close, harness kill, `-t`, during connect and during execution (2026-11-08, release exe: by test and by hand, §4, 0.1.0 release checks; logoff and shutdown not tried)
- [x] The response budget holds for text, json and csv, and the status line is always present (2026-11-08, release exe: 54 runs, six workloads, three formats, three caps, §4, 0.1.0 release checks)
- [x] cmd.exe and Windows PowerShell 5.1 (plan-history.md 2.13): the `--help` examples run as written, every path form works for `-i`, `-o` and `SQLH_CONFIG`, and piped non-ASCII SQL either arrives intact or the docs say how to keep it intact (2026-11-08: by the shell tests and live; a double BOM from a captured 5.1 pipe found and fixed, §4, 0.1.0 release checks)
- [x] `scripts/check.ps1` is green, CI (plan-history.md 3.5) passes on the remote, live tests are green with `SQLH_TEST_PROFILE`, and the docs have no claims ahead of the code (CI green on the remote since 2026-10-15; the rest is checked at release time). *2026-11-14, at `cf8b62e`: `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passed on `test` (SQL Server 2025) and `test2016`, one after the other, each 510 unit, 127 debug and 121 release process and 87 debug and 80 release live tests, exe 3,105,792 bytes; release run 13 at the same commit passed (build, sign, verify, publish). Decision 77 and R65–R66's fixes came after, so the tag's commit needs its own green run. *At `bdf6b24`, 2026-11-14 (decision 77, R65–R66, decision 79 and R67 included): `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passed on both servers, one after the other, each 514 unit, 128 debug and 121 release process and 87 debug and 80 release live tests, exe 3,106,304 bytes; release run 14 passed (build, sign, verify, publish). `06219db` changed only workflows (actions pinned to their release commits, each checked against the official tag) and docs, so the code's results stand; the tag's commit gets its own release run. **Closed 2026-11-14:** CI's `check` passed on PR #6 (merged as `9243c9a`; no code change since `bdf6b24`). Docs against code, at `9243c9a`: README's tally counts 54 Built entries, as listed, and its 2 Planned items (the rest of Phase 6, Phase 8) are unbuilt; DESIGN.md marks each unbuilt feature as such (`--stats`, the md format, kind grants, D4b), and the exe refuses the two that are flags as it says (`--stats` exit 3 as an invalid option; `--format md` not implemented yet). The tag's own release run is the release-time build.*
- [x] Code signing: the release workflow (plan-history.md 3.6) produces a signed `sqlh.exe` (done 2026-10-15: a valid, timestamped signature on the draft `v0.1.0-dev.1`), and SmartScreen and antivirus behavior is checked on a clean, locked-down workstation. *Clean, 2026-11-13 (with the clean-workstation line above): in Windows Sandbox, the downloaded-marked `0.1.0-dev.12` opened through Explorer (as a double-click does) and through ShellExecute showed no SmartScreen prompt; the ShellExecute launch ran, exit 0; Smart App Control was in evaluation mode there. Defender doesn't run in the Sandbox, so the same exe (same hash) was scanned on the owner's workstation, real-time protection on, signatures 1.463.288.0: no threats. Still to do: a colleague's managed laptop, the zip sent as it will be distributed, for corporate antivirus or EDR and AppLocker or WDAC. **Closed 2026-11-14 by decision 81:** the managed-laptop check is deferred to the public release. Signing itself: every release run since `v0.1.0-dev.1` signed and timestamped the exe; run 14's `0.1.0-dev.14` was checked by hand (valid, timestamped, hash matching `SHA256SUMS.txt`).*
